# CLAUDE.md

This file states the CURRENT rules, invariants, traps and decisions, not history (kept in `git log` and, where
users see it, `docs/release-notes.md`). Where another document is the authority (ROLES_AND_PERMISSIONS.md,
AUTH_PATHS.md, EMAIL_RULES.md, DEVELOPMENT.md, README.md, swagger.yaml), this file points there instead of repeating it.

**Keep it current, not longer.** When a change makes a statement here untrue, correct or delete it in the same
commit; never append release notes or "superseded" markers (the commit message records what changed and why).

## Commands

- **Script reference.** `package.json` `scripts` is the authoritative list (DEVELOPMENT.md explains most, perf/README.md the perf ones); `start`/`dev` default `DATA_DIR=./data` and `UPLOADS_DIR=./uploads`. Tests, smoke and balance: *Tests*; nodemon: *Dev server*.
- **`npm start --dry-run` is not a dry run.** npm takes `--dry-run` as its own config, so `start` boots a real server on `DATA_DIR` (default `./data`) and `PORT` (default 3000). Read `package.json` instead.

### Versioning

- **`version.json` is the single source of truth.** `package.json` `version`, `docker-compose.yml`'s `image: hackarena:<v>` tag and `swagger.yaml` `info.version` must agree with it.
- **Never hand-edit any of the four.** `npm run bump patch|minor|major|X.Y.Z` (`scripts/bump-version.js`) rewrites them without committing, but only warns and skips `swagger.yaml` if its version line is missing. Review the diff and run `npm run check-version`, which fails on any disagreement.
- **`check-version` fails on more than one `info.version` line in `swagger.yaml`.** YAML keeps the last duplicate key, so checking the first alone could pass while the published spec disagrees.
- **`package-lock.json`'s own version fields are deliberately NOT in the bump set.** The CI image bakes a sha256 of the whole lockfile and every pinning job fails closed on a mismatch, so syncing them would force an image rebuild and digest re-pin each release; nothing reads them and `npm ci` accepts the lag: do not "fix" it. The durable fix (hash only dependency content; change `ci/Dockerfile.cross-browser` and every image-pinning workflow guard together) is in `scripts/check-version.js`. Dependency bumps do change the hash (see *Deployment and CI image*).

### Releases

- **Bump first, then settle the release notes, then run the suite.** `tests/release-notes.spec.js` keys on the current `version.json`; a run before the bump checks the previous version.
- **Every released version the notes cover needs a recorded decision** (the spec pins the oldest): a `## <version> — <date>` heading in `docs/release-notes.md` (newest first) or an `INTERNAL_ONLY` entry in the spec, never both or neither; `INTERNAL_ONLY` when organisers and participants would see nothing, wording-only note passes included. The no-gaps check runs each finished minor line to its last release in the spec's `LINE_LAST_RELEASE`, catching a deleted entry anywhere, so a new minor line's first release fails until the previous line's last release (from `git log -p -- version.json`) is added there.
- **Bullets are compact and user-facing.** Each opens with a bold headline and stays within `BULLET_MAX_CHARS`: no version numbers, routes or file names, and no prose outside bullets. Name controls as the UI spells them, say who a change affects, and leave out mechanism, cause and history. Measured capacity and sizing figures are allowed (operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026): organisers and operators plan a venue and a server around them.

### Commits

- **STANDING RULE: `AGENT_AUDIT_MEMORY.md` belongs to the operator.** It records audit findings accepted by design; the operator edits it between sessions. Before any commit, run `git status AGENT_AUDIT_MEMORY.md` and stage it if dirty, or the next audit runs on stale dispositions. Never rewrite it unprompted; if a claim in it is stale, say so and let the operator decide.
- **Update CLAUDE.md in the same commit as the code change** whenever an agent would need to know about it.

## Architecture

- **One process per data volume.** Every cache, rate-limit bucket, session map, scanner-reputation record and work queue lives in process memory, and invalidation reaches only that process's readers. Never run behind a round-robin LB, under `pm2 -i N` / cluster mode, or as replicas sharing a volume; boot exits FATAL in a cluster worker.

### Key files
- `server.js` builds the app: middleware in order (asset caches, `express.static`, session, `/docs`), boot checks, `routes/*` in registration order, shutdown. **Its remaining inline routes (*Inline in server.js*, plus test-only endpoints and the SPA shell) are inline on purpose**: each is pinned by a source-introspection lock or owns process-resident state, so moving one means moving its lock.
- `db.js` opens `hackathon.db` (WAL, `foreign_keys = ON`) at require time and runs every migration.
- `lib/`: shared modules required directly (no ctx object); a cache or queue is one process-global instance per module. `ls lib/` is authoritative; most changes touch `middleware`, `event-policy`, `event-admin`, `competitions`, `audit` or `time` (the one "now" and the one zone formatter).
- `routes/`: one module per domain; `ls routes/` is authoritative.
- `public/js/**` is the SPA as ES modules: `core/` (`state.js` the one mutable `S`, `trusted-types.js`, `dispatcher.js`, `api.js` fetch and CSRF, `main.js` entry and `boot()`), `ui/` shared widgets, `data/country-tz.js`, one `features/<entity>.js` per domain. **`public/help.js`, `help-renderer.js` and `theme-boot.js` are classic scripts outside the module graph**: they cannot import an ES module and share via `window` globals (`window.HelpRenderer`); `help.html` gets asset versions from `/asset-versions.js`.
- **Adding a route:** `module.exports = function register(app[, deps])` with absolute, method-specific paths, never `app.use(prefix, router)`; boot-resident or test-pinned symbols arrive via `deps`. Use the matching `routes/<entity>.js` or a new module required from `server.js` in registration order; helpers shared by two route modules and any process-global state go in `lib/`. Express 5 leaves `req.body` undefined when no parser ran: read `req.body?.x`.
- **Require-graph rules.** `lib/http.js` must not reach `db.js` (`lib/audit` opens SQLite at require time). `lib/email` does not require `lib/account-reaper.js`; if it did, nothing the reaper reaches could require `lib/email` (a cycle). `lib/scheduler.js` requires competitions/audit, never the reverse; in `lib/`, only `lib/invitation-claim.js` requires `lib/group-sync.js`, so nothing either reaches may require them.
- **Every-tier rules (`lib/answer-seal.js`, `lib/staff-compete.js`) stay out of `lib/event-admin.js`**: its task gates exempt admin and superadmin first, making them inert.

### Data storage
`DATA_DIR`'s children, canonical:

| Child | Contents | Backup |
|---|---|---|
| `hackathon.db` | SQLite (WAL) with sessions (`express_session` table, same connection; no `sessions.db`) | covered |
| `attachments/`, `attachments-staging/` | challenge attachments; staged uploads | covered |
| `logs/` | access + app JSONL (`LOG_DIR` overrides; `scripts/purge.sh` follows it, refusing an empty one, `scripts/backup.sh` copies only this child); rotated by `lib/log.js`, old files pruned by `scripts/purge.sh` | covered |
| `.grading-secret` | HMAC key; loss only invalidates in-flight grading tokens | covered |
| `.unsubscribe-secret` | HMAC key; loss breaks every already-sent (365-day) unsubscribe link | covered |
| `.superadmin-password` | regenerated every boot | exempt: a restored copy names a dead credential |
| `.maintenance` | maintenance sentinel | exempt: restoring it 503s users |
| `.smtp-key` | AES-256-GCM key of the mail-server password saved in Settings (`lib/smtp-settings.js`) | exempt: a backup with key and database holds the password; after a restore the password is unreadable, mail falls back to the environment's relay, a superadmin re-enters it (RESTORE.txt step 7) |

- **A new `DATA_DIR` child needs a `BACKUP_COVERS:` or `BACKUP_EXEMPT: … — why` line in `scripts/backup.sh` and a row above.** A scanner (lock: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`) fails a `path.join(DATA_DIR, "…")` literal with no decision; without one the backup silently goes incomplete. Procedure: *Backups*.
- **`DATA_DIR` and `UPLOADS_DIR` are required, with no default and no `NODE_ENV` carve-out**: `db.js` opens the database at require time, so a guessed path attaches to a live deployment. `lib/data-dir.js` is the one definition (requires only `path`); it **exports functions, not a cached value** (specs re-point `DATA_DIR` by purging `../db` from the require cache) and stays out of `lib/config.js` so `db.js` needs no `BASE_URL` (lock: `tests/data-dir-required.spec.js`).
- **`db.js` creates a missing `DATA_DIR` 0700 and a missing `hackathon.db` 0600 before SQLite opens it;** an existing file's mode is the operator's, never changed.
- **`UPLOADS_DIR` is its own decision, never derived from `DATA_DIR`.** Temporary upload files (submissions, import ZIPs; read, then deleted) on a separate volume, not backed up; the upload capacity check measures both filesystems. Boot and an hourly sweep (1 h mtime TTL) delete from it, so a wrong value deletes live files.
- **The periodic attachment sweeps skip files whose `max(mtimeMs, ctimeMs)` is under 5 minutes old**: the staging route renames a file in before its INSERT commits, and rename sets a ctime userspace cannot backdate. One 10-minute timer sweeps staging rows (1 h TTL), then staging files; permanent attachments are swept daily. The boot sweep of `attachments/` has no age guard.

### Database tables
- Authoritative: `grep "CREATE TABLE" db.js lib/cxone-ledger.js`, plus `express_session` (made by the `express-session-better-sqlite3` store, same connection). Non-obvious (most: grep the name here for its section): `revoked_sessions`, `competition_presence` (five-minute presence buckets), `attempt_grants`, `entity_status_observations` (scheduler state), `cxone_realm_accounts` (no FK on purpose), `event_templates` (never-live event blueprints; see *Clone event and event templates*), `topics` (the Question bank's topics; see *The Question bank: topics, the tags and Save to bank*), `certificate_dispatch_jobs`, `certificate_emails`, `certificate_share_links`, `roles` / `role_permissions` (custom roles; the four built-in rows are re-asserted every boot and their permissions are code, never rows; see *Roles, scope and impersonation*), `email_proofs`, the event team's `event_team_members`, `event_team_settings`, `event_leads`, `team_report_jobs`, `team_report_sends`, `team_report_runs` (see *Event team and persona reports*), `program_attempts` (a Program learner's closed attempts; see *Program grades and Try again*) and `orphan_events`. `tasks` holds BANK questions (`owner_competition_id` NULL) and EVENTS' OWN questions (see *Challenges* > *Event-owned questions*).
- **Hand-written column lists readers depend on.** `_PARTICIPANT_COMP_COLUMNS` (`lib/competitions.js`) and the admin competitions list SELECT must name `event_kind` and `registration_field_policy`: the policy projection throws without them (deliberately loud). Participant-row SELECTs share `_PARTICIPANT_ROW_COLUMNS` (`routes/participants.js`), profile-gate reads `PROFILE_GATE_COLUMNS`/`PROFILE_GATE_JOIN` (`lib/util.js`): add a column there once.
- **Migration fatality.** Base `CREATE TABLE`s are fatal. An ALTER whose column core writes name goes outside try/catch (as `tasks.attempt_cap` does): a swallowed ADD means SQLITE_ERROR on every write. Other ADDs and all DROPs are non-fatal.
- **Every ALTER-added column the app needs is in `REQUIRED_COLUMNS`, every UNIQUE index in `REQUIRED_INDEXES` (`lib/schema-required.js`)**, else `EXEMPT_*` with a reason; a scanner enforces it. A missing one makes `/readyz` 503 `db_schema`, since table probes cannot see a column or index.
- **Create an index below the ALTER that adds its column and below every rebuild of its table.** Above the ALTER it fails `no such column` in a swallowed catch; above a rebuild, the rebuild's DROP TABLE takes it and only a later CREATE INDEX restores it (`idx_tasks_title` follows both `tasks` rebuilds). Either way it is missing until the next boot, and tests never see it: a fresh database skips both.
- **Rebuilds (to change a CHECK) derive DDL from `sqlite_master`, never a pasted copy**, run once (guarded on the stored SQL) with `foreign_keys = OFF`, `legacy_alter_table = ON` across the RENAME and `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` after. New `tasks` columns go below the rebuild blocks.
- **One-shot data migrations write a `settings` marker key** even when nothing changed (e.g. `linkedin_signup_default_off_at`), or they re-run every boot and undo admins. A column `DEFAULT` is its backfill.
- A backtick in a SQL comment inside a JS template literal ends the literal.

### Boot-time sanity checks
Before listen: cluster worker, FATAL; `PRAGMA integrity_check` and `verifySchema`, loud but non-fatal (up for triage, `/readyz` refuses traffic, and the instance-health gate holds both mail workers, the start reminders, the lockout-expiry mail and the CxOne runners until a later boot's checks pass: *Maintenance mode*); version transition logged; `https` `BASE_URL` with trust proxy off, FATAL outside test (every login would fail `csrf_invalid`); trust proxy on `http`, or `TRUST_PROXY=true`, warns; badge rendering unavailable warns (badge endpoints 503). `.superadmin-password` is written after the port pre-check.
- **A FATAL exit says why in production too:** it exits before the async log file opens, so `lib/log.js`'s exit hook writes the waiting line and, on a non-zero exit with the console mirror off, replays the last minute's error lines (at most 20) to stderr; without it a misconfigured container stops with every output empty (lock: `tests/fatal-exit-visible.spec.js`).

### Graceful shutdown
SIGTERM/SIGINT (not under `NODE_ENV=test`) and `uncaughtException` (exit 1): close the HTTP server; drain deferred audit, the CxOne queue, detached mail, group reconcile, withdrawals, the certificate-dispatch and team-report passes (5 s budget); `db.close()`; exit. A 20 s watchdog force-exits 1. **A new detached work queue must join this drain** (source pin: "the shutdown drain consults the gauge" in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`, every gauge named and both mail workers stopped first; held by behaviour for a certificate send in flight, `tests/certificate-email-ledger.spec.js`, and detached mail, `tests/smtp-pool-drain.spec.js`; the team-report pass's own gauge has only the pin). Pending CxOne set-up retries are recorded and stopped, never drained (an exit-cut create is an account nothing records); boot resumes only rows naming this `instance_origin`, once the live health gate passes (lock: `tests/periodic-sweeps.api.spec.js`).

### In-process observability
- `GET /metrics` (admin) is Prometheus text; its `# HELP` lines are the list.
- Some counters are the only witness: `hack_arena_csp_reports_total` (`csp-report` answers 204 whatever it receives, so the client learns nothing) and the silent scanner-block counters.

### Maintenance mode
- **One resolver: `_maintenanceState()` (`server.js`).** The existence of `$DATA_DIR/.maintenance` is the mode, its trimmed content the `X-Maintenance-Bypass` token. Only ENOENT is inactive; any other read error is active with no token (fail closed). The gate, `/readyz`, the bulk-seed guards (403 unless active), the withdrawal sweeper, both mail workers and boot all use it; never test existence or content separately.
- **Every background mail pass holds while it is active, and while the database failed its boot integrity or schema check** (operator rulings, 17, 24 and 25 Nov 2026; the unlock mail joined the start reminders in the same change; the IP-ban alert, raised by a request, is not held): one gate, `server.js` `_instanceInService` (integrity, schema, maintenance), never a copy. Its readers: both mail workers (handed negated to `setCertificateMailHold` and `setTeamReportMailHold`), the start-reminder dispatcher (`runReminderTick`, asked before the pass and again before each claim), the lockout-expiry pass (`runLockoutExpiryPass`: the lock and its "unlocked" mail wait), both CxOne runners (`isHealthy`), the orphaned-image sweep (`isHealthy` into `lib/event-retention.js` `sweepExpiredEvents` from the daily reaper, the superadmin's manual purge (`routes/admin-misc.js` deps) and `POST /api/test/purge-events`; none handed holds it; a held pass answers `images_held:true`, shown on the Settings retention card's Unused pictures line, the event purge still runs) and nothing else (source locks: `tests/periodic-sweeps.api.spec.js`; the image hold's behaviour: `tests/image-sweep.spec.js`). A held pass answers `held:true` and touches nothing, every job or row waiting, so no certificate, team report, start reminder or unlock mail goes out from a copied or restored database started in maintenance, nor from a corrupt or half-migrated one (README, RESTORE.txt); the boot checks run once, so a failed one holds until the database is fixed and the server restarted. The hold only delays: what a restore's lifted hold sends is the restore's step 4 choice (`scripts/restore-mail.sh`, *Backups*). An admin's Resend, Issue again and Send now are not held. **A pass's early return is answered before the pass starts:** returning inside it before its first await runs its `finally` (`_running = null`) before `_running` is assigned, so every later call gets that answer (locks: `tests/mail-hold-maintenance.api.spec.js`, the lockout pass too; integrity and schema, a spawned server booted broken, then repaired: `tests/mail-hold-instance-health.spec.js`).
- **An empty `.maintenance` is maintenance with no bypass; the way out is removing the file.** The gate requires `token.length > 0` before `crypto.timingSafeEqual`: two empty buffers compare equal and an empty header arrives as `""`, so without the clause anyone bypasses.

### Content-hashed static assets
- At boot every file under `public/` and `docs/` except HTML and Markdown is hashed (CSS after minification), and HTML `href`/`src` get `?v=<hash>`: versioned URLs are `immutable` for a year, bare ones `max-age=300`. Hashes are frozen at boot, so an edit needs a restart.
- **JS:** `_MINIFY_JS` off (dev, test) serves `public/js/**` raw; on (`NODE_ENV=production` or `MINIFY_JS=1`), a lazily required esbuild bundles `/js/core/main.js`, and a build failure falls back to raw modules rather than wedging boot.
- **A size budget holds the stylesheet, the app's page and the production bundle at their ruling-day size + 5%** (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026), measured as sent: the stylesheet minified, the bundle built and minified with `server.js`'s own esbuild options, all three compressed by its `_assetEntry`, gzip and brotli each under its own ceiling (`tests/asset-size-budget.spec.js` reads both from `server.js`). A change that goes over raises that spec's figure in the same change.

### CSS minification
- **`_minifyCss` strips spaces only around `{ } ; , > ~` and after `:`, never before `:` or around `(`, `)` or `+`**: `a :is(b)` is a descendant selector (glued, a compound that matches nothing), and `@media … and (…)` and `calc(a + b)` need their spaces (lock: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`). It copies quoted strings verbatim.
- **It stays linear by keeping its last written character in `last`:** reading `out[out.length - 1]` flattens the `+=` string at every whitespace run, quadratic enough to keep a loaded CI runner's production boot from listening within 15 s (lock: the same spec's linear-time arm).

### Self-hosted fonts
- **One family for every look: Inter (part of the operator-approved flat-design plan, 13 Nov 2026), as `"Inter Text"`/`"Inter Text Display"`**: vendored (`public/vendor/fonts/inter-latin*.woff2`) at `size-adjust: 87.1%` (the size machines without Aptos drew, so dropping Aptos moved no layout), with JetBrains Mono for code; their files are locked to exist. A new face needs its licence and its latin-ext subset (names such as Müller, Łukasz); a new open face also needs an operator decision. **Every HTML entry point, including `_emailResultPage` (`routes/auth.js`), links `/fonts.css`**, or it falls back to system fonts and breaks visual-regression determinism; only the self-contained maintenance page (`_MAINTENANCE_HTML`) uses system fonts, under its own `default-src 'none'` CSP. highlight.js is self-hosted too, so **the CSP names no third-party origin**; a CDN asset would reopen it (lock: `tests/functional-security-b.api.spec.js`).
- **No font `<link rel="preload">` unless its href is exactly a `url()` fonts.css requests.** The shell rewriter adds `?v=<hash>` to every href while fonts.css asks for the bare `/vendor/fonts/*.woff2`, so a preload is another URL: it never serves the face, steals bandwidth from the render-blocking `styles.css`, and, landing first, makes Chrome lay out and pre-paint the UNSTYLED page (its hover hit test), which the layout-shift tracker takes as its baseline, so the first styled frame scores a `<body>` shift (8 px UA margin to 0) (lock: `tests/cls-picker-skeleton.spec.js`).

### Public probe responses carry no internals
- `/healthz` is pure liveness. `/readyz` returns `ready` plus a coarse `reason` (`db_integrity`, `db_schema`, `maintenance`, `db_unavailable`; `db_integrity` adds a fixed `detail`), never a driver message, and logs driver errors server-side: SQLite messages name tables, columns and paths. Apply this to any new public probe.

### In-process caches and their invalidation

#### The caches

| Cache | Module | Key | Lifetime | Invalidated by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_meCache` | `lib/me-cache.js` | userId | 2 s, cap 5000 | `_invalidateMeCache(id)`, `_invalidateMeCacheForComps(ids)` |
| `_userTagsCache` | `lib/user-tags.js` | userId | 2 s, 5000 | `_invalidateUserTagsCache(id)` |
| `_roleCache`, `_scopeCache` | `lib/event-admin.js` | userId, never session id (sessions regenerate) | 2 s, 5000 | `invalidateEventAdminScope(id)` (both) |
| `_roleDefCache` | `lib/rbac.js` | custom role id → class + permissions (built-in roles are code, never cached) | 2 s, 500 | `invalidateRoleCaches()` (clears it and both caches above) after every role create/edit/delete commit |
| `_competitionCache` | `lib/competitions.js` | comp id | 1 s, 5000 | `_invalidateCompetitionCache(id)` |
| `_leaderboardCache` | `lib/leaderboard.js` | compId, then `_global` or `ts:<id>` | 5 s, 5000 | `invalidateLeaderboardCache(compId)`; a first entry: `invalidateLeaderboardCacheForEntrant(compId)` (coalesced: see below) |
| `_rankedBoardMemo` | `lib/leaderboard.js` | the cached entries ARRAY (WeakMap), plus the presence basis | life of that `_leaderboardCache` entry | dies with it; `{fresh:true}` never reads it. `buildLeaderboard` returns frozen rows: never mutate them (lock: `tests/storm-witnesses.api.spec.js`) |
| `_leaderboardVersions` (+ epoch) | `lib/leaderboard.js` | compId → counter | process, 5000 | bumped inside `invalidateLeaderboardCache` (a global bust moves the epoch); NOT by the entrant bust. The pulse's `lb_version` (`leaderboardVersion`) is an HMAC of event, epoch and counter under a per-process key, never the counter (before an event starts it counts joins and edits); compare it only for equality |
| `_adminParticipantsCache` | `lib/participants-cache.js` | `<actor>\|c=<id>` or `all`, then `:page:limit` | 5 s, 500 | `invalidateAdminParticipantsCache()` (clears all) |
| `_adminCompEventsCache` | `lib/audit.js` | `compId:offset:limit:p\|np` | 5 s, 500 | `invalidateAdminCompEventsCache(compId)`: inside `recordCompetitionEvent`, and directly from competition DELETE and organiser grant/revoke |
| `_actorCache` | `lib/audit.js` | lower-cased `changedBy` | 60 s, 1000 | TTL only |
| allowlist `_cache` | `lib/comp-allowlist.js` | whole list | 5 s backstop | `invalidateCompAllowlist()` |
| `_analyticsCache` | `server.js` | `actor\|scope\|req.url` | 15 s, 256 | TTL only |
| `_auditCache` | `routes/audit.js` | actor, acting human, scope, sealed reveals, `req.url` | 5 s, 256 | TTL only |
| `_metricsCache` | `server.js` | `/metrics` body | 1 s | TTL only |
| `_peerCache` | `lib/team-reports.js` | event id + scope (the managers' comparison: the reader's, or a manager's appointer's) | 5 min (`PEER_CACHE_MS`), 500 | TTL only (the compared events have ended) |
| `_platformNameCache` | `lib/settings.js` | single value | process | `_invalidatePlatformNameCache()` (settings PATCH) |
| platform-look `_cache` | `lib/platform-look.js` | single value | process | `setPlatformLook` (see the writer rule) |
| `_zoneFormatters` | `lib/time.js` | exact zone string → its formatter, or false for a zone Intl refused (RangeError only) | process, 1024 (FIFO); keys over 64 characters never kept | none: zone data is fixed for the process. Read only through `zoneFormatter`, shared by `isValidTimezone` and `formatEmailDate` |
| `_taskNumbersAssignedCache` | `lib/competitions.js` | comp id (Set) | process | `_invalidateTaskNumbersCache(id)` on task-assignment changes |
| `_kickedSessions` | `lib/sessions.js` | a purged session's cookie id → kick context or `SESSION_END` reason (sign-out popup) | 10 min, 1000 FIFO | TTL only; `invalidateOtherSessions` writes, `lookupKickedSession` (`GET /api/session/status`) reads |
| `_alerts`, `_suppressedIps` | `lib/admin-alerts.js` | alert id; IP | 1 h (`ALERT_TTL_MS`), 50 alerts | TTL only |
| `_realmRemovals` | `lib/cxone.js` | realm account id | `REALM_REMOVAL_MEMORY_MS`, 10 000 (never evicts in-flight/unknown) | `settleRealmRemoval`; runner read |
| `_provisionRetries` | `lib/cxone.js` | userId + competition id | `PROVISION_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`, ≤ `PROVISION_RETRY_MAX` retries per key, uncapped | firing; `stopCxOneProvisionRetries` |

Static-asset caches (`_cssCache`, `_jsCache`, `_mdCache`, `_htmlCache` in `server.js`; font metrics in `lib/badge.js`) last until a restart. **`_assetEntries` (`server.js` `_sendFrozenAsset`) holds each served shell (per platform name and platform look), stylesheet and JS bundle encoded, ETagged and gzip/brotli-compressed once** (cap 64): done per request, it was the biggest main-thread cost of a room opening the page at once (perf/RESULTS.md). It keeps compression()'s contract (same weak ETag, Vary, br over gzip, never on HEAD) but compresses from 1 KB, not compression()'s 8 KB (lock: `tests/storm-hotpaths.api.spec.js`; once per entry, never per request, is COUNTED in `tests/storm-witnesses.api.spec.js` for the shell and stylesheet, and the bundle under `MINIFY_JS=1`, as a revert keeping the bytes passes the contract).

**`db.prepare` is memoised by SQL text** (`db.js`, installed after the migrations, cap 2000): reader modes are reset on every hand-out and a `busy` (mid-iterate) statement is never shared. Build SQL whose text does not vary per call: an id list is one `json_each(?)` parameter (`IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?))` bound to `JSON.stringify(list)`), not N placeholders, or every call compiles a new statement (lock: the placeholder-list scanner in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js` Docs hygiene; a list fixed at module load goes in its EXEMPT map with a reason).

**Per-sign-in lookups must be index searches** (a 3000-person rush makes any O(N) lookup O(N²); lock: `tests/storm-hotpaths.api.spec.js`). Sign-in doors use `lib/account-lookup.js` `findUserByPrimary` (the primary's UNIQUE NOCASE index); the audit mirror's actor lookup and the participant import's per-row conflict scan use `findUserByAddress` (primary, then secondary, each UNIQUE NOCASE; the import's lock: `tests/import-rich-text-prepass.api.spec.js`). Every sign-up asks `emailIsTaken` (`lib/email-identity.js`) several times: three point lookups, primary, secondary and a live pending change (`idx_users_pending_email_nocase`). Never `LOWER(email)=LOWER(?)`, nor `email=? COLLATE NOCASE OR secondary_email=? COLLATE NOCASE` (the planner will not union the partial secondary index): both scan. A list of addresses is `email COLLATE NOCASE IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?))` (collation on the column; on the value it takes the BINARY index and matches by case); a lookup across slots is a UNION of indexed arms; `unsubscribed_emails` holds `normaliseEmail` output and is compared exactly. Scanner: functional-core-b Docs hygiene; plans: `tests/storm-hotpaths.api.spec.js`. A live invitation by address is `used_at IS NULL AND email=? COLLATE NOCASE` (the partial `uq_invitations_live_email`), the bulk invitation's two sites sharing `routes/invitations.js` `LIVE_INVITATION_SQL`. A user's session rows come from two expression indexes (`lib/session-index.js`, built in `db.js`); a query must repeat the expression byte for byte or it scans. The server listens with `LISTEN_BACKLOG` (4096, `server.js`): Node's default 511 overflows when 3000 arrive in 10 s, dropping handshakes.

#### The writer rule

- **Each cache exists once; import its operations, not the raw Map.** A second instance serves stale data silently. Sole exception: `routes/events.js` reads `_adminCompEventsCache` directly.
- **Invalidate after the DB commit, by id where the cache supports it.** By id avoids platform-wide misses; a bust inside the transaction lets a concurrent re-read restore pre-commit data with nothing to mark it stale. Exceptions: `recordCompetitionEvent` busts the events cache, and `setPlatformLook` the platform-look memo, inside the caller's transaction (nothing reads the memo between that clear and the commit, so a rollback leaves it empty, never stale). The perf-seed `competition_events` insert in `server.js` bypasses it.
- **Ask "does this change what the cache reports", not "is this a user write".** Competition-level writes bust `/api/me` per user; a kind change busts every member. `_bustLateWindowCaches(id)` (`routes/competitions.js`) covers late-entry close, deadline, switch or schedule PUTs and both End Now branches. Registration-field changes and competition DELETE fan out by `registration_competition_id`, not membership. `POST /api/toggle-admin-view` busts the me-cache (`isAdminView` is session-derived).
- **Profile saves bust the admin participants cache unconditionally.** Each save bumps `updated_at`, part of the row fingerprint; a gated bust causes a spurious 409 on the next save.
- **Bulk writers bust once per pass.** `lib/group-sync.js` passes `deferCacheBust:true`, then clears the leaderboard and participants caches once at the end; per-user busts of a clear-all cache mean N platform-wide misses. Me-cache busts stay per user.
- **`competition_tasks` feeds the leaderboard, so assignment changes bust it.** grep `invalidateLeaderboardCache(`.
- **Key by actor whenever the body depends on who asks.** Participants: `competitorIds(req)` (seat and hand) plus `:del` (may delete accounts). Analytics includes the scope, so revoked grants become unreachable (`invalidateEventAdminScope` does not clear it). The leaderboard is deliberately not actor-keyed: access is checked before the cache. Prefixed segments (`c=`, `ts:`) stop a caller-supplied id spelling a sentinel key.
- **Allowlist: invalidate explicitly when create or PUT writes `trusted_network_ips`, and when `start_time` changes on a competition with trusted IPs.** The 5 s TTL covers every other window change (competition DELETE, both End Now branches, auto-end, revive). It ages on `Date.now()`, never the injected `now`, and is stamped only after a successful build, so a read error is not cached as an outage (lock: `tests/trusted-networks.api.spec.js`).
- **`deleteUserCascade` also calls `invalidateEventAdminScope`.** A missing row resolves as unscoped, safe only because await-bearing admin routes re-check the actor at commit (`adminAuthzDenied`).

#### Persist versus poll

- **A persist must not read a poll's cache.** The group-board freeze, the lazy freeze in `buildGroupLeaderboard` and the badge mint (`_mintEligibility`; the automatic issue through a per-job memo built fresh, see *Automatic issue*) pass `{fresh:true}`; the builder honours it and still fills the cache. A cached board can predate the submission that ended the event (lock: `tests/freeze-fresh.api.spec.js`).
- **`group_leaderboard_snapshots` is durable, not a cache.** Freeze and revive rules: see *Scoring*.

#### Deliberate exceptions

- **A first entry coalesces its bust.** The `login_time` stamp (select-competition, select-timeslot, `_stampLoginTimeIfLive`) only adds a zero-score row, so it calls `invalidateLeaderboardCacheForEntrant`: a board built within `LEADERBOARD_ENTRY_COALESCE_MS` (1 s) is kept until that window ends, an older one dropped. A hard bust per entry rebuilds the O(participants) board on the next read, O(N²) at an event start, enough to collapse a 3000-user sign-in storm (perf/RESULTS.md). Score, solve and finish writers keep `invalidateLeaderboardCache` (lock: `tests/storm-hotpaths.api.spec.js`; the rebuild count, at most one per window for eight entrants: `tests/storm-witnesses.api.spec.js`; an entrant showing WITHIN the window is held by storm-hotpaths alone). A newcomer's join coalesces too (`lib/self-enroll.js` `_selfEnrollSideEffects`); a rejoin bringing back answers or hints busts outright. A test reading the board right after an entry polls for the entrant.
- **`recordPresence` does not bust the leaderboard.** With hundreds of participants every poll would clear it. `time_basis` and `measure_presence` edits get no explicit bust either; the board catches up within its 5 s TTL, and an open participant board at its next `LB_FLOOR_MS` fetch (60 s), as no version moves (`time_basis` is locked once the event starts; `measure_presence` only affects counting from then on).
- **`/api/me` bypasses the me-cache, read and write, under impersonation.** The payload has session-derived fields, so one userId entry must not serve both the real and the impersonating session.

#### Testing traps

- **An `/api/me` flag cannot be tested by a before/after read or a direct DB write.** The 2 s me-cache returns the old payload and a direct write invalidates nothing; use two participants.
- **A `/metrics` delta read straight after the requests is always zero.** The body is memoised for 1 s; wait over 1 s.
- **Role and scope cache tests flush before they warm.** Admin-session requests to `/api/admin/*` and `/metrics` fill `_roleCache` (`eventAdminGuard` reads the role only there, so `GET /api/csrf-token` warms nothing), scoped handlers `_scopeCache`; a hit never refreshes its timestamp, so "warm, change, send" can read an entry about to expire and pass without the bust. Flush with an idempotent superadmin write, warm, change by SQL, send, and assert all of it ran inside 2 s (lock: `tests/desk-invite-inflight-authz.api.spec.js`).

### Recurring rules — the doctrine

Each rule is stated once here, for comments and other sections to cite ("*Recurring rules*"). Keep the reason: without one a rule gets "simplified" away.

#### Mechanical guards — a scanner, not a sentence

- **The lasting guard for an enumerable rule is a SOURCE scanner, not prose.** Prose cannot list route registrations, sign-in doors or renderers, and the site that breaks is always the one added later.
- **A count in prose is not an enumeration.** "All three consumers" drifts silently; where a count matters, say it is untrustworthy and name what to grep.
- **Scope a scanner so it cannot flag correct code.** Teach it the legitimate idioms and demand a decision per item (see *Writing a source scanner*); a scanner wrong about working code gets deleted.
- **A scanner strips comments first and carries an anti-vacuity floor** (see *Writing a source scanner*): a fix's comment quotes the forbidden pattern, and a renamed symbol matches nothing; either lets a broken tree pass. Where: `stripJsComments`, exported by `tests/doc-registry.js` (`_stripJsComments` in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js` is a describe-local, byte-identical copy); require the export, never add another copy.
- **When a finding names one site, grep every sibling of the same invariant,** check the existing locks, and add a scanner if the class can recur (see *The mechanical locks*).
- **A doc that ENUMERATES must declare it.** If a doc in `_REGISTRY_DOCS` (`tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`; grep it for the list) says *every*, *all N* or *the single exception*, or lists call sites, add a line to its `<!-- DOC-REGISTRY -->` block. Only declared claims are checked: inferring them from prose flagged correct sentences. Where: `tests/doc-registry.js`.

#### Evidence — what a passing test proves, and what it does not

- **A lock is a lock only once it has been seen failing with its fix reverted.** A green suite says nothing about a lock that never ran.
- **A surviving mutation is ambiguous.** Before calling a lock vacuous, confirm the mutation landed (a `sed` that matched nothing looks like a survivor), hit the right site (not an edit branch or wire projection) and can change behaviour.
- **Pair every positive assertion with a negative control,** or a build that does nothing passes. When two mechanisms produce the same visible outcome, assert the internal signal that separates them (e.g. an audit count).
- **A route matrix asserting only a status per cell cannot catch a row-level leak:** an admitted route with an unscoped response reads as covered. Row-scope locks assert CONTENT by value (the out-of-scope rater's email appears nowhere in the payload) beside a superadmin control (lock: `tests/event-admin-row-scope.api.spec.js`).
- **Diagnose from the failing NODE the test prints, not from a derivation that fits.** State-dependent DOM can make a permanent failure look flaky; when a probe disagrees with a screenshot, suspect the probe's substring first.
- **No conditional-vacuous assertions** (`if (rows.length) expect(...)`); build the state the assertion needs. A guard must not key on the thing under test: "check only when CSS reads `fixed`" stops checking when `fixed` is deleted.
- **Make fixtures distinguishable.** For a sort, vary score, time and solved count and assert that rows move; a comparator lock asserts every permutation sorts to ONE order, never a specific order (a build ignoring the tie-break passes that), plus anti-vacuity controls (a comparator returning 0 is also permutation-invariant under the stable `Array#sort`; lock: `tests/presence-display.spec.js`). For a "must not happen" check, use an endpoint the mechanism actually covers (not one on `_SESSION_SLIDE_SKIP`).
- **Two layers enforcing one rule each need a witness the other cannot satisfy.** Pin the client half on `page.on('request')` with a positive control; if the fix is "the client must not depend on the server", take the server's answer away (`route(...).abort()`).
- **Gate axe on impact (`AXE_GATED_IMPACTS`, `tests/ui-admin-helpers.js`), never on a rule-id allowlist,** which discards critical violations nobody predicted. To scan a control that renders only for one kind or state, build that state and assert the control rendered first.
- **Lock layout defects as geometry (edges, widths, distances, narrow viewport), not markup structure.** A structural assertion passes against a new layout that brings the skew back another way.
- **"We could not make it happen" is a claim about the test, not the product.** Build a hook that holds the window open (pattern: `POST /api/test/admin-commit-delay`) and lock the guard.

#### Decisions taken once, because they cannot be taken twice

- **One-way doors are decided before shipping, never tuned afterwards.** They include printed alphabets and code lengths (Crockford minus I/L/O/U; 12-character certificate code); the five-minute presence bucket (stored counters count buckets, so a change re-scales history); opaque stored kinds `booth`/`tournament`/`program` (a rename changes the label, not the data); who counts as a late entrant (joined after the effective end; re-decided by the operator 29 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026, see *Late entry*); the finishers-only restriction on the presence tie-break (`lib/leaderboard.js` `compareLeaderboardEntries`); and the `challenge_events` type set (a new type silently lowers every solve rate).
- **Accept-and-ignore is worse than no control** (a stored field nothing honours, a checkbox never sent, a policy key nobody reads). Refuse with a named 400 or do not offer the field.
- **Gate the affordance and its explanation together, and compute the offer from the same predicate the route enforces.** A menu that shrinks without a reason gets reported as a broken feature.
- **Fail closed on unknown values and write predicates positively** (`=== 'live'`, allow-lists, an unknown `needs:` tag restricts), so a value invented later refuses rather than permits. Exception: an unknown stored KIND means a rollback and keeps serving (`lib/event-policy.js`).

#### Surfaces a human actually meets

- **Never dim a text-bearing surface with `opacity`.** The text composites below AA, unseen by axe (it samples the resting state). Mark inactive items with a dashed edge and a muted token.
- **A search or jump input is a SIBLING of the container its handler re-renders, never a child.** A re-render inside it swallows keystrokes and moves the caret, sync fetch or async. Its lock types with `pressSequentially` and a delay straddling the debounce (`.fill()` is atomic).
- **On a 200-always endpoint the client branches on the payload's `ok`, never on `r.ok`.** Ordinary 4xx responses feed scanner reputation, which blocks a whole address (exclusions: see *Scanner reputation*). Locks assert both halves: the server's 200 and the client's failure report.
- **A skip/drop counter returned in JSON needs a human-facing consumer in the same change** (e.g. import drop counts; see *Import rules*).
- **Renaming visible text includes the accessible name** (`aria-label`, tooltips), or the two disagree for exactly the users who cannot see the badge.
- **A control's accessible name opens with the words it shows, then says what a press does** (WCAG 2.5.3, axe `label-content-name-mismatch`: "<name> — open this event"). So a whole card is never the button, text-glyph icons are painted from `data-glyph`, not DOM text, and spans in one control are space-separated (a name check joins their text with none). Lock: `tests/label-in-name.spec.js`.

#### Fixtures and environments

- **Every test owns its participant and competition and asserts its preconditions.** Assign silently skips ineligible users (see *Competition-fixture checklist*, item 7).
- **A test that writes a global setting restores the hard-coded DEFAULT in a `finally` in the test body.** Not what it found, and not in `afterAll`: hooks run in registration order, after the `tests/isolated-test.js` guard. Of global settings, that guard checks only `settings.platform_name` (its other checks: see *Test flakiness*); its message and `tests/ui-admin-misc.spec.js` still say "what it found", contrary to this rule.
- **A pin that keeps a value OUT must not defer to it.** Never write `process.env.X || default` for a guard against an inherited environment.
- **CI red but local green: read the WHOLE log first** (never `grep -v` a log you do not understand), list what differs (env vars, Node major, cwd, uid, timezone) and reproduce in the pinned CI image. A flake in one environment is evidence about the product until proven otherwise.

#### The driver

- **better-sqlite3 `db.transaction(fn)` opens a DEFERRED `BEGIN`; only `.immediate()` takes the write lock at the start.** If another PROCESS holds it (`sqlite3` shell, `scripts/purge.sh --apply`), a read-then-write transaction fails `SQLITE_BUSY` at the upgrade, ignoring `busy_timeout`, so one whose failure would refuse a user uses `.immediate()`. Argue mutual exclusion from one synchronous connection in one process, never from lock mode.
- **better-sqlite3 spreads an array bind value into positional bindings,** so a wrong-typed body field becomes a 500. Type-guard every bound value (`_firstNonStringField`; see *Security hardening highlights*).

#### Changing code and docs

- **A fix is a new draft.** Defects concentrate in remedy code: review it adversarially and mutation-check it. Re-verify a reviewer's suggested wording against the code; read sub-agents' reasoning and warnings, not just verdicts. Never edit the working tree a review or verification workflow is reading; verifiers check pristine `git show HEAD:` copies in an isolated directory, or they refute real findings as "already applied".
- **Keep "true of the code" separate from "introduced by this change".** Exception: a pre-existing defect this change's own scanner or guide certifies as closed belongs to this change.
- **When relaxing a guard, grep every gate that reads the value** (`start_time` feeds `competitionStatus`, which every "locked once started" rule keys on). When removing a response field, grep for the field callers read, not a discriminator beside it.
- **Fix a boundary bug in the shared function, not with a local shortcut.** Move hoisted code byte-for-byte, never retyped; when a comment says two things cannot drift, check that one is not a copy.
- **`fetch` resolves at the response HEADERS, so an abort deadline must survive the body read and a staleness or erasure guard runs after the body is parsed.** A `clearTimeout` in a `finally` leaves a stalled upstream unbounded (it can wedge the CxOne worker pool). Server calls use `lib/http.js` `fetchWithTimeout` (its body readers clear the timer); the client hands the deadline to `core/api.js` `readBody` (see *SPA conventions*).
- **Never cache a failure** (`catch { cache = [] }`); cache only successes and retry. Where: `lib/comp-allowlist.js`. Exception: `lib/time.js` `zoneFormatter` remembers a zone Intl refuses (RangeError only), as zone data is compiled into the binary and fixed for the process; any other error is retried.
- **No empty catch block or empty `.catch` handler in server code.** A deliberately ignored error goes to `swallowed("<module>:<purpose>", e)` (`lib/best-effort.js`): same control flow, nothing rethrown, its name and code (never its message) logged at debug level, or at warn with `{level:"warn"}` for a failure an operator must see at the default level. It requires nothing at load time and logs only via an already-loaded `lib/log.js` (else it counts, `hack_arena_swallow_unrecorded_total`), so any module may use it, `db.js` and `lib/http.js` included. `lib/log.js` counts its own failures instead (recursion; `hack_arena_log_write_failures_total`). Lock: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js` Docs hygiene.
- **Write control characters as `\uXXXX`, never as literal bytes,** which are invisible in review.
- **A TDZ read (a `const` used above its declaration in a long handler) passes `node --check` and fails as a 500.** Resolve values at the top of the handler.
- **A behaviour change updates every doc that describes it in the same change:** the human-facing `*.md` docs, guides and swagger (grep them for the old spelling); a release note does not count. Guides and swagger state current behaviour without version tokens or history, keeping the reason (operator ruling). A new module, table, route or env var reaches its reference section in the same change; a split or renamed spec means grepping CLAUDE.md for its citations: a lock the reader cannot open is a claim. Nothing checks either.
- **A doc truth sweep uses three methods:** check each claim against the code; walk the changes since the last sweep for what users can now see; enumerate the UI from source (menu items, form controls per event kind, participant surfaces, outbound emails). Only the enumeration finds whole features the docs never mention.

### The mechanical locks — what is already pinned

- **The tests are the authority for what is pinned, not this file.** A prose rule can be "simplified" away; a scanner goes red. Before adding a prose rule, grep the tests for a scanner that already holds it.
- **Default home: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js` > `Docs hygiene`.** A lock gets its own spec only when it needs a fixture or covers a family. Shared strippers: `_stripJsComments` there, `stripJsComments` and `stripShellComments` in `tests/doc-registry.js`.

#### Writing a source scanner
- **Clear a red scanner by fixing the SITE, never by weakening the scanner.** Sole exception: a scanner wrong about working code — the next person will silence it, so it must not ship.
- **Strip comments first, in ONE left-to-right alternation over `//` and `/* */`.** Block-then-line lets `accept="image/*"` in a `//` comment open a phantom block that swallows real code. The shared stripper (`tests/doc-registry.js` `stripJsComments`) keeps a quoted string (`"…"`, `'…'`) whole, so a `/*` inside one (a spec's route glob) opens no comment; template literals are not tokenised (a regex literal or a nested `${`…`}` would flip the pairing), so a `/*` inside one still blinds it, and the line-keeping copies (`_stripJsCommentsKeepLines`) are still string-blind: source avoids `/*` in any string (`lib/middleware.js` spells `"multipart"`; lock: functional-core-b Docs hygiene). When reporting lines, blank comment characters but keep `\n`.
- **Carry an anti-vacuity floor** (minimum match count or a planted control); a renamed symbol otherwise leaves it matching nothing and passing.
- **Teach it the legitimate idioms rather than exempting sites:** the duplicate-CSS scanner accepts `var()`/gradient/prefixed fallbacks and `vh`→`dvh`; the `req.body` typeof scanner accepts `typeof`, `_firstNonStringField`, `_groupNameOk` and a `typeof req.body[f]` loop, never `String(X)` (it throws on an object whose `toString` is not callable); a dedup fingerprint matches the predicate, not the JOIN.
- **Demand a decision per item, never blanket coverage:** required-or-exempt with a reason (`lib/schema-required.js`, `scripts/backup.sh` `BACKUP_COVERS`/`BACKUP_EXEMPT`, drift-spec `EXCLUDED`, one permission per admin route in `lib/rbac.js` `ADMIN_ROUTE_PERMISSIONS`). Check both directions where a stale entry does harm; delete an exemption once its reason is false — it passes regardless.
- **Grow the registry with the code:** paginate an admin table → add its `{rows, search}` to `PAGINATED_TABLES` (`onChange` names a box that narrows on the server only once committed by a change event, Enter, Tab or blur, like Audit's Actor box; the filter-first lock checks only listed tables and rejects selectors absent from `public/index.html`).
- **Match paths word-bounded** (`attachments` must not match `attachments-staging`); **pin shell scripts by control flow** (branch holds `exit 1`, no bare `continue`), not substrings.
- **Scanner text trips other scanners:** an identifier in a regex or message string survives stripping — build it from parts; an unbalanced `"{{"` derails brace counting — use balanced literals.
- **Never hard-code another spec's filename** (ENOENT once it is split); assert the recipe, not a keyword prose also contains.

#### Lock families
| Family | Examples |
|---|---|
| One definition | lockout constants in `lib/lockout.js`; `EMAIL_FROM` in `lib/email.js`; downloads via `public/js/ui/download.js`; the late-entry refusal `lateEntryForbiddenMessage` (`lib/late-entry.js`); probe signatures in `lib/scanner-classify.js`; `LATE_ENTRY_STAT` (`lib/badge.js`), pushed by both share card and certificate; a team member's `tm_` fingerprint and removed ledger form (`lib/team-recipient.js`) |
| Decision-per-item manifests | schema manifest vs every `ALTER` in `db.js`; `tests/tasks-drift.spec.js`, `tests/competitions-drift.spec.js`; backup manifest; image-reference manifest (`lib/image-refs.js` vs every text or blob column, `tests/image-sweep.spec.js`) |
| Request/response shape | `swagger-conformance`, `paging-clamp`, `request-body-shape`, `caught-error-echo` specs |
| Placement | `adminAuthzDenied(`, `impersonatingHandDenied(`, `participantSessionDenied(` after the last await, before the first write; limiter after auth guard |
| Audit and logs | `runTxnWithMirrorBuffer`; `reqContext(req)` spread; `tests/request-log-context.spec.js`; no empty catch (`swallowed`, `lib/best-effort.js`) |
| Event kinds | `tests/event-kind-naming.spec.js`, `tests/event-policy-wire.spec.js` |
| CSS and markup | `FONT_SIZE_CEILING`, `tests/type-scale-scanner.spec.js`, `tests/radius-tokens.spec.js`, `dvh` siblings; `tests/interactive-contrast.spec.js`, `tests/screen-inert.spec.js`, `tests/label-in-name.spec.js` |
| HTML sinks | `tests/html-sink-escape.spec.js` (scanner `tests/html-sink-scan.js`): every value reaching showModal's `message`, setSafeHtml or innerHTML, followed through variables, helpers and call sites, is esc()-ed or a taught idiom; reviewed exceptions sit in its `ALLOW` with a reason, checked both ways; teach it any new HTML sink or HTML-taking option. Same spec: no `public/js` module writes `innerHTML`/`outerHTML` or calls `insertAdjacentHTML` outside `core/trusted-types.js` (`setSafeHtml` is the one door; classic scripts unscanned); the trusted guide renderer (`public/help-renderer.js`), run through the vendored marked, escapes every raw HTML block and every text token itself (marked leaves text after a raw `<code>`, `<kbd>` or `<pre>` unescaped) |
| SPA wiring | `tests/spa-wiring.spec.js`: every literal dispatcher-attribute name (`data-action` and its change, input, enter and mouse siblings) is registered with its dispatcher; every literal `/api/` path in `public/js` is a registered route (`${…}` names are not judged) |
| Deploy and CI | `tests/prune-runtime-deps.spec.js`, `tests/ci-config-lockstep.spec.js` |
| Docs and copy | `{{platformName}}`; `DOC-REGISTRY` manifests; `tests/release-notes.spec.js`; every guide screenshot has a `.dark.png` twin; cross-document anchors resolve; swagger's `registered_via` enums hold every value written or tested; no copy says to unassign from a frozen competition (`tests/frozen-remedy.spec.js`), that the event type is immutable, or to close late entry by setting the deadline to now |

#### Client/server mirror registry
`public/js` is browser-only ESM and cannot import `lib/`, so each copy is irreducible and diffed by a Docs-hygiene lock unless named. Change both sides together; never add a third copy.

| Client copy | Mirrors |
|---|---|
| `ui/icons.js` `DIFF_PTS`; hint-cost preview and its "% deduction" copy in `features/participant-tasks.js` | `lib/scoring.js` `POINTS`, `HINT_DEDUCTION_RATE`; `routes/challenges.js`, `routes/participant.js`, `server.js` may not re-declare the ladder or open-code the rate. The wrong-answer `PENALTY` copy ("5 points" in `features/choice-tap.js`) is NOT locked |
| `features/challenges-admin.js` `TF_MAX_CHOICES` | `lib/choices.js` `MAX_CHOICES` (lock: `tests/booth-choice-tap-edges.spec.js`) |
| `features/challenges-admin.js` `KNOWN_NEEDS` (the editor's needs choices) | `lib/challenge-fit.js` `KNOWN_NEEDS`, keys and wording (lock: `tests/topics-ui.spec.js`) |
| `ui/format.js` `compareTaskNumbers` | `lib/competitions.js`, byte-identical body |
| `features/templates-admin.js` `_DIFF_LEVEL`, `_byDifficultyThenTitle` (the template editor's order) | `lib/competitions.js` `_assignTaskNumbers` (`diffLevel`, `ORDER BY t.difficulty, t.title`: SQLite BINARY is code-point order, never JS's code-unit `<`; lock: `tests/templates-editor-ui.spec.js`, spawned numbers match the editor's order) |
| `ui/format.js` `earnsPlacement` | `lib/badge.js` `earnsPlacement`, byte-identical body (lock: `tests/placement-points.spec.js`) |
| `ui/format.js` `DRY_RUN_TAG`; `PUBLIC_DOMAIN_TAG`, `publicDomainNearMiss` | `lib/competitions.js`; `lib/event-admin.js` (plus a behavioural matrix: identical stubs pass a body diff) |
| `ui/csv.js` `neutraliseFormula` | `lib/csv.js` |
| `ui/download.js` `fileNameSlug` | `lib/attachments.js` `fileNameSlug` (the top-three ZIP's file names), body held identical (lock: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`, the sweep #72 arm) |
| `ui/theme.js` `LOOKS`/`AMBIENTS` ids; `public/theme-boot.js` `LOOKS`, `AMBIENTS`, `DEFAULT_AMBIENT`, `HOUSE_LOOK`/`HOUSE_MODE` and its precedence block | `lib/platform-look.js` `LOOK_IDS`, `AMBIENT_IDS`, `MODES`; look and ambient ids, `DEFAULT_AMBIENT` and `HOUSE_LOOK`/`HOUSE_MODE` are locked against `scripts/theme/looks.js` and swagger's enums (`tests/theme-tokens.spec.js`); LOOK and AMBIENT precedence (`ui/theme.js` `_lookPreference`, `_ambientPreference`) are held by behaviour, never a diff (`tests/platform-look.spec.js`, app booted and `theme-boot.js` alone); MODE precedence (`_themePreference`) is held only for a locked mode — change both copies together |

(The code lifetime is not copied: the shell carries `<body data-login-code-ttl>`, GET /api/config `login_code_ttl_ms`.) |

#### Stated gaps — what these do NOT cover
- **The abort-deadline scanner cannot judge hand-over sites** (`fetchTransientRetry`, `lib/http.js` `fetchWithTimeout`). The guarantee is the behavioural `GET /api/test/stall-body` test; do not cite the scanner as it, nor delete it.
- **The paginated-row-lookup scanner matches only the `{hasText}` option**, not `tr:has-text()`; widening it flags working code.
- **The typing scanner (`tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`) holds the Groups, Events, Challenges and People search boxes, and only a literal locator typed into (fill, pressSequentially, type, press, clear, directly or after first/last/nth);** an aliased locator escapes, and the other admin search boxes (Submissions, Audit, Logs, Rankings, Analytics) are not held.
- **The limiter-placement scanner checks against the FIRST auth guard only** (`requireAdmin, exportLimiter, requireSuperAdmin` passes); last-guard placement is held by review.
- **The alert-copy mirror checks a fixed list of branch marker phrases on both sides:** a new branch added to one side only, or a changed value inside a branch (e.g. the deadline's timezone), passes. Add its marker to the list or write its own test.
- **The doc registry proves only that every site, module, reason and symbol a doc declares is decided about and still exists** — not that the docs are true. Rationale, negative claims and a rewrite that keeps every symbol live pass.
- **`tests/screen-inert.spec.js` skips HTML and `public/vendor/**`**, and an aliased read escapes it.
- **The multer-import scanner (`tests/multer-limits.api.spec.js`) matches only a literal module name;** an aliased require escapes it.
- **The spawned-server readiness scanner (`tests/spawned-server-readiness.spec.js`) sees a site only where `server.js` is spelled in the spawn's arguments, and reads its readiness in the text after it (to the next site);** a path held in a variable escapes it.
- **The file-name slug scanner (`tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`) sees a copy only by its Unicode property escapes (`\p{…}`, `\P{…}`) in `public/js` and `public/*.js`;** one spelled without any (code-point ranges, a per-character loop, `Intl.Segmenter`) passes.
- **The `req.body` typeof scanner (`tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`) sees only `req.body.X` and destructured fields, and accepts a guard anywhere in the file** (except its pass-through shape, a body value handed bare to a call or behind `(req.body && req.body.X) ||`, whose guard must be in the statement or the callee on its reviewed `PASS_VALIDATORS` list; single-line only): an aliased read or an unguarded site beside a guarded one of the same name passes; behavioural `{toString: 1}` cases (`tests/multer-limits.api.spec.js`) hold the attachment `display_name` sites.
- **`tests/prune-runtime-deps.spec.js` runs a glibc copy;** the musl path is proven only by a real image build. `tests/ci-config-lockstep.spec.js` cannot prove the pinned digest was built on its base; the CI Dockerfile's build-time browser check is that half.
- **The bypass scanner in `tests/csrf-cross-tab.spec.js` sees a write only where a literal method sits within 300 characters of an `_origFetch` call, or an `XMLHttpRequest`;** a non-literal init escapes it (the profile-gate retry is pinned by name instead), and it proves a site asks and registers, not that the answer waits (its behavioural arms do). Untested: a sent door's hold marking the token dead when its answer takes over 30 s or is lost; and while a door's request never settles, no token is adopted, so writes answer "nothing was sent" until the browser fails it.
- **The `tests/label-in-name.spec.js` source scanner checks containment, not "opens with", and cannot see markup built by DOM calls;** its browser arms hold "opens with" only on the screens they visit.
- **The HTML-sink scanner (`tests/html-sink-scan.js`) trusts some properties by NAME** (`id`, `rank`, `score`, `*_count`, `skipped*`: `NUMERIC_PROPS`/`NUMERIC_PROP_RE`), so a server field so named that carried text would pass; it follows a helper only through direct and aliased calls, and reports a helper also passed as a value, an expression nested too deep to follow, and any call it cannot follow.
- **The Audit page's per-type withholding for readers without the raw logs is a hand-kept list** (`routes/audit.js` `LOG_EVENT_RAW_ONLY_FIELDS`, `LOG_EVENT_RAW_ONLY_TYPES`): nothing makes a new orphaned event type naming an event decide what it carries; only its request context is withheld whatever its type (lock: `tests/audit-row-permissions.spec.js`, planted rows by value).
- **The look-painting scanner (`tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`, `appearanceSettled`) judges a file, not a paint:** one call anywhere passes the whole file, a paint after a later navigation included, and a write through `dataset`, `toggleAttribute` or a name it cannot follow escapes it.
- **`tests/admin-mobile-menu-overlap.spec.js` measures the unscrolled page (plus the one scrolled tab-switch focus case), Chromium only.**
- **The address-lookup scanner (functional-core-b Docs hygiene) sees only a literal address or nickname column, or an interpolated `LOWER(${…})`;** a column built by concatenation, or an OR whose other arm has no index, escapes it.
- **The zone-formatter scanner (`tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js` Docs hygiene) sees a construction only where `Intl.DateTimeFormat` or a `.toLocale…String(` call is spelled out in `lib/`, `routes/`, `server.js` and `db.js`;** an aliased constructor or a call in `scripts/` escapes it, and it cannot tell a date from a number, so it refuses a number's `toLocaleString` too (also one formatter per call: keep a shared `Intl.NumberFormat`).
- **The JS opacity scanner (`tests/js-opacity-scan.js`) sees a write only where the property is spelled `opacity`** (a member, a quoted key or setter argument, an object key, a CSS declaration in a string, a markup attribute): a computed name (`style[prop]`) or CSS assembled with the word apart from its value escapes it.
- **The `contentEditable` scanner (`tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`) sees a write only where the name is spelled** (a member, a bracketed or object-literal key, an attribute call, `designMode`): a computed name escapes it.

## Tests

Playwright, Chromium by default. Harness: `playwright.config.js`, `tests/isolated-test.js`, `tests/server-env.js`, `tests/global-setup.js`; background in DEVELOPMENT.md (*The dev loop*, *Test architecture*). Fixtures: *Competition-fixture checklist — READ BEFORE BUILDING ONE*.

### Per-file isolation

- **The isolation boundary is the spec FILE, not the worker.** Once per file a `beforeAll` in `tests/isolated-test.js` SIGKILLs the worker's server, wipes its data dir, spawns a fresh `server.js` and re-seeds, so no file inherits another's database or caches. Within a file tests run serially and state carries over.
- **Every spec requires `./isolated-test`, never `@playwright/test`;** a direct import silently opts the file out and breaks a later one (lock: Docs hygiene in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`).
- **Keep `delete require.cache[__filename]` at the bottom of `isolated-test.js`** (lock: `tests/harness-isolation.spec.js`), or hooks attach only to each worker's first file; worker-lifetime state lives on `globalThis.__hackarenaIsolatedTest`.
- **Hooks register only while a file loads;** a re-require inside a running test registers nothing. Helpers require `./isolated-test` lazily (`tests/ui-admin-helpers.js` takes only `expect` from `@playwright/test` at top level).
- **Never share a module-scope cache of seeded ids across files:** the next file re-seeds, so it serves stale ids (inexplicable 404s). `tests/seeded-state.js` is a factory; each file calls `makeState()`.
- **`seedTestData` (`tests/helpers.js`) must stay safe to re-run on one server:** `_spawnAndSeed` retries it in place, so each step adopts what a failed attempt made (a competition or challenge of its name and shape; a refused shape throws by name), and no answer, sign-up or enrolment is sent twice (lock: `tests/seed-retry.api.spec.js`).
- **Readiness is `/api/config` reporting `server_started_at` newer than the spawn, never a `/healthz` 200** (it answers for whoever holds the port). A server that dies mid-file fails that test by name with its last output (`_serverDeathError`).
- **`_killCurrent` always waits for the port to free, killing by port when it has no child handle.** Every failure path before readiness kills its child: a booting server holds no port, and two servers on one data dir fail with `duplicate column name`.
- **Kill-by-port reads `/proc/net/tcp{,6}` and fd tables, never `lsof`, `ss` or `execFileSync`** (lock), and kills only a `server.js` pid whose cwd is this repo. **Never `pkill -f "node server.js"`:** it kills the operator's dev server and other agents' runs.
- **Every test server boots for the first time;** to test second-boot behaviour, spawn your own server twice on one dir.
- **Require db-reaching app code lazily, inside tests:** `db.js` opens its file at require time, so a module-scope require reads the previous file's unlinked database; `recycleServer()` purges `db.js` and `lib/` from `require.cache`. **No spec may require `lib/email`** (lock): it opens a second DB, touches `.unsubscribe-secret`, schedules an SMTP probe and can `process.exit(1)` the worker. Build unsubscribe tokens with `crypto` like `generateUnsubscribeToken`: `b64url(json).b64url(sig)`, HMAC-SHA256 over the RAW JSON `{e,x}`, keyed by the RAW bytes of `DATA_DIR/.unsubscribe-secret`.

### Ports, data directories, hermetic env

- **Worker `i` listens on `TEST_PORT_BASE + i` (default 4567) and its data dir follows the base** via `dataDirFor(i)` (`tests/server-env.js`). Take paths from `TEST_DATA_DIR`/`TEST_DB_PATH` (`tests/helpers.js`) or `dataDirFor()`, never `TEST_PARALLEL_INDEX`, which ignores the base and hits another run's database (superadmin 401s) or none (a vacuous `0 === 0`). Derive child ports from `PORT_BASE`.
- **`serverEnv()` (`tests/server-env.js`) is the single hermetic env for every test server.** Built from scratch at the code's defaults plus test values (rate limiting off, an 80/min login backstop, `NODE_ENV=test`), so a developer's shell (SMTP port, `TRUST_PROXY`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, CxOne autorun) cannot leak in; only `PATH`, `HOME`, `MINIFY_JS` and `CI` pass through. Add new vars here; never copy it.
- **`playwright.config.js` pins the WORKER's `DATA_DIR`/`UPLOADS_DIR` (`*-test-worker-<base>`) with no `||`/`??`** (lock): the operator's shell exports the dev `DATA_DIR` and `lib/log`/`db.js` act at require time, so a defaulting pin would migrate the dev database. A spec needing another dir sets `process.env` itself. The worker's in-process DB is not the server's; open that via `TEST_DB_PATH`.
- **Concurrent runs need `TEST_PORT_BASE`s at least 200 apart** (worker slots plus spawner ports to +199). `tests/global-setup.js` refuses to start ("Ports already in use") if any worker or `SPAWNER_OFFSETS` port is listening, before touching disk. `test:independence` shards use the clean bases from 4700 in steps of 200 and `test:changed` 4300; keep clear of both. **A base must keep every worker and spawner port off `FETCH_BLOCKED_PORTS` (`tests/server-env.js`, e.g. 5060, 6000, 6566, 6665–6697):** fetch() and the browser refuse them ("bad port"), so a server there boots and is never reached; global-setup refuses such a base (lock: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`).

### Workers, retries, scheduling

- **Defaults `WORKERS` 24 (2 under `CROSS_BROWSER=1`) and `RETRIES` 1, in `playwright.config.js`;** CI pins 4, the capture job 1. `scripts/test-balance.cjs`'s `WORKERS` default must match, or an unbalanced suite reads as balanced; README.md and DEVELOPMENT.md quote it. The suite is startup-bound: file count and balance beat worker count.
- **`RETRIES=1` hides a flake that passes on retry; `RETRIES=0` is strict.** "Fixed" needs a consecutive `RETRIES=0` sweep or `--repeat-each=20+`. If each run fails a different set, baseline before hardening anything: HEAD in its own `git worktree`, own `TEST_PORT_BASE`, same `WORKERS` and machine.
- **Files run longest first:** `chromiumBuckets()` splits chromium into up to four projects, all named `chromium`, by time from `tests/file-timings.json` (unlisted first). **Refresh it with `npm run test:balance -- --write` after adding or splitting a file.** A file never spans workers: split one over budget, moving shared helpers to a `*-fixture.js` or `tests/ui-admin-helpers.js`.
- **`actionTimeout` 20 s and `navigationTimeout` 30 s are ceilings; `expect` keeps 5 s.** At 0, one hang eats the 60 s test, blames the wrong step and starves `finally` cleanup.

### Spawned servers

- **A spec that spawns its own server binds `PORT_BASE + <literal offset>` registered in `SPAWNER_OFFSETS` (`tests/global-setup.js`).** A `functional-core-b` scanner checks both ways that offsets are registered, unique, >= 100 (worker slots below) and < `PORT_STRIDE` (200); `admin-sentinels-v8133.api.spec.js` refuses an offset plus a variable term. Both build the identifier from parts to dodge the missing-import scanner.
- **Every spawner spec must call `guardFixedPortSpec()` (`tests/isolated-test.js`) in `beforeEach`;** a scanner in `admin-sentinels-v8133.api.spec.js` fails any spec that binds a spawner port without it. It refuses `--repeat-each` with several workers, where copies race for one port, so run such specs with `WORKERS=1 npm run test:changed`.
- **A child's readiness is a positive identity check:** `server_started_at` no earlier than the spawn (`spawnedAt` read just before `spawn()`), then its own `.superadmin-password`; a status or a TCP connect answers for whoever holds the port. `tests/spawned-server.js` `readySpawnedServer` does both, and every spec spawning server.js to talk to it waits through it or asks `server_started_at` itself, a child never meant to be ready decided with its reason (scanner: `tests/spawned-server-readiness.spec.js`). Capture both streams; drain the port in `afterAll` (`kill()` returns before it frees). Keep the child's `close` as a promise from the spawn: `boot()` stops polling once it fires, and `kill()` awaits it, as a fresh `once("exit")` on a child already gone never settles and hangs `afterAll` (pattern: `tests/team-report-delivery.spec.js`). Witness boot errors with `spawnSync` (`execFileSync().toString()` is stdout only).
- **Every node child spawned from `tests/` or `scripts/` states its `DATA_DIR` and `UPLOADS_DIR` at the spawn site, its own or deliberately deleted** (scanner): server.js sweeps `UPLOADS_DIR` at boot. **Every spec spawning `server.js` pins `NODE_OPTIONS: ""`** (scanner), as should any child whose exit code or stderr you assert.
- **For server-env conditions (`TRUST_PROXY`, secure cookies, `LOG_SCAN_BUDGET`, real mail), spawn a server; never change the shared server's env.** Its SMTP relay is dead by design; read delivered bytes via `tests/smtp-capture.js` (`hold()`/`release()` park sends), never `perf/` tooling.

### STANDING RULES for adding tests

1. **Every new test is INDEPENDENT.** Register your own participants and events, never assert on a shared seeded user, put setup in `beforeAll`/`afterAll` (never `test("setup: …")`) and set toggles explicitly. `npm run test:independence` runs each test alone in a pristine DB; it catches tests that NEED predecessors, not ones broken by new neighbours.
2. **Keep per-file time BALANCED:** `npm run test:balance` fails any file over 1.5x `total / WORKERS`.
3. **Mutation-verify every lock** (see *Mutation verification* and *Recurring rules*).
4. **Own your fixtures and assert preconditions** (fixture checklist); find a flake's root cause instead of widening a wait.

- `test:independence` and `test:balance` are manual diagnostics, outside `npm test` and CI. `npm run test:changed` runs touched specs twice with retries on.
- **Sign in via `page.request` (`apiLogin`/`loginViaApi`) BEFORE the first `page.goto`, once per browser context** (another user gets a second context): an SPA started anonymously, or holding the pre-regeneration CSRF token, 401s its polls and its interceptor POSTs `/api/logout`. Probe readiness with `page.request`, by principal (`GET /api/me` 401s for the built-in superadmin). Finish API set-up before the browser login; fetch CSRF after the login that uses it.
- **A refusing `requireAdmin`/`requireSuperAdmin` sets `req.session.isAdmin`/`isSuperAdmin` false** (`lib/middleware.js`): order a two-gate test so the first refusal cannot mask the second (superadmin route first). `PUT /api/admin/participants/:id` ignores `role`; use `POST …/:id/role`.
- **Pin a found-but-deferred defect with `test.fail()`** so the fix turns it red. Before deleting a "duplicate", check what only it reaches; rename a test whose name overclaims.
- On a generic 500, attach `serverLogTail()` (`tests/helpers.js`) before the next recycle wipes it.

### Mutation verification

- **Mutate in an `rsync` copy on its own `TEST_PORT_BASE`, never in-tree:** nodemon (`npm run dev`) watches `lib/`, `routes/`, `public/`, so an in-tree edit restarts the operator's dev server and rotates its superadmin password.
- **Anchor excludes (`--exclude /data`, not `data`):** unanchored also drops `public/js/data/` and every mutation reads as killed. Check the copy against `git ls-files` and run it green before mutating.
- **Restore from a pre-mutation copy, never `git checkout`** (it discards uncommitted work); re-snapshot after each fix. Each parallel agent needs its own copy: cap their number, delete each when done and watch `df` (a full disk breaks SQLite writes and the operator's dev server).
- Confirm a mutation landed before calling it a survivor (see *Recurring rules*). Stub the network (a recording `curl` on PATH) before mutating a script that talks to a server. A kill in an unrelated test means that file is order-dependent.

### Screenshot captures and `_*.spec.js`

- **`npm test` never runs `tests/_*.spec.js`** (`testIgnore` unless `CAPTURE_SCREENSHOTS=1`): a local `_screenshots.spec.js` run (`npm run docs:shots`) restamps `docs/screenshots/MANIFEST.json` and rewrites nearly every PNG. Regenerate only what a change touches; revert unrelated churn. The MANIFEST's `version` marks the run, not each capture (`counts`: on disk; per-theme `regenerated`: mtime >= module-scope `RUN_STARTED_AT`).
- **CI `capture-suite` runs `_screenshots.spec.js` (light theme, `WORKERS=1`, nothing committed),** so hiding or gating a UI surface fails the merge gate. Grep captures when changing a nav item or tier gate; capture superadmin-only surfaces inside the `superadmin` describe. `_ui-polish.spec.js` is not in the job.

### Visual regression

- `tests/visual-regression.spec.js` gates stable anonymous screens only (login, register); Chromium/Linux baselines, self-hosted fonts. **Never screenshot live or admin surfaces** (timers, leaderboard rows, ECharts); structure is `tests/layout-integrity.spec.js`.
- **Tolerance is `maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.0005`; tightening it is an operator decision.** The floor is 0 px because `ready()` overwrites `#platform-version` with a literal `PINNED_VERSION` (never from `version.json`) after boot and asserts it (the mask hides the text, not the badge width); a source arm locks pin-then-assert and every shot after `ready()`.
- **Regenerate with `--update-snapshots=all` or by deleting the PNGs;** bare `-u` rewrites only failing shots, so a change under tolerance keeps a stale baseline. Diagnose at tolerance 0.

### Cross-browser, smoke, coverage

- **`npm run test:cross` (`CROSS_BROWSER=1`) adds the engine/OS/device matrix; non-Chromium projects run only `tests/cross-browser.spec.js` and `tests/cross-device.spec.js`.** Branded `google-chrome` joins only if installed (`NO_BRANDED_CHROME=1` omits it). Run the full matrix locally before merging a new arm.
- **Device descriptors forward their `screen` deliberately (a portrait phone reports landscape);** the premise arm of `tests/screen-inert.spec.js` fails if forwarding stops (the app-code rule: see *SPA conventions*).
- **`scripts/setup-cross-browser.sh` never destroys a working shim:** it swaps in the rebuild only on success (lock: `tests/setup-cross-browser.spec.js`).
- **`npm run smoke` and `MINIFY_JS=1 npm test` (prod-bundle parity) are outside `npm test`;** a server-spawn change such as a new required env var is invisible until smoke. `npm run test:coverage` is opt-in leads; never `page.route` a navigation under CDP coverage (deadlock).

### When CI is red and local is green: READ THE LOG

- **Read the FIRST failing log in full, `[WebServer]` lines included** (see *Recurring rules*). Lowering `WORKERS` does not cure CI worker crashes.
- **Usual causes: env that exists only in a developer's shell, and the UTC container.** `autoDetectCountryTz` maps UTC to no country, so a spec relying on it pins `test.use({ timezoneId })` and asserts it took; assert timezone labels relative to `#comp-tz`, never as literals.
- **Some CI-only failures never reproduce in the pinned image** (that WebKit stall): put bounded state probes in the failing action's `catch` on a tests-only branch, whose PR CI runs (a workflow edit's does not; see *Deployment and CI image*).
- **Reproduce CI-only fixes in the pinned image:** `docker run --rm --shm-size=1g -e HOME=/root -e CI=true -v "$PWD":/w -w /w mpogr/hackarena-ci@sha256:<digest from .github/workflows/checkmarx.yml>`, copy the tree (no data dirs or `node_modules`) to `/tmp`, `ln -s /ci-deps/node_modules node_modules`, `WORKERS=4 npx playwright test`. Native control sizes differ there, so a locally measured layout proves nothing about CI.
- **The image carries `sqlite3` and `rsync` (its last layer), so CI runs `scripts/backup.sh`, `scripts/purge.sh` and `scripts/restore-mail.sh` for real; it has no `lsof` or `ss`.** A shell-script lock still keeps a SOURCE arm that runs everywhere, and its behavioural arms skip with a reason on a runner without the tools (`tests/data-dir-required.spec.js`).
- Still hidden: load `tests/_worker-crash-probe.cjs` via `NODE_OPTIONS`, only while diagnosing; it must re-raise signals and no CI job loads it.
- **No test run writes core dumps:** `tests/global-setup.js` sets the core limit to 1 before Playwright starts a worker (`KEEP_CORE_DUMPS=1` keeps them). On the CI runner a crashed process's dump, piped to the host's handler, filled the disk (12 GB in 90 s) and failed whichever test ran then; 1 is the limit the kernel itself refuses to dump at (0 only reaches a pipe's handler, and systemd's is told to ignore it).
- **Every CI test run logs the runner's disk** (`[disk]` lines: `tests/ci-disk-probe.js`, started by `tests/global-setup.js` under `CI=true`; `DISK_PROBE=1` runs it locally, `0` silences it): free space, the largest directories under the temp dir, the repository and HOME, files deleted but still held open, and /dev/shm. Read them before calling a mid-run ENOSPC infra.
