- Each `.github/workflows/checkmarx.yml` job's `name` equals its id so branch protection can key on it (see *Deployment and CI image*). `full-suite-tests` and `capture-suite` upload `test-results/` with `always()`, so output of a test that passed on retry survives a green run.

### Vacuous-assertion traps

- A retrying negative on transient UI (`not.toContainText` on a toast) passes once it fades: read one snapshot after the positive match. Pair negative locks with a positive control and assert the triggering request was made.
- **Assert by value.** Redaction locks search the whole serialised response for the secret, with an unsealed twin and the author on the same request as controls; removed copy is absent by its words while neighbours render. "Unchanged" compares with a pre-snapshot holding a live value, never falsy; bust or outwait a cache that could serve the old value (5 s `_leaderboardCache`). A client capability saves for real and reads the stored row back.
- **"Visible" means a non-empty box.** A zero-height pane reads hidden (list the open panes); an `<img>` is visible before it decodes (poll `naturalWidth`); `innerText` of a `display:none` card still returns its text: assert failure cards visible; inside a shown element `innerText` skips a `hidden` subtree and never holds a form control's value, so "none of that data is drawn" reads `textContent` and each control's `value` (`tests/certificate-pane-load-failure.spec.js`). `applyReveal` targets (`#comp-grid`, `#task-grid`) are "visible" at `opacity:0` and below the fold never fade: never poll `opacity:1`; before axe or contrast scans drain the finite animations through `tests/helpers.js` `settleFiniteAnimations` (an infinite one, such as a live event's pulse on the Events list, never finishes; scanner in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`).
- `locator.isVisible()` is point-in-time and ignores `timeout`; shell markup is visible before data loads. `"a, b"` with `.first()` picks by DOM order, not arrival (`#main-nav` precedes `#comp-grid`). `click` focuses its target: dispatch it to test focus return. `.fill()` never races a debounce (`pressSequentially` does). `page.evaluate(() => fn())` awaits the promise; use a braced body to fire and forget. Chromium autofills `autocomplete` inputs once the rest of a form is filled: assert a "blank" field is blank, cleared in the same document.
- **Init scripts** may run before `document.documentElement` exists (observe `document`, or the script dies silently) and re-run per navigation; boot races past `domcontentloaded`, so record a MutationObserver maximum, not one sample. Drain `page.on("response")` handlers that await `rs.text()` in a `finally` before returning their list (`saveAndConfirm`). A diagnostic `.catch()` never runs if the helper outwaits the test timeout.
- **Drive the real thing.** A same-id copy of a static shell element (`#smtp-banner`) is not what `getElementById` returns. App dialogs and the impersonation confirm share `#modal-overlay`: assert which is open. `page.route` a tab's own fetch; rows injected by `page.evaluate()` get re-rendered over. Open Analytics via `openAnalyticsTab` (`tests/ui-admin-helpers.js`), the help drawer only after `/api/config` (`{{platformName}}` falls back). Test XSS at the sink: render a payload, assert no `<img>`/`onerror`, no handler ran, and inert text.
- **Timing.** Bound latency by `< DEADLINE/2` with a control that the read ran (`> DEADLINE/2`). Compare two timings of in-process code in CPU time (`process.cpuUsage()`), never wall time: on a runner whose other workers keep every core busy the longer pass is paused more (lock: the `_minifyCss` linear-time arm). A poll-stop lock asserts null handles (`_stopAllPolls`), never a request count over a window shorter than the poll period. Release an external `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` only once the server is seen parked (an outstanding `/healthz` on its own connection), and assert it parked (`tests/transient-lock.js` `withTransientLock`, `expectContended`). A refused upload over `REFUSE_DRAIN_MAX_BYTES` may throw `ECONNRESET`/EPIPE (answered, then cut): count it as refused; fail on other throws.
- **Locks the environment cancels.** Timezone locks run the browser in a zone other than the event's (`_sessionGetStartUtc(row)` cancels the browser offset). Blob-URL liveness is unfetchable (CSP `connect-src` excludes `blob:`): record `URL.revokeObjectURL`. Narrow a CHECK under `db.unsafeMode(true)`, or `writable_schema` is silently ignored (`tests/any-answer-migration.spec.js`). Read computed CSS (an undefined `var()` voids the declaration); a `var()` shorthand has empty CSSOM longhands, so read the stylesheet rule. `page.clock` replaces `performance` wholesale (`getEntriesByType` returns `[]`); to move `Date.now` across a reload, offset it from sessionStorage in an init script (`tests/boot-reload.spec.js`).
- **Never count a fixed number of Tabs to leave a panel:** a native date or time input holds a Tab stop per segment (in the pinned CI image, one more for its picker); Tab until focus leaves, within a bound (lock: `tests/new-event-page-ui.spec.js`).
- `GET /api/admin/leaderboard` and `GET /api/my-competitions` return bare arrays. Stubbing `window.showToast` intercepts nothing (modules import it). A submit without `gradingHashFor()` gets 409 `task_changed` before grading or counting. Never re-implement production SQL; drive the real path through its test hook.

### Competition-fixture checklist — READ BEFORE BUILDING ONE

Check ALL of these before a test builds an event for participants to act in. The numbering is fixed (tests cite items by number); add new items at the end.

1. **Will the all-solved auto-end fire?** Where it applies (`lib/event-policy.js` `autoEndOnAllSolvedApplies`; never with self-enrolment open or on a Booth Event), ONE participant finishing every challenge (solved or out of attempts; where the event asks quick questions, answering them all too) ENDS the event: Unsolved is no longer forced, `end-now` returns 400, scores and the team board freeze. To keep it live, enrol a non-solver or leave a challenge unsolved (two slots need more than one challenge per cohort). To end it this way, assert `ended_at`.
2. **Own your competition and challenges.** Other specs attach challenges to the seeded events, which may have ended; wait on a lifecycle-gated affordance via `tests/ui-admin-helpers.js` `focusOwnLiveComp`.
3. **Own your participants, with complete profiles.** Earlier tests block, lock, re-password and tag the seeded ones; entering any event writes a shared user's `active_competition_id`. An account missing a required profile field meets the undismissable completion gate, blocking clicks on "visible" elements.
4. **The staff tag and the event_admin role remove a user from the board unless the event is `staff_compete` and they hold a row in it; the benchmark tag always does** (admin boards list benchmark rows apart, unranked). A board assertion then passes vacuously.
5. **`login_time` is the solve-clock anchor.** Until a competition-scoped request stamps it during the live event, the participant is off the board unless they submit or take a hint.
6. **Submit-path events are written post-commit** (`setImmediate` drain of `pendingEvents`; `select-timeslot` too): poll for them.
7. **The assign endpoint silently skips ineligible users** and still answers 200 (each class counted in a `skipped*` field). Benchmark users are NOT skipped. Own the participant and assert `assigned === N`.
8. **An ended competition refuses assign (400)** once manually ended or past its scheduled end: create it live, assign, then end it. An all-solved auto-ended event still inside its schedule is REVIVED by the assign. Exception: a Booth Event in its open late-entry window admits a LATE ENTRANT, off the final board (see *Competition lifecycle*); to fake one, re-time every timestamp, `competition_groups.assigned_at` included, with `tests/late-entry-fixture.js`.
9. **The scheduler skips a first sighting** (`lib/scheduler.js` records the status and `continue`s): an entity created already ended never emits `auto_ended`. Create it live, run `scheduler-tick`, then expire it.
10. **How many events does your participant have?** With one, the sole-event landing skips the picker; picker and negative auto-resume tests need two surviving events. `users.left_event_at` suppresses the landing; a set `active_competition_id` resumes whatever the status (after the end, behind the click-intercepting "Competition Ended" modal: `POST /api/dismiss-ended` first). Clicking Events fires `deselect-competition` (stamps `left_event_at`, NULLs the marker), so select your event explicitly (UI specs: `enterCompAndNav`, copied into each `tests/ui-participant-{a,b,c}.spec.js`), never `.first()` on the picker grid; an empty `#tasks-subtitle` means you are on the picker. `end-now` refuses an upcoming event (400) and only stamps one past its schedule, so create an ended Program in the past.
11. **Does the acting admin compete?** A user-backed admin enrolled in a `staff_compete` event gets `403 competitor_cannot_edit` on every write to it, its sessions, groups and challenges except removing itself, so edit through a second admin. The built-in superadmin has no row and never competes.
12. **Does the rule depend on sessions?** Per-slot leaderboard scoping, the per-slot auto-end scan and `/api/status`'s own-session window look correct when broken with a single slot. Populate 2+ slots.
13. **Can the admin path build the account state you need?** `…/approve` refuses an unverified account. An enrolled-but-unverified participant needs a join-link registration (`registerPost` with its `joinToken`: approved, `verified=0`, enrolled), then a sign-in with a DESK code (`…/login-code/reveal`, which `registerPost`'s never-signed-in state still allows), which stamps the 48 h grace (an emailed code would verify it). A plain `registerPost` account (its default fixture rewind) is unverified AND pending; a real sign-up is neither.
14. **Does the rule depend on scope?** One event cannot catch unscoped impersonation or event-admin code: enrol the participant in an in-scope AND an out-of-scope event, add a global-admin control, and assert their own session and marker are untouched. In a refusal matrix each row reaching a handler owns its victim; refused rows share one granted nowhere (lock: `tests/event-admin-matrix.spec.js`).
15. **Would a missing filter show?** An export or cross-product lock needs several participants × challenges with independent signals, a DECOY competition with its own activity (read its output too) and an assigned-then-hidden challenge (lock: `tests/competition-activity-export.spec.js`).

#### Test flakiness — root-cause conventions

Find a flake's root cause; never widen a timeout or add `waitForTimeout`. Failing that, assert preconditions and print the actual state on failure.

- **Poll every post-commit write.** Submit-path `challenge_events`: `expect.poll` on a fresh read-only better-sqlite3 connection. `app.jsonl` audit mirror and `GET /api/admin/logs`: `tests/helpers.js` `findLogLine` / `pollAdminLogs`. The access log has its own writer (`res.on("finish")`); poll for each line.
- **Filter paginated admin tables first**: `filterComps` / `viewComp` (which clear the status chips), `findSeededTaskRow`, or the box's narrowing helper (`narrowEventsTable`, `narrowChallengesTable`, `narrowPeopleTable`, `narrowGroupsTable`) in `tests/ui-admin-helpers.js` (lock: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`). A search is a substring match and can leave several rows: click inside the named row (`.first()` of its two `comp-open` targets, the name cell and Open), never `.first()` on the table.
- **Press a row only after the render that typing (or a load) causes.** A press that goes down on a row and comes up on its replacement gets no click from Chromium, while Playwright reports the click done, and a row visible before the render proves nothing (the unfiltered first page often holds it). The Groups, Challenges and People tables render by replacing every row (search debounce 160 ms, a load), so their witness is `rerenderedAfter(page, tbody, act)` (marks the rows, waits until none is left); the Events table keeps every row unchanged at its place (*Lists, counts and load failures*), so its witness is a render count past the typing's start with the typed text recorded (`S._compRenders`, `S._compRenderedQuery`). The narrowing helpers are the one way specs type into those four boxes (scanner in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`; behaviour in `tests/admin-row-press.spec.js` and `tests/phase3-ui.spec.js`). Each proves a render ran, so nothing else may be pending: it first waits out the tab's load (`S._loadingCount` at 0 for Events and People, no skeleton row for Challenges, the first rows for Groups), and a later reload is the caller's to wait out: `rerenderedAfter` on Groups and People (as `tests/ui-events-groups-journeys.spec.js` does), no skeleton row left on Challenges (a reload's skeleton rows already replace the marked ones), the render count on Events.
- **`GET /api/admin/participants` ignores `?search=`.** Only `/picker` reads it, and page 1 holds the oldest users. Use the page-size cap and filter client-side.
- **Settle signals fire too early.** `#login-page` paints without the app: before acting on it (Continue, the form, following the OS theme) call `expectSpaBooted` (`tests/ui-admin-helpers.js`; it waits for `<html data-booted="1">`, set last in `boot()`, after every handler and the OS-theme listener), or a load whose app never booted reads as "Continue did nothing". **A test painting `<html>`'s look in place (`data-look`, `data-theme`, `data-ambient`) first awaits `tests/helpers.js` `appearanceSettled`** (`S._appearanceSettled`): boot's look read (`features/appearance.js` `loadOrgDefaultLook`) is never awaited and repaints the look when it answers, which can be after `data-booted` and after the test's paint (scanner: functional-core-b Docs hygiene; lock: `tests/platform-look.spec.js`). `#main-nav` is static markup: wait for `#main-nav .nav-btn` or the view content, and for the admin model (`S.adminParticipantsData`, `S.adminCompetitionsData`, `S.adminTasksData`; poll it: `clickTab`'s row wait is met by the Challenges and Logs skeleton rows before their read answers), then for `#modal-overlay` to go. The participant Challenges/Leaderboard nav buttons are ALWAYS visible and `aria-disabled="true"` until an event is entered (or, for Leaderboard, on an unranked kind): "entered" is `#nav-challenges:not([aria-disabled=true])` visible, "no board" is `#nav-leaderboard:not([aria-disabled=true])` hidden. Signed in, header controls other than Search and the live chips live in the account menu (`tests/chrome-helpers.js` `openAccountMenu`, `pressAccountMenuItem`). Read a count label and its items in ONE `page.evaluate`, polled until consistent.
- **`boundingBox()` is null before layout.** Assert `toBeVisible()`, then `expect.poll` the height; on fading overlays drain their animations first (`settleFiniteAnimations(page, sel)`).
- **Playwright's "stable" counts frames, not time,** and a cold WebKit in the CI image can paint seconds apart under load: the cross specs' `assertReachable` calls `settleBox` (`tests/helpers.js`) before its trial click. On the CI runner WebKit can also stop painting for 20 s or more while the page is fine (unreproduced here; nothing found restarts it), hanging every frame-based check: every cross-spec click, tap, check, hover and trial click goes through `pressThroughRenderStall` (lock: a Docs-hygiene scanner over both cross specs), `assertReachable`'s settle through `settleThroughRenderStall`, every navigation through `gotoThroughRenderStall` (`tests/helpers.js`; one not committed in a third of its budget, 10 s at most, is sent once more, as a fresh page's first navigation can sit uncommitted for 30 s there (every page the cross specs open is a GET); a second send Chromium cancels itself (`net::ERR_ABORTED`, before it reaches the network) counts as not committed and the budget is waited out; in a stall the test can miss `load` and even `domcontentloaded`, so after 4 s it asks the page's own Navigation Timing record, never `readyState`, which reads `interactive` before deferred and module scripts run; lock: `tests/render-stall-press.spec.js`). Only when the action (before dispatching input) or settle timed out and no frame painted in 1 s more do they check readiness in-page on timers (each try scrolling with the next of Playwright's own retry alignments: `nearest` alone leaves a control the page was scrolled past under the sticky header) and send the input with `force` (a settle skips the animation wait), printing `[render-stall]` in the CI log. An input sent and never confirmed (a timeout after `performing <action> action`; Playwright sends a click's move, down and up together) is never sent again: a witness armed in the page before each press (sent only once it is armed, waiting up to half its budget for a busy page) waits the rest of the budget for it to reach the control, else the press fails by name; a trial (its input is real, blocked by Playwright's hit-target interceptor) is answered by the in-page checks (lock: `tests/render-stall-press.spec.js`). The page then remembers the stall for 60 s, so the next action, while nothing paints, goes straight to the in-page checks. Never add a frame-gap pre-check: gaps of several seconds are ordinary there and it failed working tests. Keep each cross-spec test short: there every reachability check and press costs seconds (about three times what the pinned image measures on a developer machine), and one test making fourteen of them ran out of its 60 s; split a journey rather than raise its timeout. A drawer helper waits for `#admin-page` visible (`#admin-sidebar` is static markup) and asserts `.mobile-open`.
- **Submit and cache timing.** Lose a submit response with `route.fetch()` (graded), then `route.abort()`; assert it was graded and the retry fell in the 3 s fold (`server.js` `dedupCutoff`). Stop the pulse (`S.statusPollInterval`, a timeout: it paints the score pill) before asserting the pill. Take a stale-cache warm read after the last invalidation (`select-competition` invalidates) and before the deciding submit.
- **Pick a witness that sees the event.** LinkedIn share: stub `window.open` (as `tests/credential-reach.spec.js` does) and watch the share POST; the `noopener` path in `public/js/features/badge-share.js` fires no popup. Poller restart: compare interval IDs; the `visibilitychange` hide handler (`public/js/core/main.js`) never nulls `S.lbInterval`. Large avatar: `tests/helpers.js` `makePng` / `pngDataUri`; filler after the `=` padding of a data URI is not decoded.
- **An out-of-band sign-in via `page.request`** (e.g. `codeLogin`) leaves the booted SPA a stale CSRF token, so its next write 403s `csrf_invalid`: reload. On timeout, dump the visible error element (`#login-error`; on the sign-up form `#reg-error`).
- **`tests/ui-admin-helpers.js` `login`/`submitLogin` sign an address in by code** (pressing "Email me a code instead" on a password step) and answer "Set your password" with "Keep using emailed codes", which STORES `login_method='code'` on that admin for the rest of the file; a spec about the password step drives the card itself.
- **axe `color-contrast` oscillates on scrollable lists.** Disable only that rule, only for such component scans.
- **Seeded participant contract.** The `tests/isolated-test.js` `afterAll` guard fails the file if `settings.platform_name` leaves its default or alice/bob/carol lose their seed shape (role, flags, lock, `secondary_email`, tags, memberships). Restore a changed seeded user in a `finally` to the HARD-CODED seed value and assert it, logging in again first.
- **Set toggles to the state you want; never flip blindly.** `…/participants/:id/block`, task `…/lock` and kebab menus toggle (`ensureTaskLocked`, `openKebab`).
- **Assert the no-op flags.** Participant removal answers `{ok:true, removed:false}`; `select-timeslot` for a live slot without `confirmLive` answers `{ok:false, requireConfirmation:true}` (staff are never asked). `r.ok()` proves nothing.
- **The built-in superadmin has no `userId`,** so a lock on a user-session route needs a user-backed admin.
- **Clear process-global state in `afterEach`.** `clear-ip-ban-alerts` after raising one; `scanner-toggle` off plus `scanner-reset`, or a 127.0.0.1 block 429s the rest of the file. Tear a block down through an admin session made BEFORE it: the block exempts a signed-in admin, not the login.
- **Races: park, then act.** Use a delay hook and poll its `parked` counter; land the competing write after the last await, never after a sleep. Accept every correct loser: two invitation claims for one address 409 at `emailIsTaken` or at the `used_at IS NULL` claim (`routes/auth.js`); require exactly one 200.
- **An upload meets the 1 GiB free-disk floor before any quota** (`lib/attachments.js`): on a runner whose disk dips under it every upload answers "Server storage is too low…", so a test asserting another upload refusal waits for the room first (`tests/helpers.js` `awaitUploadRoom`).
- **Mail:** match recipients exactly, decode RFC 2047 subjects and quoted-printable bodies, assert exactly one code in the body. Poll for the reason you drove, not the first message (a sign-up's Continue already mailed an `email_proof` code under the same subject).
- **Diagnostics and keys.** `expect.poll`'s `message` is evaluated when the call is built; collect diagnostics in `.catch()`. A test of which row a global scan matched needs a unique, clock-seeded key per run.

#### Test-only endpoints

Registered only under `NODE_ENV === "test"`, absent from Swagger. Every `/api/test/` path gets the 16 KB body cap on both parsers, matched by prefix (`server.js` `_isTestPath`; lock: `tests/functional-security-b.api.spec.js`), so a new hook needs only a row here. Delay hooks clamp `delayMs` to 0-5000. The CxOne withdrawal runner has no hook; use `tests/cxone-provision-harness.cjs`.

| Endpoint (`/api/test/…`) | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `POST rate-limit-toggle` · `rate-limit-sweep` · `rate-limit-age` | Force limiters on; one janitor pass; rewind bucket starts by `{ms}` |
| `GET last-login-code?email=` | The last code mailed to an address (sign-in code, or address proof: `via:"proof"`), plaintext, in memory, test processes only (`lib/login-code.js` `_testLastLoginCode`); how specs sign in and sign up through the real doors (`tests/helpers.js` `codeLogin`/`registerPost`, `tests/ui-admin-helpers.js` `login`/`submitLogin`/`signUpViaUi`) |
| `POST registration-legacy-state` | Rewind a just-registered account to the FIXTURE state `registerPost` defaults to (unverified with a live verify_code, never signed in, pending unless join/invitation). No sign-up creates it, and an invited or join-link account never looked like it; what a sign-up creates is pinned in `tests/signup-real-state.api.spec.js`. `{deferJoin:true}` (`registerPost`'s `deferJoin`) also re-defers a join-link registration's enrolment to the first sign-in (marker set, row removed) |
| `POST reset-recent-code-sends` | Forget the 120 s re-send suppression, to request a second code on purpose |
| `POST reset-origin-code-budget` | Clear the per-origin sign-in-code budget |
| `POST scheduler-tick` | One status-scheduler pass |
| `POST legacy-shared-assign` · `event-copies-migrate` | Assign doors put the bank question itself in (`{on}`, the previous model; always switch back off); run the event-owned-questions batch now (`{dryRun}`), busting what it moved |
| `POST certificate-dispatch-tick` | One certificate-dispatch pass; `{force:true}` treats every job and retry as due (skipping the settle delay and back-off). A pass serves every event's jobs (20 a pass, oldest first), so its counts include events the file ended earlier: read your event's own rows, or pass `{competitionId}` to run only that event's jobs and retries (the shared relay fails every send, so a forced pass also re-renders and re-sends every certificate the file's other events queued); `{maxSends}` lowers that pass's per-job send cap (never above `MAX_SENDS_PER_PASS`), so the cap is provable with a few certificates rather than a hundred renders |
| `POST team-report-tick` | One team-report pass (`lib/team-reports.js`); `{force:true}` treats every job as due, the 48 h nudge included, so a spec that must keep the nudge waiting back-dates the one job instead |
| `POST reminder-tick` | One dispatcher tick (`{delayMs, forceDelivered}`); `ran:false` means already running, or `held:true` the instance is out of service; `started` counts rows settled unsent (start passed) |
| `POST lockout-expiry-tick` | One lockout-expiry pass (`unlocked`); `held:true` while the instance is out of service |
| `POST sweep-attachments` | All three file sweeps; `{stagingRecencyMs}` shrinks the staging window |
| `POST sweep-sessions` · `reap-accounts` · `purge-events` | One expired-session, unverified-account or event-retention pass (`purge-events` also purges soft-deleted templates, `{templateTtlMs}` shrinking their 30-day window, share links 30 days past their expiry and unreferenced images past a 7-day grace; `{limit}` caps each table and the share links, `{imageGraceMs}` shrinks the image grace, `{imageLimit}` caps the images, `{imageRefsWarnMs}` lowers the slow-collection warning; the images hold while the instance is out of service, `images_held`) |
| `POST presence-seed` | Bank presence buckets (the only way to create a presence difference) |
| `POST\|GET submit-read-delay` | Park the submit file read; GET reports `parked` |
| `POST select-timeslot-delay` | Park the route in its race window |
| `POST admin-email-delay` | Park the admin email change between commit and SMTP send |
| `POST\|GET admin-commit-delay` | Park `testAdminCommitPark()` routes before their commit-time re-check; GET reports `parked` and `enteredAt` (the first entry since arming, which `parked()` bounds its act from: never from the send, whose pre-park work a slow runner stretches); `{release:true}` lets every parked request go at once (a cache arm warms, changes, then releases inside the 2 s window) |
| `GET import-prepass` | The last challenge import's rich-text pass as the server measured it (`server.js` `_importPassWitness`), in main-thread CPU time, never wall time: the pass's and the transaction's CPU, the longest the loop went without a turn while the pass ran, the responses finished meanwhile, and whether the commit park had been entered when it ended (`tests/import-rich-text-prepass.api.spec.js`) |
| `POST slow-session-write` | Hold a request after mutating its session |
| `POST\|GET certificate-send-delay` | Park every certificate send (`lib/certificate-dispatch.js` `_sendFor`) after its PDF render and before its re-checks; its own state, apart from `admin-commit-delay`. `{delayMs}` arms it (0-5000, zeroing `parked`), `{release:true}` lets every parked send go; GET reports `delayMs`, `parked`, `enteredAt` |
| `GET stall-body` | Send headers and one byte, never finish the body |
| `POST shutdown` | Run the SIGTERM drain (`gracefulShutdown`, which no signal reaches under test) and exit 0; only on `{confirm:"shutdown"}`, answered once the workers are stopped. Spawned servers only (`tests/team-report-delivery.spec.js`, `tests/certificate-email-ledger.spec.js`) |
| `GET leaderboard-builds?competitionId=` · `POST\|GET compression-count` | How often a board was computed (`leaderboardComputeCount`; a cache hit not counted); POST wraps zlib's compressors once per process and zeroes the counts, GET reads them (`tests/storm-witnesses.api.spec.js`) |
| `POST throw` · `block-loop` | Error handler's 500 branch (`sync`/`async`; `after-send`/`after-send-large`/`after-write`/`after-write-bad-end` respond first: `-large` sends 16 KB, past the 8 KB compression threshold, so a gzip body is still flushing; `-bad-end` makes `res.end` throw); a real event-loop stall |
| `POST raise-ip-ban-alert` · `clear-ip-ban-alerts` | Raise or clear alerts in the server's own store |
| `POST scanner-toggle` · `scanner-reset` · `scanner-age-window` · `GET scanner-state` | Scanner tracker on; clear; rewind its window; inspect one IP |

## Rules by area

### The three event kinds — the policy table

`lib/event-policy.js` `POLICY` owns everything derived from `event_kind`; its values and the wire lock beat any comment.

| key | answers | `booth` | `tournament` | `program` | wire |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sessions` | timeslots? | `forbidden` | `many` | `forbidden` | `sessions` |
| `schedule` | duration or deadline | `window` | `window` | `deadline` | `schedule` |
| `ranked` | score/rank/placing? | `true` | `true` | `false` | `ranking_enabled` |
| `certificate` | what it attests | `attempt` | `attempt` | `completion` | `certificate` |
| `trustedNetworks` | rate-limit bypass? | `eligible` | `if-single-session` | `never` | `trusted_networks` |
| `revealAnswers` | publish key at end? | `allowed` | `allowed` | `forbidden` | `reveal_answers` |
| `groupLeaderboard` | team board? | `allowed` | `allowed` | `forbidden` | `group_leaderboard` |
| `measurePresence` | net active time? | `forbidden` | `allowed` | `allowed` | `measure_presence` |
| `lateEntry` | admit after end? | `allowed` | `never` | `never` | `late_entry` |
| `choiceLayout` | phone choice UI (presentation) | `tap` | `standard` | `standard` | `choice_layout` |
| `clockStart` / `clockStartDefault` | solve clock may start at the first open? / create seed | `allowed` / `first_open` | `allowed` / `first_open` | `never` / `entry` | `clock_start`, `defaults.clock_start` |
| `cxoneProvisioning` | may it create Checkmarx One accounts? | `forbidden` | `allowed` | `allowed` | `cxone_provisioning` (never `cxone_accounts`: `leakyNamePattern` matches "count") |
| `startReminder` | "starts soon" mail? | `allowed` | `allowed` | `forbidden` | — |
| `autoEndOnAllSolved` | may all-finished fire? | `never` | `allowed` | `allowed` | — |
| `autoResume` | resume at sign-in? | `true` | `true` | `true` | — |
| `refuses*` | `file` answers / `needs:` tags unfit? | `true` | `false` | `false` | — |
| `*Default`: autoEnd / cxoneDisabled / selfEnroll | create seeds | `0/1/1` | `1/0/0` | `0/1/0` | all but autoEnd, as `defaults.cxone_disabled`, `defaults.self_enroll_enabled` |
| `qualifyingQuestionsDefault` | seed "Add the qualifying questions"? | `1` | `0` | `0` | `defaults.add_qualifying` |
| sign-up form | per event: `registrationFieldStates` | configurable | configurable | configurable | `registration_fields`, `registration_fields_configurable` |

#### Asking the policy

- **Ask `eventPolicy(row).<key>`; never compare `event_kind` to a literal** in `lib/`, `routes/` or `public/js`: a rule re-derived per consumer drifts. New behaviour gets a new key, shipped as a wire key for `public/js`. With no row pass the kind string (`eventPolicy(kind)`); for the sign-up matrix, `registrationFieldDefaultsForKind(kind)`. Partial locks: `tests/event-kind-form-scope.spec.js` (only `features/competitions-admin.js` and `features/templates-admin.js`), `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js` (only sign-up and late-entry questions in `lib/`/`routes/`).
- **Tolerant on an unknown value, strict on a missing key.** `eventKind` maps an unknown value (a rollback) to `DEFAULT_KIND` `tournament` but throws on a row with no `event_kind`, so a partial SELECT cannot silently pick a kind.
- **A `forbidden` key is refused on create AND PUT, against the stored kind** (`reveal_answers_forbidden`, `group_leaderboard_forbidden`, `measure_presence_forbidden`, `time_basis_forbidden`, `cxone_provisioning_forbidden` for an explicit `cxone_disabled:false`): a create-only check lets an edit reach a refused state, and nothing downstream re-checks. Presence / `time_basis` are refused only when changed from the stored value: the form echoes every control on every save, so refusing an echo makes a Booth Event unsaveable.
- **A prohibition is not a seed.** `autoEndOnAllSolved` and `lateEntry` are checked at the gate (`autoEndOnAllSolvedApplies`, `lateEntryAllowed`) whatever the column holds (see *Competition lifecycle*). Program `autoEndOnAllSolved: "allowed"` and `autoResume: true` for all kinds are operator rulings.
- **Program `revealAnswers` is forbidden** (reveal, move the deadline, and everyone earns a certificate from the key). **Program `trustedNetworks` is `never`** (a sessionless event passes the single-session test and keeps the bypass for years).

#### Labels, values and the client

- **Stored values are opaque; labels ride the wire.** A rename is copy, not a migration; nothing maps a label back to a value (lock: `tests/event-kind-naming.spec.js`).
- **The vocabulary rides the SPA shell** (`<body data-event-kinds data-event-collective>`, from `server.js`), not `/api/config`, so a throttled `/api/config` cannot block event creation.
- **No fallback kind label: a wrong chip is worse than none.** `ui/format.js` `eventKindLabel` returns null, the chip renders nothing, the New event page offers no blank starter, the create form and the kind filter refuse to open. Only `eventCollectiveNoun` falls back (to `event`).
- **One place to create, one form, no pre-selected kind.** Every create starts on the New event page (`admin-templates-tab`, `features/templates-admin.js`): ONE card per kind (no whole-card action), offering the kind's recommended setup (its built-in template, matched by `event_kind`, `tpl-pick`; none while missing) and Start blank (`new-event-start`; wire `isDefault` only adds "Most common"), then "Your templates", which lists only non-built-in templates. All open `openCompetitionForm(null,{kind[,template]})`, where the kind is a field shown as what was chosen (owner's decision, 27 Nov 2026: the kind card already asked): a kind chip and **Change type** (`comp-kind-change`, `compKindChangeToggle`), which opens the `comp-kind-seg` radios under it (hidden until pressed; withheld, with the note's reason, where the type is locked; collectors read `#comp-kind`): changing it on a create re-renders the form for the new kind (`compKindSelect`, carrying name, start and template; it confirms first if any other control changed since open, `_compFormKindSnap`), on an edit it confirms through `compKindPick`, naming what the new type turns off (late entries included), and keeps the event's OWN sign-up matrix (the form sends all five fields), never the target kind's defaults. `openCompetitionForm()` with no kind opens the page. Rarely used settings sit in `<details id="comp-advanced">` (collapsed on a create AND an edit, owner's decision, 27 Nov 2026; specs open it with `openCompAdvanced`, which `openCompCreateForm` calls unless `advanced:false`); its summary states `Checkmarx One accounts: on|off` (`#comp-advanced-cxone`, following the box) where the kind may create them (`cxone_provisioning` `allowed`; a Booth Event's form has no Checkmarx One section at all), as does the Create confirmation; the **Dry run** box (`#comp-internal`) is the `test/dry run` tag itself (ticking adds it on save, removing the tag anywhere unticks it; lock: `tests/dry-run-tag-box.spec.js`), and New template opens on `Choose a type…`, refusing Create until one is picked (operator rulings, 14 Nov 2026). A window kind's event fields have no end inputs, only the read-only `#comp-end-preview`; a Competition's session rows (`addCompFormSession`) take a duration or an end (`.ts-end-date`/`.ts-end-time`).
- **The create form picks no questions** (owner ruling, 27 Nov 2026: one door into an event). A blank event gets them from its Questions section's **+ Add from bank**; a template's come with its spawn. No `#comp-ch` section and no "Create and pick challenges" (locks: `tests/create-form-challenges.spec.js`, the stage B arms of `tests/ui-admin-tasks.spec.js`).
- **A create from a template goes through the template's door**: Save spawns it (`POST …/event-templates/:id/spawn`: challenges, tags, join-link settings, provenance; `add_qualifying` rides the spawn, never the PUT), then applies the form to the new event as a PUT; never a plain create, which would drop the challenges. A PUT refused after the spawn keeps the form on the create that made the event (`_markSpawnedForm`, **Finish creating**): the next press sends the settings to that event, then the Checkmarx One override, never a second spawn. A save whose ANSWER was lost may have landed and moved the token: the next press re-reads the event and takes its token only when it moved and the row holds every value the lost save sent (`_compRowHolds`), else the server's 409 stands, so a change someone else made meanwhile is never overwritten (lock: `tests/event-form-paths.spec.js`). An event admin (who creates only by spawn or clone) gets the recommended setups without Start blank, and the quick-start panel instead of the form (lock: `tests/new-event-page-ui.spec.js`).
- **Kind copy is `{article} {label}`** (`eventKindPhrase` / `eventKindPhraseClient`); `article` is a table field, never a first-letter rule.

#### What reaches the client

- **`eventPolicyForWire(row)` is the one projection**: computed, never stored or accepted from a request, on every event payload; `eventKindsForWire()` carries it per kind. Key set pinned (lock: `tests/event-policy-wire.spec.js`).
- **A key ships with the control it governs, not before.** Withheld: `startReminder`, `autoEndOnAllSolved`, `autoResume`, `refuses*` (server-side validation) and the seeds (an event's values are its own columns; a seed with a form control rides the kind's entry as `defaults`).
- **`ranked` ships as `ranking_enabled`** to dodge `leakyNamePattern` (the no-total-count lock in `tests/functional-security-c.api.spec.js`; see *Scoring*); new keys must not match it, and the scanner gets no exceptions.
- **Every kind is configurable (operator ruling, 30 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026), so the projection throws for any row that lacks `registration_field_policy`**: SELECT it wherever an event row reaches `eventPolicyForWire`/`registrationFieldStates`. (`linkedin_signup_enabled`, a legacy column nothing reads, is refused when sent: `400 linkedin_signup_removed`. Likewise `self_enroll_allow_instant`, retired by operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026: create, PUT and template settings refuse it `400 self_enroll_instant_removed`, and a stored template key is dropped on read.) Admin payloads drop the raw `registration_field_policy`.
- **Create seeds come from the kind's `defaults`** (`cxone_disabled`, `add_qualifying`, `late_entry_enabled` (`lateEntryDefaultForKind`: on where `lateEntry` is `allowed`), and for the template editor `self_enroll_enabled`), never a client constant, keeping the route's `=== undefined ? kindDefault` branch reachable. The template editor (`templates-admin.js` `SEEDED_KEYS`) shows a seeded setting the template omits as the kind's seed and leaves it out of the save until changed, so `_bpResolve` still decides it.
- **`policyOrDefault` merges field by field** (inside `registration_fields` too) against a permissive default, so a stale payload keeps old behaviour; `registration_fields_configurable`, `late_entry` and `choice_layout` default restrictive. Never gate the form's sign-up and late-entry sections on the merged value (they vanish when server and SPA are out of step): read the raw key (`_rawConfigurable`), else the shell's kind entry, else hide.

#### Changing an event's kind

- **`eventKindChangeLock(comp, slots)` is the one predicate** (wire `event_kind_locked`): `started` unless upcoming, `has_sessions` while any slot exists. The same kind is not a change. Never delete sessions to allow one (operator ruling).
- **The form sends `event_kind` on every save**, edit included, or a kind change is unreachable; after a change `_compFormPolicy` follows the form's kind, not the row. `saveCompetition` must send every field `_collectCompFormData` collects, or it silently does not save.
- **Two session rules are guarded at both ends** (one guard alone is bypassed by reordering): sessionless ⇒ zero sessions (`routes/sessions.js` `sessions_forbidden`; the lock refuses the kind change), and trusted IPs (create/PUT refuse `trusted_networks_forbidden` and, with sessions, `trusted_networks_multi_session`, PUT counting the prospective `_slotsPut`; `POST .../timeslots` refuses while `trusted_network_ips` is set).
- **The PUT forces off any setting the new kind forbids that the admin left unsent** (reveal, group board, presence and `time_basis`, trusted IPs, late entry), recorded in `trusted_networks_changed` (trusted IPs) or the `edited` audit. `auto_end_on_all_solved` resets to the new seed (no form control; a Program would end on its first finisher). `cxone_disabled`, `self_enroll_*` and `leaderboard_mode` stay: seeds never overwrite an admin's choice.
- **The schedule is converted, not carried over** (`deadline_required` / `duration_required`). The PUT also re-runs `challengeFitError` (`event_kind_challenge_unfit`), purges pending start reminders the new kind forbids, and invalidates members' `/api/me` caches.

#### "Event" is the GENERIC noun; "Competition" is one KIND

- **GENERIC → Event; KIND → keep; IDENTIFIER → never.** Read copy (SPA, mail, guides, swagger, alt text) calls the container an Event; the tournament kind stays Competition. Stored values (`scope=competition`), schema, routes, API fields, audit types, identifiers, DOM ids, `data-*` values and screenshot filenames never change.
- **No kind label may equal `EVENT_COLLECTIVE`**, hence "Booth Event". Rename traps: "event event" (write "audit event") and "a event".
- **Challenge-interaction signals read "Activity"** (Submissions column, analytics chart, CSV and on-screen "Total Activity", the competition-activity export), keeping "Event" collective; a CSV header and its on-screen header change together.
- **Tests select tabs by `data-tab-id`**, never by label (`TAB_SLUG` / `clickTab` in `tests/ui-admin-helpers.js`; specs inlining it define a local `_tabSel`).

### Authentication & authorization

AUTH_PATHS.md walks every sign-up, sign-in, verification and recovery path; read it before changing one. See also *Roles, scope and impersonation* (admin re-checks), *Security hardening highlights* (rate limits), *Required user profile fields*, *SPA conventions* (client sign-out classifier).

#### Sign-in methods and doors
- **Every account row signs in with a password or an emailed code: `users.login_method`, owned by `lib/login-method.js` (operator ruling, 11 Nov 2026).** NULL follows the ROLE: class `all` (built-in `admin` and `superadmin`, every unscoped custom role) gets `password`, every other role `code`. A stored value is the person's own choice; no role change touches it (a promotion moves only a NULL default). Ask `effectiveLoginMethod(row)`/`loginMethodWire(row)`, which throw on a row without `login_method` and `role` (`loginMethodWire`, like `hasUsablePassword`, also on one without `password_set_at` and `password_hash`). LinkedIn sign-in and sign-up are gone (operator order, 28 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026).
- **A password is usable only when `hasUsablePassword(row)`: `password_set_at` stamped AND a bcrypt hash stored.** Creates store `NO_PASSWORD_HASH` (`lib/util.js`; the participant import too, `password_set_at` NULL), so none signs in; an older account whose real password and `password_set_at` survived signs in with it again (operator ruling, 11 Nov 2026). Only `PUT /api/me/sign-in-method` sets a usable one. `password_hash` and `password_set_at` never reach the wire (`GET /api/me` spreads its row: destructure them off).
- **A password never closes the code door:** every account row can ask for an emailed code ("Email me a code instead"), so a forgotten password is a code sign-in and a new password. The built-in superadmin is the only account without a code door.
- **`POST /api/auth/sign-in-method` picks the card's next step, 200 for every input.** Any non-address (no `@`) gets the same `{method:"password", code_offered:false}` with no lookup, so the break-glass username is never confirmed; an address whose PRIMARY holder is code-eligible and uses a password gets `{method:"password", code_offered:true, password_set}`; anything else `{method:"code"}`.
- **`POST /api/auth/password` answers 200 for every outcome; the client branches on `ok`** (a venue NAT's wrong passwords must not feed scanner reputation). It accepts only a `password`-method account that `hasUsablePassword`, so a code account's old password signs nobody in (`login_failed{reason:"password_not_in_use"}`). One bcrypt per outcome (`dummyPasswordHash`), so timing does not separate "no account" from "wrong password". `pending_approval` and `verify_by_code` are disclosed only after a correct password. The row is re-read after the bcrypt await, and `opts.stillValid` re-asks for the same hash after `regenerate()`, so a password changed meanwhile mints no session. It never verifies, approves, enrols through a code-bound event or claims invitations (it does consume a pending join marker, as the code redeem does): only an emailed code proves the inbox.
- **`POST /api/login` is the built-in superadmin's door alone** (see *Built-in superadmin (the one break-glass account)*). **Any other name or address gets one generic `401 {code:"use_sign_in_code"}` with no lookup** (no SELECT, no bcrypt, no lockout charge), so the answer cannot depend on whether an address exists. Never add a lookup there.
- **Every account sign-in ends in `_signInAs` (`routes/auth.js`): the code redeem, the password door and the address proof's sign-up.** First the code redeem and the password door clear the lockout, every caller calls `stampLoginTimes` (must succeed, else the sign-in fails: it is the desk reveal's only record of a first sign-in), and every caller but the invitation sign-up consumes the join marker, passing `req` so the join is recorded as the person's own with the request's context; it closes the open impersonation window (`impersonation_ended{reason:"login"}`), `regenerate()`s, asks `opts.stillValid`, ends other sessions with `kickerContext`, records `login_success{method}` (`login_code`, `password`, `email_proof`), stamps `req.session.signInMethod`/`signedInAt`, applies auto-resume after the regenerate and sets the tier flags. `finishSignIn` (`public/js/features/auth.js`) routes on its login-shaped answer (`role`, `userRole`, `id`, `competitions`, `autoResumeCompetitionId`, `missing_fields`, `verified`, `verification_grace_until` and `loginMethodWire`'s four fields). The `tests/event-auto-resume.spec.js` scanner holds only the resume (it sees the one emit, in `_signInAs`); each caller's stamp is held by review.
- **`PUT /api/me/sign-in-method` (Profile → How you sign in) is the one credential writer**, refused under impersonation for every tier. `code` keeps a stored password, which the password door then refuses; `password` with none set needs the new one; CHANGING one needs `currentPassword`, or an EMAILED-code sign-in by this session under 10 minutes ago (`RECENT_CODE_SIGN_IN_MS`, `req.session.signInViaEmail`; a desk code never counts, and the view switch carries the stamps), and a wrong current password charges the lockout. New passwords are 8 characters to 72 UTF-8 bytes (`passwordLengthError`: bcrypt reads only 72 bytes). After the bcrypt awaits the commit re-asks `participantSessionDenied` and compares the stored hash (409 `credential_changed`). A changed password ends the account's other sessions; setting or changing one mails `password_changed_notification` to the primary. Locks: `tests/login-method.api.spec.js`, `tests/login-method-ui.spec.js`.
- **A sign-in answer with `password_required` opens "Set your password" (`openPasswordRequiredPrompt`) before routing**; "Set password" and "Keep using emailed codes" (stores `code`) are its only exits. It is asked at each interactive sign-in until resolved, never when a session reloads. `GET /api/me` and `GET /api/admin/me` carry the same four fields, never under impersonation (a credential is the owner's alone; the profile then shows no "How you sign in" row).
- **The login response carries the user `id`**; the admin view sets `S.currentUser` from it for the own-row checks.
- **`verificationGraceState(user)` (`lib/self-enroll.js`) is the JS grace classifier and `ACTIVE_PARTICIPANT_SQL` (`lib/competitions.js`) its SQL mirror**; grep both before changing verification semantics. On lapse `requireParticipant` destroys the session with 403. `requireAdmin`/`requireSuperAdmin` stay strict.

#### Sign-in codes
- **`lib/login-code.js` owns codes. `isCodeEligible` is the one predicate: not blocked, not denied, any role**; `codeBlockedReason` its inverse (`account_inactive`). **The desk reveal is narrower** (`_codeBlockedReason`, `routes/participants.js`): participants only (an admin's code on another admin's screen signs in as them), approved only (a desk code never approves), **never signed in only** (`hasSignedIn` false: `login_time`/`last_login_time` both NULL, else 409 `signed_in_before`; operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026: a shown code is a full session as the person, so once signed in the desk can only email one); the same function feeds the row's `can_use_code`/`code_blocked_reason` (the kebab note names Email sign-in code) and the desk duplicate remedy, so the three cannot disagree. The reveal is also a rival door (`403 competitor_cannot_edit`; see *Staff can compete, and the lock that binds every tier*); a desk code redeemed by an account no longer a participant, or since signed in by another door, is spent and refused. **Teach neither the send cap**: desk reveal works on a spent budget. No feature flag (a scanner forbids `LOGIN_CODES_ENABLED`).
- **The secondary address never signs in (operator ruling, 11 Nov 2026): every sign-in door resolves the PRIMARY only** (`findUserByPrimary`, `lib/account-lookup.js`), as a secondary is an unconfirmed claim anyone can make about an address no account holds. An address held only as a secondary is reserved (`emailIsTaken`): no code or proof, and a redeem or password with it fails. Invitations refuse it (an `errors[]` line, nothing stored) and Roles & permissions "Add user" answers 409 `email_is_secondary` with no account id, so neither hands its holder an event or role. It still counts for the corporate-email rule and gets mail sent to both addresses. Lock: `tests/signin-hardening.api.spec.js`.
- **`_sendSignInCode` (`routes/auth.js`) is the code request's mint path**: eligibility, suppressions, budgets, event binding, audit, a detached send to the primary. Never await SMTP there: the request answers a uniform 200.
- **Re-send suppression: a live code mailed under `LOGIN_CODE_RESEND_MIN_MS` (120 s) ago is never re-minted** (`codeRecentlySent`; audited `login_code_refused{reason:"recently_sent"}`), so a double-submit or someone else typing the address cannot kill the code being typed. In-process: a restart fails open to the account cap. The SPA enables "Send a new code" after the same two minutes (`RESEND_AFTER_MS`); a sooner button would do nothing. The desk reveal never asks it (while a desk code lives, `deskCodeLive` refuses emailed mints: next bullet). The admin's Email sign-in code asks it (`{sent:false, reason:"recently_sent"}`); only a send that fails within the door's deadline (`send_failed`) lifts the hold, a delivered or still-queued (`deferred`) one keeps it, so its lock delivers through a capturing relay (`tests/sign-in-code-delivered.spec.js`).
- **A live desk code is never replaced by an emailed one, for its whole life** (`deskCodeLive`, decided from the stored code, so a restart forgets nothing; operator ruling, 11 Nov 2026): the walk-up is typing it. Continue mints nothing (`login_code_refused{reason:"recently_sent"}`) and the admin's Email sign-in code answers `{ok:true, sent:false, reason:"desk_code_live"}` until it expires (lock: `tests/signin-hardening.api.spec.js`).
- **The event binding belongs to the CODE** (`users.login_code_comp_id`): an emailed mint writes the request's event (`joinToken` from a join link, QR or join code) or NULL, a desk mint always NULL, and `clearCode` clears it with the code; the mail names the event only where redeeming would add the account to it (`codeBoundJoinWillEnrol`, `lib/self-enroll.js`: a HAND-KEPT copy of the code-bound ladder, `_consumeCodeBoundEnroll` then `_enrolAtSignIn`, plus "already a member"; change both together; lock: the sign-in code arm of `tests/email-scenarios.spec.js`, mail against outcome for a participant, an organiser, a member and staff where staff compete). Only redeeming an EMAILED code enrols (`_consumeCodeBoundEnroll`, via the join marker's `_enrolAtSignIn` ladder), **and only in a browser that itself asked for a code for that address naming that event** (`_sessionNamedEvent`, `routes/auth.js`: its own `codeRequests` note): anyone holding the join link can ask for any address, so typed in any other browser the code, or a new address's proof, carries no event (no enrolment, no join-link approval, and a proof's event does not carry into the sign-up), and the mails say "in the browser where you asked for it" (lock: the last describe of `tests/join-drop-event.api.spec.js`). **It asked through the event's join link as it is now, too:** switching a link off or replacing it unties every code and proof asked through it (see *Self-enrollment links*), so the mails add "as long as its join link is still open". Never an admin tier (`_enrolAtSignIn` refuses every admin-class role, so checking a link makes no row or Checkmarx One account; operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026), nor the `staff` tag on a `staff_compete` event: the SPA's "Compete in this event?" confirm decides that, through the ordinary join door. Never enrol from a form submit: that is forced enrolment.
- **"Not your event? Continue without it" unties a code already emailed with the event** (operator rulings, 18 and 20 Nov 2026): `POST /api/auth/code/drop-event` (`routes/auth.js`, 200 `{ok:true}` always, limiter first, `_AUTH_PATHS`, `_IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED`) clears `login_code_comp_id` (or `email_proofs.join_comp_id`, or the session's own `emailProof.joinCompId`) ONLY for the code this session's request minted: the code request writes `req.session.codeRequests` (fingerprint, event, `codeDropTag` of the minted hash: an HMAC under the per-boot session secret) for EVERY well-formed outcome, so its answer and cookie stay uniform, and the untie compares that tag with the stored hash (compare-and-set: a code re-minted since, by any browser, is not this one's), and clears the note's event either way, so a code carrying it that this browser cannot untie still joins nothing here. **The untie itself saves the session and reads the address's code or proof on every well-formed call too** (`codeDrops`), or its cookie would say whether this browser's request minted anything, which for a free-mail address says whether an account holds it. The code, its window, budget and suppression stay; nothing is mailed; the join marker and existing rows stay; an approval-gated account loses the link's approval with its event. Audited `login_code_event_dropped` (user) or an orphaned `email_proof_event_dropped`. The client's `leaveJoinedEvent` (`features/auth.js`, the banners' and the event-code dialog's `event-code-discard`) unties first, then forgets the event, and keeps a live send remembered with no event, so Continue reopens the code step rather than asking for a new code (two minutes on, that would replace the untied one); its `ha:last-code` marker records the send's event and is never reused for another (lock: `tests/join-drop-event.api.spec.js`, the browser arms of `tests/signin-dialogs.spec.js`).
- **Request and redeem answer 200 for every outcome; the client branches on `d.ok`** (limiter 429 and 403 `csrf_invalid` excepted). Refusals for a known row record `login_code_refused` with a reason, a wrong redeem code `login_failed{reason:"invalid_code"}`; the counters are the only monitoring.
- **Never steer a code holder to request another**: a new request supersedes the held code and spends a capped send. A redeem 429 never says "expired".
- **A code is 8 Crockford base32 characters, a one-way door once in email templates**; for margin raise `LOGIN_CODE_TTL_MS`, not the length. Only a sha256 is stored. `login_code_at` stays at the first mint across re-requests. The `normaliseCode` U/V fold is open (see *Known open gaps*).
- **`LOGIN_CODE_MAX_SENDS` (3 per account per window) is an email budget**; one origin (IPv4 /24, IPv6 /64, `originKeyFor`, read from the address's groups, never its compressed text) may spend 2 of it (`_originSpend`); never key the budget on (account, origin): that makes parallel budgets, so mail, and invalidations of the code the owner is typing, grow with every origin an attacker reaches (`lib/login-code.js`). **`clearCode` (and the redeem's claim, `consumeCode`) closes the window and clears the origin share and the suppression**, so a third sign-in inside ten minutes from one desk is never refused. The fresh-window branch must assign `login_code_sends`, not skip the increment (lock: `tests/desk-code-budget.spec.js`).
- **Redemption is keyed on (primary address, code)**; `resolveCodeAttempt` hashes unconditionally. A correct code is spent by the claim `consumeCode` (a compare-and-swap on the matched hash, before the redeem's other writes), never a bare `clearCode`, so single use never rests on there being no await (lock: `tests/code-redeem-single-use.api.spec.js`). A wrong well-formed code counts toward lockout; a correct one is honoured while locked and clears it, so never gate on `isLockedNow`. **A wrong code gets the same bare `{ok:false}` whatever the account's state** (approved, pending, locked, blocked, denied, or no account): approval, block and denial are consulted only once a code matches, and the lock only decides whether the guess is counted, so only a correct code may say `pending_approval`. Block and deny leave a live code stored, so the redeem's own `isCodeEligible` is what refuses it (lock: `tests/signin-code-refusals.api.spec.js`; a denial alone: the v6.16.17 L1 arm of `tests/functional-security-b.api.spec.js`).
- **The lockout is keyed by account, ONE failure budget for codes and passwords** (a wrong code, password or current password charges it; a sign-in guess while locked is not counted again; a correct code or password is honoured while locked and clears it). **A lock is an alarm, not a bar:** it mails the holder and refuses no guess, so what bounds guessing is each door's rate limits (see *Rate limiting*); never describe it as stopping guesses. **Never re-key it to (IP, account)**: guesses spread over many sources would never raise the alarm. `lib/lockout.js` alone owns the constants and helpers (a Docs-hygiene lock forbids a second `LOCKOUT_MAX` or raw lockout-column writes). Kept at their sites on purpose: the audit emits, the literal `trackDetachedSend(sendLockoutEmail(...))` (scanned), the unblock UPDATE and the bulk-approve lock test in its SQL `WHERE`. The lockout mail has no reset link: it says a correct password or emailed code still signs in and lifts the lock.
- **Only an emailed code (`login_code_via='email'`) proves the inbox**: it verifies and, unless `require_account_approval` (`lib/settings.js` `getRequireAccountApproval`, default off, a superadmin Settings switch; a database straight from 8.14 boots with it on, once, operator ruling 15 Nov 2026: `db.js`, marker `approval_gate_upgrade_checked_at`, lock `tests/approval-gate-upgrade.spec.js`) is on, approves a pending, not-denied account (`approved_by='login_code'`, `approved{via:"login_code_auto_approval"}`). With approval on (unless the code's event or the account's pending join is a live join link: `joinApproves`, approved `via:"self_enroll_auto_approval"`), or with a desk code, an unapproved account's code is consumed and answered `{ok:false, error:"pending_approval"}`. Blocked and denied never sign in or approve. A desk or NULL provenance re-stamps a 48-hour grace on an unverified account (operator decision).
- **Redeeming an emailed code claims every live invitation addressed to the account's PRIMARY address** (`claimInvitationsForAccount(user, user.email, …)`, `lib/invitation-claim.js`): events through `competitionAdmission`, the late-entry took-part refusal and `staffRowRefusal`; groups through `detectMemberAddClashes`; failures logged, never blocking. Nothing is claimed without a proven inbox: sign-up claims on its address-proof grant (`POST /api/register`), an account held for approval at its first sign-in (lock: `tests/invitation-claim.api.spec.js`, each skip listed before a grant on the same invitation; a desk code claims nothing).

#### Email first — the address proof and the sign-up grant
- **One entry screen, no Sign Up button.** Continue on any address but a password account's opens the code step; `POST /api/auth/code/request` answers byte-identical `{ok:true}` to all of these, awaiting no SMTP: an account's primary gets a sign-in code, one nobody holds an **address proof** (`lib/email-proof.js` `issueProof`, table `email_proofs`, never a user row; mail reason `email_proof`), and a free-mail one with no live invitation riding the request (`invitationId`), or a reserved one (somebody's secondary, a pending primary change), nothing (orphaned `email_proof_refused`, domain + fingerprint, never the address). The proof mail's `email_sent` record names `to_domain` and `to_fp` (the same `ep_` fingerprint) and `error:"send_failed"`, never the address or SMTP text: an anonymous caller chose the address. Proofs share the sign-in code's budgets (`LOGIN_CODE_MAX_SENDS`, the origin share, the re-send suppression) and spend a per-IP one (see *Rate limiting*); the `LOCKOUT_MAX`-th wrong guess burns its code (empty hash) but keeps the row until it expires, so the address's send budget and re-send suppression survive (a delete handed out a fresh budget), and records orphaned `email_proof_burned` (domain + fingerprint); the 5-minute session GC sweeps expired rows (lock for both codes' lifetimes and the proof's send cap and origin share: `tests/signin-code-expiry.api.spec.js`). A base `CREATE TABLE` (fatal, so no `REQUIRED_COLUMNS` entry: that is for ALTERs), not a `DATA_DIR` child (backup unaffected).
- **Redeeming a proof creates nothing.** `redeemProof` deletes the row (single use); the session is regenerated with a grant in `req.session.emailProof` (`EMAIL_PROOF_GRANT_MS`, 15 min; its event and invitation). The answer is `{ok:true, needsProfile:true, email}`; a wrong or dead code gets the sign-in code's `{ok:false}`.
- **`POST /api/register` asks `liveProofGrant(req.session)` before every field check, the type guard and the picture check included** (403 `email_proof_required`), uses the GRANT's address (ignoring a body `email`) and refuses one an account took meanwhile (409 `email_taken`). It creates the account verified, approved unless `require_account_approval` is on (then `{ok:true, pendingApproval:true}`); a sign-up through a live join link, QR or event code is approved whatever the setting (operator ruling, 3 Nov 2026; confirmed 17 Nov 2026, an account already waiting included), spends the grant, claims invitations to the address, and signs it in through `_signInAs` (the code redeem's own bookkeeping). Event and invitation come from the grant; a body `joinToken` only fills a grant that has none (or whose link has closed since). An invitation id naming another address is dropped (never a 409); with no invitation or event riding the request, a live invitation to the proven address is the registration's (approved, enrolled); a held sign-up claims nothing (lock: by source, `tests/email-first-signin.api.spec.js`). **An invitation approves only through an inviter who may approve** (operator ruling, 23 Nov 2026): while `require_account_approval` is on, an invitation whose inviter lacks `participants.approve` NOW (`invitations.invited_by_id`, else the `invited_by` address; gone, blocked, unapproved or the old `admin` default fails closed: `lib/rbac.js` `inviterMayApproveNow`, asked inside the write) makes a waiting account held like any held sign-up, its invitation still live, claimed at its first approved emailed-code sign-in; an account that may approve re-inviting the address takes a live invitation whose inviter cannot (`_takeOverForApproval`, `routes/invitations.js`: `invited_by`/`invited_by_id`, counted `inviter_replaced` on `batch_sent`) (lock: `tests/enrol-only-approval.api.spec.js`).
- **The short form:** first and last name, company, job title, the detected country and timezone (an undetectable zone becomes `UTC`), then whatever the event requires. The nickname is not asked: `_suggestNickname` (`routes/auth.js`) makes a unique one under the `/api/check-nickname` rules.
- **Nothing merges:** one person with two accounts under two addresses keeps both.
- **Specs register through `tests/helpers.js` `registerPost`**, which runs the real doors in a fresh context, then (unless `keepState: true`) rewinds the row to a FIXTURE state via the test-only `POST /api/test/registration-legacy-state` (unverified with a live verify_code, never signed in, pending unless a join link or invitation made it), so specs written for it still test what they test. No sign-up creates that state: a spec about what a sign-up creates passes `keepState: true` (`tests/signup-real-state.api.spec.js` pins an ordinary and a held sign-up before and after the first sign-in; `tests/email-first-signin.api.spec.js` the doors). A malformed, free-mail or taken address never gets a proof, so a lock on those refusals asserts the entry door via `tests/helpers.js` `entryCode` (what Continue mailed, if anything) plus the form's 403 `email_proof_required`; field-validation locks send a valid work address so the grant exists and the refusal is the field's.

#### Sessions and CSRF
- **One session per principal, the built-in included**: `_signInAs` and `/api/login` call `invalidateOtherSessions` (`lib/sessions.js`).
- **Purged ids are tombstoned in `revoked_sessions` before the DELETE**; the store `set` wrapper (`server.js`) refuses them and `destroy` tombstones first, so no in-flight save resurrects a deleted row. Retention `2 * SESSION_MAX_AGE_MS`. `_dropRevokedSessionCookie` (`server.js`) drops the one `Set-Cookie` entry carrying a tombstoned id. `wasSessionPurged` fails open on a DB error except in the commit-time re-checks (`failClosed:true`).
- **Every `invalidateOtherSessions` call passes a context** (scanner): a sign-in door `kickerContext(req, user)` (`passwordless` follows `effectiveLoginMethod`: a code account is told to guard its inbox, a password account to sign in with a code and change its password, each to tell an organiser; pass a full row, since a partial one reads as passwordless), any other purge a `SESSION_END` reason (`lib/sessions.js`; today only `ended`), reported as `/api/session/status` `reason`; without one the popup claims inactivity. `ended` names no cause.
- **Sessions expire after 60 minutes idle and slide on a data write** via `_slideSession` (`touch()` plus `slidAt`). **Never use `rolling: true`**: it re-stamps the cookie, not the row, so `_sweepExpiredSessions` deletes active sessions.
- **Only authenticated non-GET `/api` requests slide.** `_SESSION_SLIDE_SKIP`/`_shouldSlideSession` exclude endpoints that `regenerate()` or `destroy()` themselves and timer-driven non-GETs, matched on the normalised path. `/api/select-competition` deliberately slides.
- **Keep both arms of the keepalive gate (`public/js/features/session-monitor.js`): `wasRecentlyActive || inLiveCompetition`**. Either arm sends it only once the expiry is within `keepaliveSkipAboveMs` (55 of the 60 minutes), not every 30 s tick: a session-row write per tab per tick was the cost (lock: `tests/participant-pulse-ui.spec.js`). A tick reading an expiry past the warning threshold closes an open warning first (another tab's writes slid the shared session, and the skipped keepalive cannot close it; lock: `tests/ui-account-journeys.spec.js`).
- **`GET /api/csrf-token` answers 503 `session_cookie_blocked` when `_sessionCookieWouldBeDropped(req)`**, always `no-store`. Each anonymous call persists a row the 5-minute session GC removes.
- **Tabs share one session, so a tab whose token another tab's session replaced re-syncs before it writes** (`public/js/core/api.js` `ensureFreshCsrf`). Each tab publishes a fingerprint of every token it is issued under localStorage `ha:csrf-fp`; the interceptor asks before every write, and every write bypassing it asks `readyToWrite` and registers in flight (`trackInflightWrite`; scanner in `tests/csrf-cross-tab.spec.js`). It takes the live session's token only when `_liveSessionIsMine` or the tab shows nobody; else the tab is out of date (`S._sessionOutOfDate`) and sends nothing. In one tab, a signed-in door that regenerates the session sends via `sendReplacingSession`: the interceptor's `_HOLDS_WRITES` (EMPTY: `PUT /api/me/sign-in-method` keeps its own session, so it is no such door; kept for a future one) or `_sessionTransitionPost` (the view switch, impersonation in or out). Doors run one at a time; each holds the tab's other writes, lets those in flight settle, re-checks the token, then sends; held writes leave re-stamped once a token REQUESTED after the door's answer arrived is issued. A failed door releases them; any other end of a sent door's hold marks the token dead (`_tokenDead`). A new such door joins one of the two.
- **A refused CSRF token (`EBADCSRFTOKEN`) ends every identity-bearing session, the built-in's included**; `_sessionHasIdentity` (`lib/sessions.js`) is the one definition, shared with `_isIdentityProbe401`. Anonymous sessions are kept unaudited so the retry's token works (lock: `tests/csrf-token-delivery.spec.js`).
- **CSRF handler order is load-bearing** (`destroy()` drops `req.session` synchronously): close any impersonation window; record `sessions_invalidated{reason:"csrf_invalid"}` on the owner (acting admin under impersonation, never the target); capture `_actorAtDestroy`; destroy (lock: `tests/csrf-session-end.api.spec.js`).

#### Built-in superadmin (the one break-glass account)
- **Its password is regenerated each boot and written to `DATA_DIR/.superadmin-password` from the `portCheck` listening handler, never module scope**, so a duplicate `npm start` cannot overwrite the live credential. No env override. It rotates after 10 server-wide failed logins in 15 minutes (10-minute cooldown). The one account with no code door (break-glass for a mail outage). Its sign-in passes `kickerContext(req, { email: "superadmin", builtin: true })`, so the kicked browser's popup gives a restart as the remedy.
- **Its USERNAME is the setting `breakglass_username`** (`lib/settings.js` `getBreakglassUsername`, default `superadmin`). `POST /api/login` signs in only the current name (case-insensitive, re-checked against every account's addresses at each attempt); an old name, any address and a wrong password for the right name all get one generic 401, so the door is no oracle for the name. **The SPA never learns it:** it is typed into the ordinary sign-in card's email box, where a non-address gets the password box alone (no code button, no server lookup) and is sent as `{username, password}` (the legacy `email` field is read too), and no anonymous response (shell, bundle, `/api/config`) carries it. **There is no special sign-in URL** (`/break-glass` is a 404; `/register` stays, for invitation mail). Locks: `tests/breakglass-username.api.spec.js`, `tests/login-method.api.spec.js`.
- **Only the break-glass session itself (`builtinId`, not impersonating) reads or changes it** (`GET|PATCH /api/admin/breakglass-username`, else 403 `breakglass_session_required`, user-backed superadmins included; held twice with `EVENT_ADMIN_FORBIDDEN`). `validateBreakglassUsername`: 3–64 printable characters, no whitespace, no `@` (the card sends only a non-address to `/api/login`), never an account's address. Audited as a `breakglass_username` settings event with `reqContext`, never the names (half a credential; regular admins read app.jsonl); the key is on `lib/log.js` `_REDACT_KEYS`, so no log line, audit copy or settings export carries it. The Settings "Break-glass account" card renders only in that session (`S.isBreakglass`, from the login answer and `/api/admin/me`). The session stays signed in; the new name is needed from the next sign-in.

#### One-click email verification
- **`GET /verify` verifies in one click**, an allowed exception to "GETs never mutate" (see *Email and alerts*). Registration mails no link (the code verifies); `POST /api/resend-verification` does.
- **`_completeVerification` answers every outcome, failures included, with a 302 and `Cache-Control: no-store`; never a 4xx, never inline at `/verify?code=`.** `/verified.html`, `/verify-expired.html` and `/verify-failed.html` are routes (`_RESULT_PAGES` → `_emailResultPage`), not files in `public/`.
- **`verified=1` marks the code consumed; `verify_code` stays as a 48-hour idempotency receipt.** Paths setting `verified=1` keep it, except `/confirm-email`, which replaces the address and clears it; where `verified` returns to 0 (import overwrite), clear it. `sweepStaleVerifyCodes` bounds the receipts.
- **`verified=1` proves some address, not necessarily the current primary.** Neither the secondary email nor `pending_email` is a sign-in identity (see *Sign-in codes*).

#### Email change and corporate-address rules
- **Primary-email changes are staged in `pending_email`, never applied directly.** `PUT /api/me` cancels the stage on any save leaving the primary unchanged (an omitted `email` counts); the admin change has no cancel branch and answers 200 whatever SMTP did. `pending_email_code` never reaches a client.
- **Guard primary-plus-secondary writes with `sameSlotEmails` (`lib/util.js`)**: per-column unique indexes allow primary equal to secondary.
- **`isFreeEmail` (corporate-email rule) is bypassed by an admin `invitationId`, the superadmin import, a superadmin participant edit and Roles & permissions Add user (operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026: an admin account may use a personal address; these three audited `bypassed_free_email_rule`), and the perf bulk-seeder.** A join token does not bypass it. An existing address on a registration form skips it because nothing is created.

#### Unverified-account reaper
- **Only pre-email-first and imported accounts are ever unverified** (every account door creates verified; the participant import may write `verified: 0` to force re-verification). The verification link, the 48 h grace, the "Verify email" chip with its resend and the reaper are kept for them (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026; retiring them needs its own release: a migration settling the unverified accounts and a reworked `registerPost` unverified default).
- **`REAP_GATE_SQL` (`lib/account-reaper.js`) alone defines what is reaped**, grandfather floor (`account_reaper_floor_at`) included; any reader other than the sweep interpolates it. `registered_via='linkedin'` rows stay exempt.
- **`first_verified_at` is never cleared; NULL is the reaper's gate.** Updates use `COALESCE(first_verified_at, ?)`. Export and import carry it.
- **`deleteUserCascade` is the single deletion helper** (shared with admin delete; busts caches, purges sessions, stamps the Checkmarx One ledger, so a reaped account's created accounts are removed as a manual delete's; its `account_reaped` carries the delete's `cxone_*` counts, and a warn line when any need a human). Skipped under `NODE_ENV=test`; tests call `POST /api/test/reap-accounts`.

### Required user profile fields

- **Base set (the short form: operator order 29 Oct 2026, confirmed 14 Nov 2026): `first_name`, `last_name`, `email`, `company`, `title`, `country`, `timezone`; the nickname is suggested at sign-up.** `PUT /api/me` answers 400 to an empty `company`, `title`, `country` or `timezone` (an empty `job_function` or `city` is stored as NULL). An empty name, nickname or email keeps the stored value. Any other field is asked only if the registration event requires it. **The gate may only shrink:** a default change never adds a field an existing account is asked for (lock: `tests/email-first-signin.api.spec.js`).
- **Add a new users-table or profile-dialog field to `FIELDS` in the "User-field matrix" (lock: `tests/ui-admin-competitions.spec.js`),** or it can silently drop off export, import, CSV, `GET /api/me` or `GET /api/admin/participants`.
- **`GET /api/check-nickname` (`routes/auth.js`) must apply every nickname rule registration applies** (trim, `_nicknameCharsOk`, minimum 3 characters, `COLLATE NOCASE` uniqueness), or a green "available" tick precedes a 400 on submit.
- **Single-line profile text holds no control character (C0, DEL, C1): `lib/util.js` `profileTextOk` is the one predicate, over `PROFILE_TEXT_FIELDS`** (names, company, title, city, country, job function, salutation, phone, both certificate names; letters of every script legal). Every write door refuses one with 400 `{code:'profile_text_control_chars', field}`, checked on the value as sent, before any trim: sign-up, `PUT /api/me`, the admin profile edit (every tier, never silently kept), Roles & permissions Add user and the desk invite, each `error` naming the field in words (`PROFILE_TEXT_LABELS`; the forms show only `error`), and bulk-seed, whose `error` names the key (`users[i]: <key> …`); the participant import skips the row and counts it (`control_chars_skipped`). Stored values are not rewritten. A new door or field uses it (lock: `tests/profile-text-control-chars.api.spec.js`).
- **`_profileMissingFields` (`lib/util.js`) is the one predicate.** It returns `company`/`title`/`country`/`timezone`, every configurable field that resolves to `required`, and `corporate_email`. Callers: `GET /api/me` (`missing_fields`, `profile_complete`), `_signInAs`, `POST /api/select-competition`, `requireCompleteProfile`, `requireCompetitionContext`.
- **It throws when a row lacks the `profile_policy` or `registration_competition_id` key, or has a pointer without the joined `_reg_event_kind` and `_reg_field_policy` keys.** A NULL value is normal. Select `PROFILE_GATE_COLUMNS` with `PROFILE_GATE_JOIN` (a scanner checks this).
- **`GET /api/me` deletes every `_`-prefixed key before responding.** A named deny-list over a spread would leak the next joined column.

#### Per-event registration fields

- **`lib/registration-fields.js` owns the matrix. Call `registrationFieldStates(comp)`; never re-derive it.** The fields (`personal_title`, `phone`, `city`, `linkedin_url`, `job_function`), each `required|optional|hidden` (the editor's Required · Optional · Off; `data-relax-default` may be `hidden`), in the JSON column `competitions.registration_field_policy` (NULL: the kind's defaults). Every kind is configurable (`CONFIGURABLE_KINDS` is derived from `KIND_DEFAULT_STATES`; operator ruling, 30 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026): create, PUT, templates and clone accept the matrix for any kind, and a kind change keeps it. Every kind's default is `LEGACY_STATES`, the short form (City and Job function optional, Salutation, Phone and LinkedIn off; `BOOTH_DEFAULT_STATES` is the same object), so a NULL column asks no existing event or account anything new. A stored policy still naming `password` (the removed sixth field) is read without it, the key dropped on its next save (`parseRegistrationFieldPolicy` reads known keys only), never a 400.
- **Only `required` is demanded. `hidden` and `optional` both mean "not demanded" at every deciding site.**
