- **Each `[data-relax-field]` group in `public/index.html` declares its own `data-relax-default`; the resting sign-up form (no join link) works from markup alone** (see *Client/server mirror registry*). `_applyRelaxedProfileFields` (`features/self-enroll.js`) writes `data-relax-state` when a join link resolves and restores the defaults when it clears; `doRegister` reads `data-relax-state`, else the default.
- **`corporate_email` is outside the configurable set for every kind, path and policy. `secondary_email` is deliberately not configurable (operator exclusion): it is how an account with a personal primary meets the corporate-email rule (the gate asks for it), so an event that hid it could refuse a walk-up who has no way to comply.**
- **Audit a matrix edit per field, never as raw JSON:** `changes.registration_field_policy.fields[key] = {from, to}` in resolved states (`routes/competitions.js`); a save that moves no field (only rewriting the stored shape, e.g. dropping the retired `password` key) is written but records nothing (lock: `tests/booth-registration-fields.spec.js`).

#### The registration pointer

- **The requirement is resolved live from `users.registration_competition_id` (the event the account registered through), never from the selected event.** A newly mandatory field reaches existing registrants, even after the event ends. The check is per user, not per event (accepted).
- **Writers: the join link (a NEW registration), the desk invite with a named event (re-read at commit), and a one-shot boot backfill in `db.js` (`desk_registration_link_backfill_at`).** Sign-up, invitation, an existing-address registration and import leave it as it was.
- **The pointer has no foreign key, on purpose: a cascade would erase it exactly when it is needed.** A NULL or dangling pointer falls back to the kind in `users.profile_policy`. An unknown kind maps to `LEGACY_STATES`.
- **A legacy `registered_via='linkedin'` account is asked and edited like any other** (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026): it changes its names and LinkedIn URL itself, and a required `linkedin_url` is asked of it.

#### Completion gate and server enforcement

- **`openProfileCompletionGate` (`public/js/features/auth.js`) cannot be dismissed (no X, Cancel or Escape). It is awaited before the participant nav or routing is built.** Triggers: `finishSignIn()` (every sign-in door, after any "Set your password" prompt), `boot()`, `selectCompetition()`, `loadMyCompetitions()` (once, `S._compsProfileRetry`), and the fetch interceptor on a participant's 400 `profile_incomplete`. The interceptor leaves paths on `_PROFILE_GATE_SKIP` alone and re-sends any other request once. Concurrent callers share `S._profileGatePromise`.
- **After each save, the gate re-reads `GET /api/me` and re-opens for any field still missing.** For a `corporate_email` miss it asks for the secondary email, because a primary-email change is only staged.
- **No admin tier is ever gated: the overlay would cover the Admin View button, their only way out.** The real guard is the sign-in answer (`_signInAs`, every account door), which sends `missing_fields: []` for admins (`routes/auth.js`); keep it in step with the client role tests. Switching to Participant View first shows the dismissible `confirmParticipantViewProfile`.
- **`requireCompleteProfile` (`lib/middleware.js`) gates actions with no competition context. `requireCompetitionContext` runs the same check first on every competition-scoped route.** Grep `requireCompleteProfile` for the live set. These must stay exempt: `GET`/`PUT /api/me` (the fix path), `PUT /api/me/sign-in-method` (its prompt runs before the gate), logout, resend-verification, and csrf/config/session-status/keepalive.

#### Admin edit-profile permissions

- **`PUT /api/admin/participants/:id`: a regular admin gets a blanket 403 on an admin or superadmin target.** On a participant target, a regular admin may only fill blanks (`fillRequired`/`fillOptional`; the avatar only while unset or `default1`) and change both emails; a refused value is silently kept. A superadmin may edit any field on any target. No tier can erase a base-set field, but a superadmin can clear `personal_title`, `phone` and `linkedin_url`. The client mirrors these rules (read-only "View Profile"). A primary-email change is only staged (see *Email change and corporate-address rules*). No field is locked by registration source.

#### Edit-profile dialog contract

- **One renderer, `public/js/ui/profile-form.js`, serves both the self and admin dialogs.** A leaf module (imports only `ui/` and `core/`). Element ids differ only by prefix (`prof-`, `adp-`). The only policy input is the `locked(field)` predicate. Its `REQUIRED_FIELDS` (names, nickname, email and the base set PUT /api/me refuses to empty; never City or Job function) alone drives the red star, `aria-required` and the self-edit save's "Required:" refusal; what the registration event requires is the completion gate's. A save repaints the header through `ui/nav.js` `refreshAccountHeader`: the participant bar in participant view only, the account button alone in Admin View. Lock: `tests/profile-editor-save.spec.js`.
- **A save sends only changed fields: `profileChangedFields` diffs against the server row (`overlay._row`) per body key (`PROFILE_BODY_TO_ROW`).** Never diff against the DOM as it was when the dialog opened (`autoDetectCountryTz` fills it first), and never per element id. A field counts as changed when dirty, not when truthy: a cleared field is sent as `""`. Validation still reads the full DOM `fields`.
- **Save's no-op test is that diff (no keys: close, no PUT). The raw-value snapshot `overlay._isDirty()` only drives the prompts: "Discard changes?" on Cancel/X/Escape, "Save changes?" on Save.** A whitespace-only edit reads dirty but changes nothing; a PUT would toast "Profile updated." and cancel a staged primary-email change. The snapshot is taken after the selects are populated. The LinkedIn URL must be `http(s)`, checked on client and server.
- **Optimistic concurrency: dialogs send `expectedUpdatedAt`. The compare-and-swap is part of the profile UPDATE's WHERE, as the first statement in the transaction, and uses `IS` (not `=`). A stale value gets 409.** Read the version through `profileVersionSql()`, because a new row's `updated_at` is NULL. Only profile writes bump `updated_at`, never login or event selection, and no trigger maintains it.
- **`avatar-select` is one delegated action for both editors: `selectAvatarIn` (`ui/profile-form.js`) sweeps only the clicked `.avatar-grid` and calls its `_onSelect`.** Both dirty snapshots include `overlay._avatar`, or an avatar-only edit reads clean.
- **Uploads are downscaled in the browser (`downscaleImageFile`, `AVATAR_UPLOAD_MAX_PX`), decoded with `createImageBitmap` onto a white-filled canvas.** `URL.createObjectURL` plus `<img>` fails silently (the CSP `img-src` has no `blob:`); without the fill, transparent PNGs go black.
- **Avatars are validated early and stored late.** `validateAvatarDataUri` runs first; a rejected avatar gets 400, never a silent `default1`. Store the avatar only after every other check passes, and reclaim the replaced blob. Never use `sanitiseAvatar` as a guard: it INSERTs, and an orphaned image stays until the image sweep, a week later. A replaced avatar is reclaimed at once only when `imageIsReferenced` (`lib/image-sweep.js`, the sweep's own manifest) finds no other holder. A dead `/api/images/` reference keeps the current avatar, and so does any existing picture that is not the account's own current avatar (`sanitiseAvatar(…, {current})`; without `current`, no existing picture; the import's `preserve` asks only that it exists): an account must not point its avatar at another person's photo or an older certificate's image, whose own deletes would break it (lock: `tests/profile-stale-avatar.spec.js`).
- **`country` is free text on the server (trimmed, at most 80 characters, no ISO allowlist).** Imports store values such as "UK". The client keeps a `<select>` because it narrows the timezone list. `_preserveUnlistedOption` re-offers a stored value not in the list.
- **Timezone validators must test whether `Intl` can construct a formatter, not look the zone up in `Intl.supportedValuesOf`.** That list omits `UTC`, which containers report. See `isValidTimezone` (server, through `lib/time.js` `zoneFormatter`: one memoised formatter per zone, which `formatEmailDate` also uses; never a fresh `Intl.DateTimeFormat` or `.toLocale…String(` in server code, scanner: functional-core-b Docs hygiene) and `_isValidTz` (client). Registration, `PUT /api/me`, the admin edit and the import trim and validate the zone (the import blanks an invalid one). Bulk-seed stores it unvalidated.

### Roles, scope and impersonation

ROLES_AND_PERMISSIONS.md is the source of truth for the four built-in roles, custom roles, the event-admin route matrix and the accepted asymmetries; update it in the same change as any role behaviour.

#### Tiers and the commit-time re-checks
- **Server tier checks ask the role's CLASS, never a role literal** (`lib/rbac.js`: `roleClass(role)` → `none`|`granted`|`all`, `isGlobalClassRole`, `roleIdsOfClass(cls)` for a SQL `json_each` list; `{live:true}` skips the 2 s role-definition cache at a commit-time or lifecycle re-check). `granted` is scoped by `competition_admins` exactly as the event admin (`isEventAdmin` = class `granted`; off leaderboards), `all` is unscoped and a privileged target; an unknown role key is `none` (fails closed). **`privilegedTargetFor(req, role)` (`lib/rbac.js`) is the one server "privileged target" predicate, at every peer door** (grep it and its alias `_privilegedTarget`): class `all`, a role holding a permission the actor lacks, or an unknown role; never for a superadmin. Any account can sign in by a code to its primary, so editing a privileged account's addresses takes it over: never test a role literal or the class alone there (lock: the source scanner in `tests/privileged-target-custom-role.api.spec.js`; a new peer door joins its `DOORS`; the participant import's overwrite, outside `DOORS`, is held by behaviour: *Import rules*). Built-in role literals remain only where a rule is about that built-in role (the impersonated organiser seat is the built-in `event_admin`; an account holding a custom role is not impersonable, `impersonation_target_custom_role`). A custom role reaches a `requireSuperAdmin`/`requireGlobalAdmin` route only by holding its permission, read live (`customRoleAllows`); `adminAuthzDenied` takes `{perm}` (a built-in role maps it to its tier). New routes guard with `requirePermission(perm)`.
- **A platform-wide permission (`lib/rbac.js` `PLATFORM_WIDE_PERMISSIONS`) is held only by a role of scope `all`**: their routes reach beyond any one event (the set's comment lists why each is there: the organiser grant and a blank create included), and a `granted` role is bound exactly as an event admin. The role form refuses one on a `granted` role (400 `role_permission_needs_every_event`), `_readRoleDef` drops a stored one and boot prunes such grants once per set (`every_event_grants_pruned_at`), so nothing else needs its own check. **Every other permission a `granted` role holds narrows to its events on every route, superadmin-tier ones included**: `customRoleAllows` admits it past requireSuperAdmin/requireGlobalAdmin, so each such handler asks a §4 predicate (scanner: the scoped-role arm in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`, where `actorId(` alone does not count; lock: `tests/rbac-scoped-role-reach.api.spec.js`). A new superadmin-tier route either narrows or gets a platform-wide permission. **A non-grantable permission (`grantable:false`) is held only by built-in roles**: `ops.cxone` (the platform's Checkmarx One key and its removals; operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026) and `ops.smtp` (the mail server; owner decision, 27 Nov 2026) are two, superadmin only. The editor does not offer one, the form refuses it (400 `role_permission_not_grantable`), `_readRoleDef` drops a stored grant and boot pruned such grants once (`ungrantable_grants_pruned_at`). Each prune records a `role_updated` by `system` per role it changed.
- **Client tier checks name roles positively (`admin`/`superadmin`/`event_admin`), never `!== 'participant'`**, so a new role inherits nothing; a CUSTOM role is an admin role only by the scope mode the server sends (`role_info.scope_mode` `granted`|`all`, on the sign-in answer, `/api/me` and `/api/admin/me`), asked through `ui/nav.js` `accountIsAdminRole`. Where: `public/js/ui/nav.js` `buildParticipantNav`. **Roles are changed on Roles & permissions only** (owner's decision, 27 Nov 2026): the People row has no Promote/Demote, only "Change role on Roles & permissions" (`participant-open-roles`, a superadmin, not on their own row or a rival's), which opens that page searched to the person (`users-admin.js` `showUserInRoles`).
- **`requireAdmin`/`requireSuperAdmin`, `GET /api/admin/me` and `POST /api/toggle-admin-view` re-read the user row every request** (blocked, denied_at, verified, approved, role): out-of-band changes (import, direct SQL) write no tombstone. The built-in superadmin (`builtinId`) has no row and skips it.
- **A guard vouches for the headers, not the body.** An admin route that parses its body after the guard (multer, import parser) or awaits before its first durable write (bcrypt, ZIP walk) calls `adminAuthzDenied(req, {tier})` (`lib/middleware.js`) after its LAST await, nothing awaited between check and write; one await earlier passes every test but misses a revocation. Grep `adminAuthzDenied(`. Superadmin-only routes ask `{tier:"superadmin"}` (a superadmin demoted mid-hash must not mint a superadmin row); a `requireGlobalAdmin` route that awaits before its write must ask `{tier:"global"}`. An admin READ that discloses after an await asks `{read:true}`, which admits any hand the organiser's panel opens to (a regular admin's read-only view), read live; a write keeps the superadmin-only hand (the top-three ZIP: `tests/top3-inflight-authz.api.spec.js`).
- **`adminAuthzDenied` reads, uncached, the row (hand and seat under impersonation) and the tombstone (`wasSessionPurged`)**; each catches what the other misses. Tiers come from the positive map `ADMIN_TIER_ROLES`; an unknown tier throws. Refusals: 403 `admin_access_revoked`, or 401 `admin_session_ended` when purged; the copy names no cause and nothing is created. Accepted: anything ending the admin's session mid-upload (sign-in elsewhere, view toggle, role change, idle sweep) refuses the upload.
- **A commit-time re-check that depends on role or scope first calls `invalidateEventAdminScope(actorId(req))`** (the seat's cache; only with a non-null id, since none clears every actor), else it trusts the 2 s `_roleCache`/`_scopeCache` window an out-of-band narrowing lands in. Sites: the direct upload's owner re-check (`routes/challenges.js`) and the desk-invite commit (`routes/participants.js`), one transaction asking in order: actor, approval (while `require_account_approval` is on a role without `participants.approve` gets 403 `desk_approval_required`, asked live and also first at the headers, before the address: operator ruling, 23 Nov 2026), the event re-read by plain SELECT (not `getCompetition`), competitor lock, scope (a scoped actor naming no event gets `DESK_EVENT_REQUIRED`), email, `competitionAdmission`.
- **Participant-tier writes that await re-ask at commit.** `participantAuthzDenied(userId, compId)` checks blocked, denied, approved and membership (`isStaff` short-circuits it) and skips `verified` on purpose (unverified impersonation targets, accounts in the 48 h verification grace). It sees only the SEAT, so under impersonation also ask `impersonatingHandDenied(req, {readOnlyTier})`: tombstone → `impersonation_ended`; hand no longer admin → session destroyed (`actor_inactive`); hand no longer superadmin → `impersonation_readonly`, session kept. Sites: task submit (both); certificate mint (`impersonatingHandDenied`, `readOnlyTier:true`). Every such site also asks `participantSessionDenied(req)` (scanner): account lifecycle first (under impersonation the SEAT's, answered with the forced exit by `sendParticipantSessionDenial`), then, for a session acting as its own account (admins too), the tombstone → 401 `session_ended`. The mint also re-asks membership (lock: `tests/participant-inflight-session-mint.api.spec.js`: removed, signed in elsewhere or blocked while the certificate is drawn).
- **Superadmin-only:** editing admin or superadmin accounts (see *Admin edit-profile permissions*); role changes (no self-demotion); deleting a participant playing a live event (needs `force`, audited `forced_from_live_competition`); `PATCH /api/admin/settings` (the platform name is the sender name on all mail).
- **`server.js` `_gateAdminDocs` re-reads the admin row live, never `session.isAdmin`,** so a demotion bites at once, and answers for the account's class in either view (an organiser in Participant View still reads their own guide); it covers `_docsPathNeedsAdmin` (the admin, event-admin and superadmin guides, `/docs/release-notes.md`, the admin screenshot dirs; others get 401), and both `_serveMarkdownHashed` and `express.static` send `private, no-store` there. Event admins never get `superadmin.md` or its screenshots: it names the built-in account's password file. Gating the route and hiding the button are one decision. **`_docsPathNeedsAdmin` is a deny-list: every other file under `docs/` is served to anyone and ships in the image, so each file is a decision**, gated there or on the reasoned public list of the Docs-hygiene lock in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`; an undecided file would be public.

#### Event-admin scope
- **Tier and scope are separate facts.** `role='event_admin'` passes `requireAdmin`, fails `requireSuperAdmin`; `competition_admins` rows say which events (zero grants: valid, fail-closed). Revoking the last grant does not demote (else a scoped act makes a superadmin role change); leaving the tier deletes every grant in the same transaction (`event_admin_grants_cleared`). `event_admin` is granted per event, never offered as a promotion. Every identity payload carries `isEventAdmin`: absent reads false and opens the global panel.
- **`eventAdminGuard` (`lib/rbac.js` `permissionGuard`) is global and default-deny.** Every `/api/admin/*` and `/metrics` route has ONE permission in `lib/rbac.js` `ADMIN_ROUTE_PERMISSIONS` (`{perm, rows:'scoped'|'open'}`); give every new admin route one (a route merged ahead of its code goes in `PENDING_ADMIN_ROUTES`). An unmapped route answers 403 `event_admin_unclassified` (event admin) / `admin_route_unclassified` (custom role); the gate never refuses the two built-in global roles. A built-in event admin lacking the permission gets `event_admin_forbidden` with the route's `EVENT_ADMIN_DENY_COPY` sentence. With none, a superadmin-only permission or an `INLINE_REFUSAL_ROUTES` route falls through to the route's own guard, and any other gets `event_admin_forbidden` with `permissionDeniedCopy` and `permission`; a custom role gets `permission_denied`. `EVENT_ADMIN_ROUTES` / `EVENT_ADMIN_FORBIDDEN` are DERIVED from the map. The guard decides "is this endpoint theirs", the handler "which rows" via `lib/event-admin.js` predicates (locks: the classification scanner in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`, both directions, every catalogue permission used, `scoped` handlers call the primitive; `tests/rbac-builtin-equivalence.api.spec.js`, every frozen route × built-in role against the frozen pre-RBAC maps in `tests/fixtures/rbac-legacy-routes.json`, never regenerated, later routes' permissions against their own guard; one entry, the create refusal's sentence, hand-amended by operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026).
- **`POST /api/admin/competitions/:id/admins` and `DELETE .../admins/:uid` are held twice** (`EVENT_ADMIN_FORBIDDEN` and `requireGlobalAdmin`); each suffices alone, so a passing suite is no reason to delete either.
- **Empty scope means `1=0`, never "no predicate".** `scopedCompetitionIds` returns `null` (unscoped) or `[]` (zero grants); `scopeFilterSql` emits `AND 1=0`. A hand-rolled `ids.length ? clause : ''` opens everything to the actor who must see nothing. `taskVisibilitySql` (`lib/event-admin.js`; `routes/challenges.js` delegates as `_taskVisibilitySql`) keeps its own-and-`public-domain` arms for `[]`.
- **Ownership is by user id** (`tasks.created_by_id`, `competition_tasks.assigned_by_id`, `groups.created_by_id`), as emails move. NULL: no event admin owns it; no backfill. **An event's own question (`tasks.owner_competition_id`) is also its organisers'**: `taskEditDenied` and `taskViewDenied` grant on an owner in scope what authorship grants, and a bank question an in-scope event holds an ASSIGNED copy of counts as "used by an event I run" (`taskViewDenied`, `taskVisibilitySql`, the tasks export's `_taskVisibilityScope`: keep the three alike).
- **Gating the entity does not gate the response rows.** `participantVisibilityScope` (feedback raters, participants export) and `userEventCompetitionScopeSql` (participant History AND the person export) exist once; never copy them. The History clause withholds a row naming ANY out-of-scope event: `details.competition_id`; strict per-key compares (`competition_id` at any depth, `cloned_from`, `from_competition_id`, `to_competition_id`, `competition_ids[]`, and `from`/`to` on `active_competition_changed` only, as elsewhere they hold roles and addresses) that also catch a deleted event's id; and a `json_tree` backstop for any text value that is an existing out-of-scope event or session id. A row naming no event is admitted, one whose details are not JSON withheld (it cannot be judged); it filters in SQL so `total` matches; a user event naming an event under a new key adds it to `_USER_EVENT_ID_KEYS` (lock: `tests/event-admin-history-cross-event.api.spec.js`). **An `email_sent` row records the event(s) its mail concerns** (`sendEmailLogged`'s `about`: `{competitionId}`, `null` for none, `{competitionIds}` for a mail listing several; operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026), so the clause withholds one about another event; a row without the key (older, or a send passing nothing) reaches a scoped reader without its subject (`userEventDetailsSql`, the one projection for History, the person export and the Audit page). A user-facing send passes `about`. The participants list narrows via `_userInScopeSql`; group chips like the competitions column. `event_admin_for` comes only from `_eventAdminForMap`, which must SELECT the policy columns.
- **Scope before existence.** A not-found for an id being scoped comes after the scope check (the assign PUT's competition ids, participant lists, Submissions), so it is no oracle.
- **Narrowed projections read by desired-state endpoints are a three-part change.** `GET /api/admin/tasks` narrows `competitions[]`, adding `withheld_competition_count`/`withheld_live`/`withheld_compete` only when withholding. `PUT .../tasks/:id/competitions` compares concurrency and computes removals against `visibleSet`, tests `toAdd` against the full set, scope-checks `competitionIds` and `expectedAssignments`, and caps the CHANGE, not the list.
- **Scoring doors take the strict gate:** publish, visibility and a `qualifying:` tag's add or removal use `taskScoringEditDenied`, as each moves every holding event's scoring or all-solved denominator; at the tag doors its refusal names no out-of-scope event (`_qualifyingScopeMessage`: ruling N7's rule for that door). The client gates on the row's `can_edit_scoring`/`can_edit`, never re-deriving them.
- **Event admins are off leaderboards by ROLE, not the `staff` tag**, which would also bypass membership and list every event (`lib/user-tags.js` `getStaffOrBenchmarkSet`; the board builder, `userScoresOn` and the Overview's pulse all read its role list, `offBoardRolesJson` (per person `isOffBoardRole`)).
- Group create seeds pass `memberTargetsDenied`; `groupDeleteDenied` delegates to `groupWriteDenied`; `_linkRevealable` withholds an invite link naming another organiser's event.
- **An organiser's invitation approves only an account it makes theirs:** for an actor scoped to their events the invitation's auto-approve asks the Approve button's `participantDenied` AFTER the enrolment loop, in its transaction, and never approves a non-participant account; one whose every enrolment was skipped (spectator, author, took part) stays pending, listed in `leftPending` (the dialog keeps the address and says why). A global admin's approves first, as before: the groups' reconcile after commit enrols only an approved account. **A waiting invitee is shown to its events' organisers only** (operator ruling, 23 Nov 2026: "FOR THEIR EVENTS ONLY"): People lists a pending account whose live invitation (`users.invitation_id`) names an event in the viewer's scope, with `waiting_for` (in-scope events only); it is in none of their events, so `participantDenied` refuses their row actions (the client's `_waitingOnly` row offers only View and a note: no row button, selection box or chip control) and only an unscoped admin approves it; the pending badge counts members only (locks: `tests/enrol-only-approval.api.spec.js`, `tests/enrol-only-approval-ui.spec.js`). **The selection bar's Approve (bulk approve) asks the rival question as a row's Approve does** (`skippedRival`; its toast speaks every skip it counts). Lock: `tests/invitation-approve-permission.api.spec.js`.
- **The Invite dialog enforces the route's event rule for event admins only** (`openInviteDialog`): their list is read fresh, never from `S.adminCompetitionsData` (`[]` before its first load would read as "no events"), and a sole tickable event is ticked AND disabled yet still sent, as `:checked` matches a disabled box: never filter on `:enabled` (lock: `tests/event-admin-invite-ui.spec.js`).

#### Participant row-action matrix
- **`tests/participant-actions-matrix.spec.js` locks which kebab items each tier sees on each kind of row, and that each works.** Add a row there for each new item.
- **Roles & permissions' user rows offer People's account doors** (Edit/View profile, History, Block/Unblock, Unlock, Email sign-in code; owner decision, 27 Nov 2026: admin accounts are not on People): `participants-admin.js` `accountDoors` (flags) with `accountPeerNoteHtml` / `accountProfileLabel` / `accountBlockDoorHtml`, the one definition People's row menu also renders, over People's own rows for those accounts (`GET /api/admin/participants?kind=admins`, read only with `participants.view`, kept in `S.adminUserRows`; the door handlers find a row through `personRow`). Lock: `tests/roles-account-doors.spec.js`.
- **`targetPrivilegedFor(role, row)` is the single client "privileged target" predicate** (kebab, `adminEditProfile`, chips); never copy it. A custom role is decided by the row's `role_class` and `privileged_target` (the server's answer for this viewer), named by its `role_label`; for an event admin any non-participant is privileged. A regular admin on a privileged row gets a non-focusable `.kebab-note`, a read-only "View Profile" and a `.chip-note` instead of chip controls: a silently shrunk menu reads as broken. A custom-role row never offers Impersonate; its note says so to a viewer managing the row. Exception (ruling): outside an event workspace (no `S.adminSelectedCompetitionId`: the cross-event People page) "Make/Remove event admin" renders nothing and no note, as a per-event grant has no subject yet.

#### Participant impersonation
- **It is a session swap, not the Participant View toggle.** `POST /api/admin/participants/:id/impersonate` snapshots the admin into `req.session.impersonator`, calls `regenerate()` (the SPA re-fetches the CSRF token) and sets `userId` to the target. Admin and superadmin targets are refused for everyone; blocked, denied and unapproved get 409 `impersonation_target_inactive`. Unverified targets are allowed on purpose; every gate reading the target's `verified` must agree. Rivals are refused (see below); zero-grant event admins cannot enter (`participantDenied`).
- **`impersonationGuard` is global, ahead of every session-acting route** (only csp-report and the health probes precede it), matching on `normaliseRoutePath` (Express routes are case-insensitive). Scope: `/api/*` plus `POST /badge/:token/shared`; add any new non-`/api` route with a session-user side effect. A regular admin's non-GET writes get `impersonation_readonly`; a superadmin passes behind an SPA confirm (UX, not a boundary). It re-reads the ACTING admin's row (never the snapshot; built-in superadmin exempt) and destroys the session if they no longer qualify; a read failure must not grant the write. Hoist no carve-out above this re-read. To test the write tier, demote the acting superadmin to `admin` (not participant, whose lifecycle refusal 403s first either way) beside a built-in-superadmin control.
- **Three predicates, never merged** (`lib/impersonation.js`):
  - `impersonationWriteAllowed`: nothing durable changes (exit, keepalive, select/deselect-competition, toggle-admin-view, login, logout, navigated-away, the certificate preview and join-QR render (POSTs only for their body), `/api/test/*`).
  - `_IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` (both sign-in-code routes, `/api/auth/password`, `PUT /api/me/sign-in-method`) and `_IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED_RE` (nested entry, `impersonation_nested`) bind every tier, checked before the superadmin bypass: the first would sign in as the target, or set the target's credential, with no `impersonated_by`. So does `_IMPERSONATION_JOIN_PATHS` (`POST /api/competitions/join`, `impersonation_join_refused`; operator ruling, 21 Nov 2026: a join made while viewing as someone must not count as theirs, and every "joined" figure counts enrolments); the route repeats it, and the events page shows its sentence in place of the event-code box (`body.impersonating`). Lock: `tests/impersonation-join.spec.js`.
  - `impersonationReadOnlyWriteAllowed`: the certificate mint only, anchored at both ends. Sharing (`/badge/:token/shared`) stays refused for the read-only tier, whose banner still says "read-only".
  - **Viewed as a participant, their certificate's code and token reach only a hand holding `certificates.lookup`** (operator ruling, 23 Nov 2026; `routes/badge.js` `_participantMayReadCodes` over `lib/rbac.js` `handHolds`, the HAND read live, never `actorPermissions`, which answers for the seat): `GET /api/me/certificates`, every mint answer (decided at the send) and the LinkedIn post (404) carry `withheld:true` instead; `/api/me`'s view-as block carries `certificate_codes` (lock: `tests/impersonation-certificate-codes.api.spec.js`).
  - The client mirrors the first in `_impersonationWriteExempt`, never the mint (see *Client/server mirror registry*).
- **Peeking writes no entry bookkeeping** (`login_time`, `last_login_time`, `active_competition_id`, joined events); deselect skips its marker write.
- **A target turning ineligible mid-session gets a restore, never a destroy:** `requireParticipant`, keepalive, `requireAdmin`, `/api/admin/me` and, at commit, the submit and the mint answer through one helper, `restoreImpersonationTargetInactive` (`lib/impersonation.js`): `impersonation_ended`/`target_inactive`. `/api/impersonation/exit` is gated inline on the marker so it stays reachable.
- **Every exit closes the window.** Any path ending an impersonation by regenerating or destroying the session emits `impersonation_ended` first, via `impersonationEndedDetails` or `server.js` `_closeImpersonationWindowOnSessionReplace` (the view toggle carries the marker across its regenerate and emits nothing). Destroys because the actor no longer qualifies use `destroyImpersonationSession`. Each reason needs a History label (locked); nothing recovers a missed close. Logout writes `impersonation_ended` on the target and `logout_manual` on the ACTING admin's row (`impersonated_by` dropped), never a logout on the target. `invalidateOtherSessions` matches the impersonator snapshot, so the admin's re-login kicks the session and the target's does not.
- **Attribution:** `impersonation_started`/`_ended` land on the TARGET's row; the actor is the acting admin; every emit in the window carries the impersonation context (see *Attribution and reqContext*). Ownership columns record the seat. Seal predicates take `actingAdminId(req)`: no seal by proxy.
- **An event admin's scope narrows the participant surface.** `impersonatorScopedCompetitionIds` re-reads the acting admin (`null` unscoped, `[]` nothing) and narrows the picker rows; `impersonationScopeDenied` enforces it (grep it; `requireCompetitionContext` has its own branch that never NULLs the target's marker). `/api/me` carries `scoped_to_my_events`/`withheld_event_count`.

#### An event admin can be looked at in their own panel
- **The session SHAPE is the key.** `impersonator && !isAdmin` is the participant view; `impersonator && isAdmin` is the organiser's panel, where `isEventAdmin`, `scopedCompetitionIds`, `actorId` and `eventAdminGuard` read the SEAT. `isSuperAdmin` is never set, so full access is everything the organiser may do. Setting `isAdmin` without re-keying hands the platform over through the seat.
- **Entry takes `{view:"admin"}`; switching uses `POST /api/toggle-admin-view`**, which carries the marker across its regenerate, refuses before regenerating, and asks `actingAdminCompetesInTargetEvents` itself (it is allowlisted past the guard's re-ask). It skips auto-resume and attributes `admin_view_toggled` to the acting admin. **The panel opens only to the built-in admin and superadmin** (`lib/impersonation.js` `organiserPanelOpensTo`, at entry, the switch and both live re-checks; never a role class): a custom role views a participant only (lock: `tests/impersonation-custom-role-panel.api.spec.js`).
- **The panel's GETs are live-checked:** `requireAdmin`/`/api/admin/me` re-read the acting admin and force-exit if the seat is no longer a live `event_admin` (demoted or promoted).

#### Staff can compete, and the lock that binds every tier
- **`competitions.staff_compete` is a per-event switch for all three kinds, with no policy key** (fatal ALTER, `lib/schema-required.js`). Ask the `lib/staff-compete.js` predicates who competes; read the raw column only in projections, the PUT and the `competing` SQL.
- **Three words.** "Staff" (scoring) is the `staff` tag or `event_admin`. "Competitor" (the lock) is ANY actor with a `competition_users` row on a switch-on event, whatever tier or tag; never the built-in superadmin. "Own" = holding a row.
- **`competitorIds(req)` is the seat AND the hand.** Every competitor door and `rivalsOf` passes it; a scanner forbids `actorId(req)` as the actor, since keyed on the seat alone a competing superadmin could act through another organiser's seat. The module is pure (lazy requires, `db` passed in); predicates throw on a row missing a key.
- **Lock shape and order.** Every refusal is `403 competitor_cannot_edit` in the `competitorRefusalBody` shape: `refuseCompetitor` at the doors, `competitorRefusalBody` inside a transaction, inline in `impersonationGuard`. `_competitorRemedy` offers self-removal only before the event fully ends. Competition and session doors check the lock after the 404 and above scope; assignment doors check it first. Grep `refuseCompetitor(` and `competitorRefusalBody(`.
- **Two carve-outs (operator rulings).** A competitor may DELETE their own row, skipping lock and scope; ended events refuse self-removal (outside a late-entry window), so they stay locked out for good. A competitor keeps the Join Link item, opening read-only (link, QR, code; the toggle and Regenerate withheld with the reason): the reveal is a read, and signage needs it.
- **The lock reaches groups** (`competitorGroupDenied`: member changes, delete, chips, invitation pre-pass) and **rivals' accounts** (`competitorRivalDenied`: verify/approve/deny/block/impersonate/email-code/desk-code/role/delete, benchmark tag, and moving either address through the profile PUT, whose other fields stay editable); the guard re-asks the rival check on every impersonated write. List, picker and Roles & permissions rows carry `rival_in` (one `rivalsOf` per page); the `participants-admin.js` kebab and Roles & permissions' Change role withhold those doors with the reason (profile, History, unlock, ordinary tags stay); withhold any new rival-gated door there too.
- **A switch-on event holds sealed challenges only, closed at four doors:** assignment, the switch, the unseal (409 `answer_seal_in_compete_event`, any status; never add an ended skip, as no revive path re-asks the seal) and the author. The unseal names only in-scope events plus `withheld_competition_count`; the editor (`challenges-admin.js`) withholds Unseal behind `#tf-unseal-held-note` on the row's `withheld_compete`, as the narrowed `competitions[]` alone would offer a refused unseal.
- **Two quirks are pinned; do not "fix" them:** single assignment doors answer the seal first, the bulk door fit then seal; the switch refusal names people `participants[].id`, the assign door's `skippedAuthors[].userId`. Each dialog reads its own shape.
- **The switch compares against the stored value** (an echo is no change) and re-checks in the transaction (`_staffCompeteLockRace`); the form always sends it; refusals sit above `validate_only`. OFF is refused while staff-TAGGED users are enrolled; ON reconciles bound groups after commit. Codes live only in the frozen `CODES`.
- **Non-admin row-writers share `staffRowRefusal`** (`lib/self-enroll.js`): staff-TAGGED users on a non-compete event are spectators with no row (event admins still get one). At the assign, invitation and import doors a held row is a membership, never a skip.
- **Unchanged by the switch:** the analytics `staff` exclusion; CxOne and assignment mail treat a competing admin as a participant.

### Competition lifecycle

#### Status and the two kinds of ended

- **Status is derived from time on every read; never store it.** `lib/competitions.js` (`competitionStatus`, `timeslotStatus`, `competitionFullyEnded`). Server-side, start + duration only via `scheduledEnd(row)` (lock: `tests/schedule-arithmetic.spec.js`, which does not scan `public/`).
- **A stamped `ended_at` is exclusive** (`ended_at <= now` is ended, tested before the inclusive live test): End Now and the auto-end ask `competitionFullyEnded` in the millisecond they stamp. Every revive clears it.
- **Read the effective end** (`competitionEffectiveEnd` = MIN(`ended_at`, scheduled end)); End Now rounds minutes up, so the scheduled end can trail `ended_at` by up to 60 s. Multi-session: the competition's `ended_at` counts only with `ended_manually=1`; else the event ends when every session has.
- **`ended_at` + `ended_manually` tell the ends apart.** NULL = time-expired (extending revives by arithmetic alone); set + 1 = End Now or a duration shrink; set + 0 = all-solved auto-end or, on a multi-session competition row, the last session's End Now (`routes/sessions.js`): judge that row from its sessions. Admin copy (incl. `_getAssignRevivalHints`) branches on all three and never says "all solved" (under a cap, finished = solved or out of attempts).
- **End Now** needs a live event (400), except an idempotent close of a time-ended event with no stamp. It sets `ended_manually=1` and the rounded-up duration, ends upcoming sessions by moving them to the end at duration 0, freezes team boards and calls `_bustLateWindowCaches`.
- **Only a superadmin can edit an ended event whose effective end has passed**; its name is frozen (the unreachable client `endedButFuture` stays so list, dialog and PUT agree). A Booth Event's registration-field controls stay editable when ended (hint `_regEnded`: applies if it runs again) to prepare a rerun; add no ended-lock.

#### Revive rules

- **A revive requires that this request moved the schedule**: `_scheduleMoved` (`start_time|duration_minutes|deadline_at`) feeds `_extendRevives`, the one definition (also the late-deadline base); sessions use `_tsScheduleMoved`. Else a save echoing the schedule within 60 s of End Now reopens a settled event. The client's `revivalWarnings` mirrors it (deadline kind: only when `deadline_at` moved, never `fd.duration_minutes`).
- **The all-solved re-check applies only when `!ended_manually && !self_enroll_enabled && autoEndOnAllSolvedApplies(row)`.** If everyone has still finished, it stays ended (`allSolved:true`); the remedy is adding participants. A manual end plus an extension always revives. Multi-session events revive per session.
- **Assigning a NEW participant revives an auto-ended entity whose scheduled end is ahead**, only for a row actually inserted, not via the late window; it grants no extra attempts.

#### Auto-end vs self-enrollment

- **Trigger**: a session (or session-less event) auto-ends when `autoEndOnAllSolvedApplies` holds, every scoring participant has finished every published challenge and answered every published qualifying question (`qualifyingAnsweredAll`), and self-enrolment is closed. `publishedTaskCount` and `scoringParticipantIds` change together; the predicate is `participantsFinishedAll` (`lib/attempts.js`), shared with both revive gates.
- **Gate the scan on `correct || justExhausted`** (a correct-only gate never ends a capped event), solved-COUNT fast path first. Decide on `freshComp`, re-read after the upload await, never `req.competition`.
- **`auto_ended` keeps `reason:"all_participants_solved"`** though it means solved-or-out-of-attempts; renaming breaks History for old rows. `exhausted_participants` is added only when non-zero.
- **Open self-enrolment suppresses the auto-end** (`autoendPath="self_enroll_open"`): a walk-up may join any moment, and ending would 404 the live link. Time-based ends and End Now are unaffected.
- **Disabling self-enrolment re-arms the auto-end for the next completing solve and never ends the event itself**: a settings save may not end a live event. Lock: `tests/mutations-core.api.spec.js`.

#### A Booth Event never auto-ends on all-solved

- **The kind carries a prohibition (`autoEndOnAllSolved: "never"`), read at the gate**, not merely a 0 default: three writers agreeing on 0 is one writer away from false. Create binds the column from `autoEndOnAllSolvedDefault`, never a literal; `COMPETITION_FIELD_SCHEMA` omits it; `db.js` normalises legacy booth rows on boot. A Program is `"allowed"` on purpose.
- **Keep the perf tags `auto_end_forbidden_for_kind` and `auto_end_disabled` distinct.** Lock: `tests/booth-auto-end.spec.js`.

#### Competition timeslots (sessions)

- Zero sessions = single-slot behaviour. A session choice locks once the participant enters a live one (on a first-open clock, where entry writes no `login_time`, `last_login_time` records the entry for the lock); `select-timeslot` re-derives status inside its transaction. An ended session is refused, except to staff looking at an event they do not compete in (`staffUser`; the picker offers it them, `viewer` in `renderTimeslotPicker`), so an admin in Participant View can read each ended session's challenges and board (a reported bug, 17 Nov 2026; lock: the ended describe of `tests/staff-compete-participant-ui.spec.js`).
- `leaderboard_mode` (`global`|`per_slot`) and `time_basis` lock once any session starts (re-checked inside the write); `time_basis` and `measure_presence` also lock once ended. Challenge assignments freeze once any session ends (see *Challenges*).
- **Session delete routes call `freezeGroupLeaderboardSnapshots` themselves**: deleting the last upcoming session can end the event, and no scheduler tick notices.
- **`bulk-delete` checks every id before deleting any** and returns its `updated_at`; the editor runs it before the PUT, patches that token in, and on a PUT failure names the deletion already done.
- Start and deadline inputs use `wallClockToUtcChecked` (refuse a DST-gap time, accept an ambiguous one); end-time helpers use `wallClockToUtc`. `#comp-tz` is never stored.

#### The competition PUT

- **`validate_only` (an explicit bool) dry-runs every pre-transaction refusal against the prospective session set** (`delete_timeslot_ids`); in-transaction re-checks can still refuse. New refusals go above its return and read `_slotsPut`.
- **`expectedUpdatedAt` makes a stale save 409.** Every lifecycle write bumps `updated_at` on its row and its parent.
- **A new flag** touches `COMPETITION_FIELD_SCHEMA`, the INSERT default, COALESCE at PUT, both audit events (`created` records the `edited` set), the hand-written admin-list SELECT (a missing column renders empty and the next save wipes it) and `_compWire`. Create defaults use `=== undefined ? policy default : (v ? 1 : 0)`; bind `event_kind` explicitly. Read `eventPolicy(comp)` only after the 404 check.
- **`_compWire` is a deny-list over `...rest`**: it strips `self_enroll_token`, `cxone_api_key`, `trusted_network_ips` (a venue address; only the admin list SELECTs it), `registration_field_policy` and every `_`-prefixed key, so a derived internal must start with `_`. `eventPolicyForWire` gets `comp`, not `rest`.
- **The form never sends an unrendered control** (`_optCheck` gives `undefined`; `saveCompetition` copies only `!== undefined`); a `false` would clear stored columns. The kind comes from the hidden `#comp-kind`, never a radio query.

#### Schedule, start-time and kind locks

- **`resolveSchedule()` decides the schedule columns at create and for a deadline kind on the PUT**; a window kind's PUT writes `duration_minutes` as validated. A Program's `deadline_at` is authoritative; its duration is derived (rounded up, may exceed the 525600 submitted cap) from the start being written (`_effStart`), re-derived only when deadline or start moved, or an echoed deadline would reopen a closed Program.
- **Refuse, never ignore**: `deadline_not_accepted` on a window kind; `deadline_too_far` past `DEADLINE_MAX_SPAN_MINUTES` (20 y, the API bound; the typo guard is the form's confirm past 2 y, never asked while "No fixed deadline" is ticked); `duration_not_accepted` when it disagrees with the derived one (echoes round-trip) or changes on a Program PUT.
- **`isOpenEnded` is derived, never stored**, over `eventDeadline()` (never for a closed event), deadline kinds only, from now against `OPEN_ENDED_THRESHOLD_DAYS`. "No fixed deadline" fills now + 10 y: `duration_minutes` is NOT NULL, and 525600 would close the Program on its anniversary. **The participant and admin wires carry it per read as `open_ended`** (`listParticipantCompetitions` rows, the `/api/status` body and so the pulse, `GET /api/admin/competitions` rows); the client never re-derives it, and while it holds every surface showing a Program's time left or end says "No fixed deadline" (`ui/format.js` `NO_FIXED_DEADLINE` / `eventEndText`, `features/competition-picker.js` `timeLeftWords`; the participant's event card says it once, on its date line, its countdown line empty while live (an upcoming one still counts down to its start); the header's day chip hides, the workspace time tile keeps its place with no bar), and every mail's `Ends:` line comes from `lib/competitions.js` `eventEndMailText` at send time (operator rulings, 14 Nov 2026; locks: `tests/open-ended-program.spec.js`, `tests/open-ended-dates.spec.js`, `tests/schedule-arithmetic-email.spec.js`). A new such surface or mail reads `open_ended` / `eventEndMailText` too. The event form shows the stored date on purpose: it is what was recorded; with the box ticked its save confirmations say "No fixed deadline (stored as <date>)" (`_noFixedDeadlineWords`, `features/competitions-admin.js`).
- **`startTimeChangeLock`**: a superadmin may move a live event's start only with no sessions and no footprint (fails closed without `isSuperAdmin`); moving it ahead (the only live→upcoming transition) reopens every started-lock. It also feeds DELETE (`delete_live_in_use`) and `start_time_locked`. A start change may not end the event (`start_time_would_end_event`); the footprint is re-checked inside the write.
- **`eventParticipantFootprint` probes each table in turn, not a head-count** (staff submit without membership rows; removals keep submissions); `competition_groups` is left out on purpose. A removed member who never entered leaves no absence row; one who entered leaves one on either clock (`recordRemovalAbsence`).
- **Kind change** is allowed only while upcoming with no sessions (see *The three event kinds — the policy table*): fixing a pristine live event takes two saves, start then kind.

#### Late entry (Booth Event)

- **The window extends play, not the event.** Never widen `competitionStatus`/`competitionEffectiveEnd` for it (they feed minting, `revealSolution` and the locks), nor the trusted-network end (`lib/comp-allowlist.js`; see *Security hardening highlights*), whose `SQL_SCHEDULED_END_AHEAD` stays SQL (`datetime()` truncates to seconds; `scheduledEnd()` would shift it up to 999 ms). "Late entry" is a display category, not a status; the client reads `late_entry_window_open`, never re-derives it.
- `lib/late-entry.js` is pure and takes the effective end as an argument. `late_entry_deadline_at` NULL = end + 48 h; a value = pinned. A late entrant joined (`assigned_at`) strictly AFTER the effective end; anyone who joined while the event was live is an ordinary participant (main board, podium, certificate). A one-way door, re-decided by operator ruling, 29 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026 (no 10-minute pre-window); certificates already issued keep their frozen `late_entrant`. Lateness is derived per read, frozen only onto the badge at mint, so **an event that ended before the installation took that rule keeps the pre-window** (`lateEntryJoinCutoff`, the floor `settings.late_entry_rule_floor_at` handed over at boot by `db.js`: the certificate floor on 9.1.1+, now from 8.14, the epoch on a fresh database; lock: `tests/late-entry-rule-floor.spec.js`): an upgrade never rewrites a final board.
- **Every admission door asks `competitionAdmission`** (`open`/`late`/null), except new-account invitation registration, which uses it only to mark a late admission. A late admission never revives or bumps `updated_at`. Anyone who took part before the end is refused a new row (`late_entry_took_part`); in the window only late entrants can be removed. Re-assigning an original member is a no-op (promotion would add a solo row to an unfrozen team board); a late entrant's held row (`lateEntryStateFor(...).isLate`, not a join-time test) is promoted (`membership_promoted`, `late_entry:true`). The two sign-in doors (a code bound to the event, the deferred join marker) refuse who took part and still sign in (lock: `tests/late-window-signin.api.spec.js`).
- **Admin rows compute `late_entry_window_open` server-side** (SELECT `event_kind`, `late_entry_*`, `ended_manually`; one `nowMs` per response). Participant rows keep `late_entrant` (removable) apart from `late_entry_playing`. The account delete's speed bump for a `late_entry_playing` member whom the window's removal refuses offers no removal and names that rule (`removable:false`, `removalRefused`: `not_late_entrant` or `late_entrant_has_certificate`).
- **Every re-add calls `closeAbsenceAndRestoreAnchor`**, restoring `assigned_at` and `login_time` from the `competition_user_absences` row the removal wrote; a NULL restores nothing. `select-timeslot` writing `login_time` unconditionally is not a hole: its first lock refuses anyone with both set on a live or ended slot.
- **Close late entries now** (`POST …/late-entry/close`) is offered and refused from the same `lateEntryCloseState`. It pins `max(end+1, now−1)` in one immediate transaction with no await (already closed: 200 `alreadyClosed`) and emits `late_entry_closed` (`closed_at`, `to` = pin, `from` = resolved deadline, `from_pinned`); render from `closed_at`, never `to`. Reopening is a superadmin editor save.
- `lateEntrySwitchLock` freezes `late_entry_enabled` once ended; its refusal names Close late entries now only while the window is open. The `late_entry_forbidden` sentence lives only in `lateEntryForbiddenMessage`. On the API, `""` resets the deadline to automatic; `null` changes nothing.
- **A pin is refused, never clamped** (`_validateLateDeadline`): after the end the save leaves, at most 30 days on (`LATE_ENTRY_MAX_SPAN_MS`). An exact echo of the stored pin on a save that neither moves the schedule nor switches late entries back on skips the cap only (`capExempt`): a re-tick wakes a pin the admin's own earlier save moved the end under. **A save that keeps a stored pin it does not send is judged too, cap included,** when it moves the schedule or switches late entries back on (while they end up on): 400 `late_entry_deadline_stale`, naming the pin and the remedy (in the app, a later deadline or Automatic in Edit settings; through the API, a new deadline or `""` with the change). The form always sends one while ticked; API clients and Start now meet it (its kept end rounds up to the minute, past a pin set through the API within it) (lock: `tests/late-entry-stored-pin.api.spec.js`; Start now: `tests/ui-events-groups-journeys.spec.js`).
- **The form echoes the stored pin** while the pair's text equals `utcToWallClock(pin, #comp-tz)` (text, not instants: minute rounding, fall-back hour); `_collectLateDeadline` gives `undefined` unrendered or unticked, `''` for Automatic. Automatic shows the server's `late_entry_deadline_effective` until the end moves from `S._compFormLateSeedEndMs`.

#### Clone event and event templates

- **One writer: `_materialiseEvent` (end of `routes/competitions.js`), shared by `POST …/:id/clone` and `POST /api/admin/event-templates/:id/spawn`.** Synchronous, one transaction; a new route making an event from a blueprint goes through it, never a third INSERT. The create route does not use it (its handler is source-pinned).
- **Copied:** every setting column the create form and PUT write, tags, a FRESH event-owned copy of each question (`copyForNewEvent`: the source event's own question as edited there, or the template's bank question; numbers NULL, assigned lazily when live; skip entries name the source's question; save-as-template stores each copy's bank origin while it is still in the bank), sessions spaced as they were, the first at the new start (offsets from the earliest session, never the row's start, which no session route keeps current), a pinned late-entry deadline as far from the new end as from the source's PLANNED end (dropped and reported when `_validateLateDeadline` refuses it), never a pin Close late entries now wrote (`late_entry_closed` in `forced_settings`: the copy follows its own end), the all-solved auto-end only for the same kind (else the kind seed; 0 where prohibited). The schedule copied is the PLAN: for a row ended by hand (End Now rewrites the duration and moves unstarted sessions to the end) `_plannedSchedule` replays its `created`/`edited` history to its latest `ended_manually`; a session still under a minute is left out (`skipped_sessions`). **Never:** participants, group bindings, submissions, hints, presence, badges, history, `trusted_network_ips`, `cxone_api_key` (and `cxone_group_name` when the source had its own key: the group would be looked up in another tenant), lifecycle columns. A source creating CxOne accounts with its own key gives a clone or saved template accounts OFF (`cxone_disabled`, in `forced_settings`; `_bpOwnKeyTurnsAccountsOff`), else they would land in the default tenant (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026). **A fresh `self_enroll_token` and `join_code` are always minted** (as at create), so a printed QR never admits anyone to the copy.
- **Always upcoming** (`start_time_not_future`; default now + 24 h), so no "locked once started" rule binds a copy.
- **Template settings are the create form's fields, validated by `COMPETITION_FIELD_SCHEMA` picks (`TEMPLATE_SETTINGS_SCHEMA`), unknown keys refused (`template_setting_unknown`).** Duration may run to the 20-year deadline span; window kinds are capped at 525600 in `_bpSchedule`. `_bpResolve` REFUSES what the kind forbids for a template (the create codes) and FORCES it off for a clone that changes kind (named in `forced_settings`). Settings are re-validated at every spawn (409 `template_invalid`).
- **Challenges are skipped, never failing the event, each with a reason (`_bpTaskSkip`):** `missing`, `not_visible` (`taskViewDenied`, asked first, reported with no id or title, which the template read withholds), `qualifying` (another event's own question: a clone, a template save and a spawn never share one; a clone of an event holding a BUILT-IN one, a `DEFAULT_QUALIFYING` key, gets all four fresh as the defaults word them (`addQualifying`; the source's rewording is not carried) and does not report those keys; one holding only organisers' own questions gets none and names them (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026; lock: `tests/clone-template-plan.api.spec.js`); explicit assignment may share one, never on a topic the event already asks: see *Event team and persona reports*), `not_published`, `locked` (non-superadmin), `unfit` (`challengeFitError` against the NEW kind), `unsealed` (staff-compete), `competing` (held by an event the actor, seat or hand, competes in; asked right after `not_visible`, the event named only when `competitionDenied` passes; the skipped entry's `message` is its `note`, words for the toast's brackets; operator ruling, 23 Nov 2026). A template save refuses all but `locked` (`template_task_<reason>`, 403 for `competing`; a PUT re-judges the whole stored list); the editor's picker never offers a qualifying question. A clone's toast names what it did not carry (`notCarriedSummary`: `forced_settings`, `skipped_sessions`).
- **Tiers.** Clone and spawn are `scoped`; an event admin using either is granted a `competition_admins` row on the new event (scope, me and participants caches busted after commit): the only doors through which that tier creates an event. A scoped organiser with no grants is refused a spawn (403 `event_admin_no_events`), as the clone's scope check refuses it. Template create/PUT/DELETE and save-as-template are held twice (`EVENT_ADMIN_FORBIDDEN` and `requireGlobalAdmin`). The template list/read withholds challenge ids the actor may not view. A competitor may neither clone the event they compete in nor save it as a template (one `_competitorRefused`, asked after the 404; operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026), nor carry such an event's challenges into a template or an event they make (`competing` above; the assign doors stay open on purpose), and the workspace ⋯ menu withholds both by the same `!competing` (locks: `tests/event-clone-templates.api.spec.js`, `tests/templates-editor-ui.spec.js`).
- **Built-ins** are defined once in `lib/event-template-builtins.js` (stable `builtin_key`, one per kind: each kind's recommended setup on the New event page), seeded once at the end of `db.js` behind `event_templates_seeded_at`, with no challenges. Each keeps the definition it replaced as `previous`: a one-shot `db.js` migration (`event_templates_renamed_at`) moves a stored built-in to the current one only while it equals `previous` exactly with no challenges (an admin's edit is kept; a new name another template holds, NOCASE, is skipped and logged), and the `builtin_key` backfill matches either name. A Booth spawn adds the qualifying questions through both create doors' kind-seeded box, never a template setting (the API spawn adds them only on `add_qualifying:true`). Template writes are audited as orphaned `event_template` events; the new event's `created` carries `via: clone|template` plus `cloned_from` / `template_id`. Locks: `tests/event-clone-templates.api.spec.js`; the refusals (`kind_conflict`, `template_source_invalid`, `template_invalid`), the `locked`/`unsealed` skips (clone, spawn, save-as-template) and a regular admin's template saves keeping a locked challenge, the dropped late pin, Restore's "(built-in)" name, the `builtin_key` backfill and the rename: `tests/clone-template-paths.api.spec.js`.
- **Protection is server-enforced (operator ruling, 30 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026; lock: `tests/event-template-protection.api.spec.js`).** `_tplProtection` (`routes/competitions.js`) is the one predicate: a built-in is never deletable (409 `template_protected`; `POST …/:id/reset` restores its built-in definition), nor a template a global admin marked `protected` (`POST …/:id/protect`) until unprotected; the wire's `can_delete`/`delete_refused` come from it, and the DELETE repeats the guard in its UPDATE's WHERE. Every other delete is SOFT (`deleted_at`): list, read, PUT and spawn see only live rows (`_liveTemplate`), `GET /api/admin/event-templates/deleted` (registered above `/:id`) lists them with `missing_builtins`, `POST …/:id/restore` brings one back, and `lib/event-retention.js` purges them `TEMPLATE_RESTORE_DAYS` (30) after deletion. A soft-deleted row keeps its name (the NOCASE unique index), so `_tplNameClash` says to restore it. `POST /api/admin/event-templates/restore-builtins` recreates a built-in missing by `builtin_key`. All five new routes are global-admin only and held twice.

#### Self-enrollment links

- **`self_enroll_token` is a secret.** Only the admin reveal (`GET …/self-enroll`), regenerate and the join-code lookup ship it; `_REDACT_KEYS` and `_scrubLogPath` cover it. `join_code` resolves to the token, so it is a capability (redacted).
- **The token preview (`GET /api/competitions/self-enroll/:token`) answers a uniform 404; the join-code lookup (`GET /api/competitions/join-code/:code`) 200 for every outcome** (`d.ok`; only its 429 differs), as mistyped codes from one venue NAT would trip the scanner block.
- **Regenerate rotates token and code in one write**, no await after the code uniqueness check; `uq_competitions_join_code` stays below its ALTER.
- **A link does nothing until `self_enroll_enabled` is on.** No self-enrolment cap, by operator ruling (lock: `tests/event-kind-form-scope.spec.js`).
- **Switching a link off (the event PUT) or replacing it (Regenerate) unties every live code and address proof asked for through it** (operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026; `lib/self-enroll.js` `untieJoinLinkBindings`, in that write's transaction, counted as `join_link_untied` on its record): redeemed, they sign in or make an account without joining, even once the link is back on (the live link itself still joins: the form's `joinToken`, the app's join after sign-in). Session state, beyond that write, is re-checked: the `codeRequests` note's `asked` entries and the sign-up grant's `joinLinks` carry the `joinLinkTag` (a token fingerprint) of the link asked through, and the event counts only while `liveJoinLinkTag` still equals it (switched off and on is the same link; no tag fails closed). A dead body `joinToken` this browser asked through counts as none, dropped before the register's live-invitation lookup, never the invalid-link 409; so does a grant whose link closed (or event stopped taking joins) after the code was entered (`grantComp`): a live form token fills it, else a live invitation to the address, else approval holds it; the answer carries `join_link_closed` (lock: `tests/join-link-untie.api.spec.js`). The pending join marker is not untied.
- **Join is idempotent and does not select the event**; register-via-join enrols at first sign-in (`pending_self_enroll_comp_id`). **Leave** is for self-enrolled rows only, refused once ended, sends no email, and clears the request's own session event only if it is this one (not in `removeUserFromCompetition`).
- **Pending join** (`localStorage["hck-pending-join"]`, `exp` = preview `effective_end`) is purged on success, the preview's 404, terminal refusals, "Forget this code" / "Not your event?" or `exp` + 30 days; never on clock expiry or 429/5xx. `clearStoredJoin(token)` removes only a matching token. A rehydrated token auto-joins only a `participant` (a clicked one, `S._joinFromUrl`, any role); no rehydration after a join-URL or invitation load.
- **An anonymous `/join` visitor always lands on the email entry screen with the event on the card**, even when the preview fails (`S._pendingJoinToken || S._arrivedViaJoin`); the event rides the code request. The card's status chip reads the preview's `status` and `late_entry_window_open` through `compDisplayCategory` (an ended Booth Event in its window reads "Late entry open"; lock: `tests/late-entries-ui.spec.js`). Typed codes use `_resolveJoinCode` → `joinViaPendingToken`; a resolved join never hides `#comp-join-code-box`.
- **`GET …/delivery`** (404, then scope 403): `enrolled`, `verified`, `email_failed` (people, not messages); the panel shows errors as errors, never zeros, at any status.

#### Entering, resuming and the clock

- **`autoResumeAllows` checks policy only, never status**, shared by both marker writers and `applyAutoResumeToSession` (sign-in doors; `toggle-admin-view` with no event, not impersonating), which SELECTs the marker itself, sets `timeslot_id`, admits staff without a row, else falls to `_soleCompetitionLanding` (own rows, `staff:false`; no timeslot; never in `boot()`). Only deletion, removal or `deselect-competition` (stamps `left_event_at`; fire-and-forget only from `showCompetitionsPage`) stops a resume.
- **`listParticipantCompetitions` is the one list builder** (`/api/me`, `/api/my-competitions`, login), batching timeslots in one `IN (…)`; `withTags`/`withTimeslots` only shape the wire. A second builder brings back N+1 at login.
- **Removal keeps submissions and hints and clears `active_competition_id`**; keep the membership check on resume too.
- **The first `login_time` = `anchorLoginTime`** (earlier of now and earliest activity), written by `select-competition`/`select-timeslot` on a live entity, else lazily by `_stampLoginTimeIfLive`; never under impersonation. **On a first-open clock none of those write it** (see *Solve clock*).
- **Start reminders**: one per participant, 1 h before start, refused where `startReminder` is `"forbidden"` and under a minute before the start (`start_reminder_too_late`, `START_REMINDER_MIN_LEAD_MS`: the 30 s tick plus margin, so normally claimed in time). Moving the start re-times them; a kind forbidding them purges them. Held while the instance is out of service (*Maintenance mode*); never mailed once its event or session has started (operator ruling, 25 Nov 2026; *Wording at send time*).
- **Presence** is a signal, not proof, and under-counts by design (see *Presence needs corroboration*).

### Challenges

#### Event-owned questions

- **A question added to an event lives in that event** (owner decision, Nov 2026; `lib/event-copies.js`, pure, takes `db`). `tasks.owner_competition_id` NULL is a BANK question (the library), else the event owning it; `tasks.copied_from_task_id` is the bank question a copy came from (no FK on either: an origin must outlive its bank row). Every assign door (single, bulk, the desired-state PUT, the perf seed) asks `eventTaskFor` and inserts the EVENT'S OWN copy (made now with every column read from the schema, tags, active attachment rows naming the same file, a version of change_type `copy`; or the copy an earlier add made); another event's question is refused (`task_owned_by_other_event`, bulk `skipped.ownedElsewhere`). Clone and spawn use `copyForNewEvent` (always fresh). `POST /api/admin/tasks` with `competitionId` creates a question owned by that event from birth. Bank edits never reach a copy; a copy's edit, freeze, lock and grants are its one event's. **Qualifying questions keep their own sharing rule (N7) and are assigned as they are**, never copied.
- **A bank question is "in" every event holding its assigned copy**: `eventsHolding` (the desired-state PUT's current set), the admin list's `competitions[]` (each such entry carries `copy_id`), DELETE `…/competitions/:cid` and attempt-grant resolve a bank id to the event's copy (`assignedEventTaskId`), and the competitor lock is by LINEAGE (`competitorEventsForTask`: the question, its bank question and every copy of that). The participant's challenge routes resolve a bank id named in the session's event to its copy (`resolveEventTaskParam`), so a tab opened before the migration keeps working (the grading hash names no id).
- **Lists:** the library (the Question bank outside a workspace, the template editor's picker, the tasks export without `?competitionId=`, the import's title conflicts) is bank questions only; a workspace shows its own questions plus the bank questions it holds no copy of (`ui/task-search.js` `bankTasks`/`workspaceTasks`/`pickerTasks`, the one client test).
- **A file two rows share:** a copy's attachment rows name the bank row's file (`INSERT ... SELECT`, the shape the backup scanner accepts without a generator); the task DELETE and the event DELETE unlink a file only when no row still names it. A participant's download naming a BANK question's attachment id in an event holding its copy is served the copy's row for that file (`resolveEventTaskParam` on `taskId`, then by storage path; lock: `tests/event-owned-questions-lifecycle.api.spec.js`).
- **Re-adding reuses the copy, and the copy is judged:** `eventTaskTarget` (the row `eventTaskFor` would put in, read only) feeds every assign door's per-pair refusals (`_assignTarget`/`_assignTargetStateRefusal`, `routes/challenges.js`: draft, lock, fit, seal, qualifying key), as an existing copy may have been edited since it was taken out; the bank row's own draft and lock gates still apply first.
- **Deleting an event deletes the questions it owns** (`removeEventOwnedQuestions`, in the event DELETE's transaction after its answers and analytics; tags, versions, ratings, grants and attachment rows by cascade; unshared files unlinked after the commit). One an event template names, or one another event's row still names, is RELEASED into the library (`owner_competition_id` NULL) instead. Counted in the orphaned `deleted` record's `cascaded.owned_questions` (`owned_questions_released`).
- **The batch migration** (`migrateSharedTasksToEventCopies`, end of `db.js`, marker `event_owned_questions_migrated_at`, ended events included, one transaction per event so an interrupted boot resumes) copies every (event, bank question) pair the event assigns or holds rows for and MOVES the event's rows per `TASK_TABLE_MANIFEST` (moved: `competition_tasks`, `submissions`, `hint_reveals`, `challenge_events`, `attempt_grants`; copied: `task_tags`, `task_attachments`; stays: `task_versions`, `task_feedback`, `event_templates.task_ids`). A new table with a `task_id` gets a decision there (lock: `tests/event-owned-questions.api.spec.js`, both ways). Preview it read-only before an upgrade: `DATA_DIR=… node scripts/event-copies-dry-run.js [--json]` (a script, not a route: the batch runs before any route answers). Test hooks: `POST /api/test/legacy-shared-assign {on}` (the previous model, to build an old database through the real doors; `tests/event-admin-helpers.js` `withLegacySharedAssign`) and `POST /api/test/event-copies-migrate {dryRun}`.

#### The Question bank: topics, the tags and Save to bank

- **The cross-event page is the Question bank** (sidebar label and pane title; inside a workspace the same pane reads **Questions**, with **+ Add from bank** and **+ New question**). Copy only: `admin-tasks-tab`, routes, ids and stored values keep their names, and participants still see **Challenges** (owner request, stage B).
- **A question names at most one topic** (`tasks.topic_id`, table `topics`, `lib/topics.js`; routes `routes/topics.js`): names unique NOCASE (`uq_topics_name_nocase`), 1–60 characters, no control characters. Topic names are platform-wide (any `challenges.edit` holder makes and files under any); rename and delete are a global admin's or the topic's creator's (`topic_not_yours`), and a held topic is deleted only after a global admin moves its questions (`move_to`, `can_move`); a scoped creator deletes only an empty one. The task create/PUT take `topic_id` (400 `topic_not_found`), History records it by name, a version restore never writes it, an event's copy takes its bank question's topic (`copyTaskIntoEvent` copies every column), the export carries `topic` by NAME and the import resolves or makes it (`server.js` `_applyImportedTopic`). Audited as orphaned `topic` events (`created`/`edited`/`deleted`). Locks: `tests/topics.api.spec.js`, `tests/topics-ui.spec.js`.
- **The one-shot migration** (end of `db.js`, marker `topics_from_categories_at`) files each question with no topic under its FIRST `category:` tag, one topic per name. No stored tag is deleted.
- **Free-form tags are neither shown nor edited** (owner request): no Tags column, no tag box, no tag dialog for questions. The reserved markers are controls in the editor's **Topic and sharing** row: **Shared with all organisers** (`public-domain`; bank questions only, an event's own question gets a note instead; a stored near miss is named beside it and removed when it is turned on) and **This question needs** (`needs:<what>`, the client's `KNOWN_NEEDS` beside unknown stored ones). They are written after the save through the existing tag routes, so every tag-route rule (owner, seal, fit, near miss, qualifying) still decides and a refusal is toasted. The tag routes stay (tests and the API use them); the template editor's picker shows no tag chips: it filters by **Topic** with the bank's own filter (`topics-admin.js` `paintTopicFilter` / `taskInTopic`, one definition; owner decision, 27 Nov 2026; lock: `tests/templates-editor-ui.spec.js`), its search box still matching a title, topic or tag (`taskMatchesQuery`).
- **Save to bank** (`POST /api/admin/tasks/:id/save-to-bank`, `lib/event-copies.js` `copyTaskToBank`) copies an event's own question into the bank as a NEW bank question: no owner, no origin, never a link or an overwrite; tags (never `qualifying:`), attachment rows naming the same files, unlocked; `created_by_id` the saver, or the source's author when sealed. Order: 404, `save_to_bank_not_event`, competitor lock, `taskViewDenied`, `sealedEditDenied`, `save_to_bank_qualifying`. Recorded as the new question's `created` version, `via: save_to_bank`, naming `saved_from` and the event. Lock: `tests/save-to-bank.api.spec.js`.

#### Answer types

- **`answer_type` is `text` | `json` | `file` | `choice` | `any`; the enum lives in five places.** `VALID_ANSWER_TYPES` (`lib/scoring.js`), route guards' error text, swagger, `#tf-atype` (`public/index.html`) and the `tasks` `CHECK(answer_type IN …)` in `db.js`, which no JS grep finds and SQLite cannot ALTER (a new type needs a table rebuild). Every renderer must learn it too: participant detail, `viewTaskAsParticipant` (separate code), the editor and `formatCorrectAnswer` (`public/js/ui/format.js`, the one display mapping, scanner-locked).
- **Grading.** `text`: any line of a newline-separated accepted list, case- and whitespace-blind. `file`: one string, same fold, never a list. `json`: deep equality. `choice`: the id set. `any`: any non-empty answer.
- **The PUT canonicalises incoming AND stored text lists (`canonicaliseTextAnswers`) and binds `undefined` when equal, before `_scoringEditAttempted`**, or a title-only save counts as a scoring edit.
- **Empty `correct_answer` is 400 for every type but `any`, checked after choice resolution**: COALESCE treats `''` as a value and whitespace-blind grading would accept an empty submission.
- **`any` stores `correct_answer = ""` (NOT NULL), so blank-key checks use `answerTypeHasKey`, never `=== "any"`.** Submit 400s an empty `any` answer before grading (no row, penalty or attempt).
- **`any` never has a hint.** Writers test `richTextHasContent` (`lib/sanitise.js`), not truthiness (the sanitiser keeps `<p></p>`); a switch to `any` clears it with a `CASE` write, since COALESCE cannot write NULL.
- **An `any` challenge with two or more `choices` is a PICK-ONE question (`isSurveyTask`, `validateSurveyChoices`, `lib/choices.js`): exactly one option id, every option correct, any other answer refused 400 before grading.** No new answer type (no `tasks` rebuild); `correct_answer` stays `""`. The answer is stored like a choice answer (`answer` text, `answer_canonical` ids), so dedup reads the ids; Recalculate grades it as `any`, so no option edit (add, remove, reword) turns an earlier answer wrong (a QUALIFYING one's options keep their places once answered, and its answer can be changed until the end: see *Event team and persona reports*). Options keep the author's order (often ordered buckets), never shuffled. Participant wire `pick_one`, client mode `pickone` (no penalty modal). `choiceTapApplies` takes it where `choice_layout` is `tap`: one tap on a tile answers, no confirm, since no option can be wrong (`tests/event-team-ui.spec.js`). A solved view gets the participant's pick as `my_choice_ids`, marked `.is-picked` in a neutral colour, never `.is-correct`, as when answering (`_markCorrectInCard`). An `any` answer, pick-one or not, is announced "Answer accepted", never "Correct" (`_announceSolve`, `solvedPanelHtml`), and no card draws an attempts line where no answer can be wrong (`ui/format.js` `answerCanBeWrong`, by answer type alone: a quick question of another type is graded and spends attempts).
- **Allowlists are the only guard on unCHECKed enum columns; PUT checks `active` is boolean/0/1** (TEXT reads inactive in SQL, active in JS). Lengths come from `TASK_FIELD_CAPS` (`lib/task-fields.js`) for create, PUT and import.
- **`taskGradingHash(answerType, correctAnswer, choices)` is a keyed HMAC, derived not stored, and REQUIRED on submit** (missing or stale: 409 `task_changed`, no penalty); unkeyed it is an offline oracle for the key. `_gradingKey` (`lib/scoring.js`) creates `.grading-secret` with `wx`, atomically replaces one under 16 bytes or unreadable, and adopts a racing peer's only if long enough: a short key is a known key.
- **Submit dedup (3 s, `server.js`) matches `COALESCE(answer_canonical, answer)` against `answerCanonical ?? answer`** (a changeable Quick question is compared with the latest answer instead: see *Event team and persona reports*). `answer_canonical` alone stops it for text/json/file (NULL); `answer` alone folds an option named "A | B" into picking A and B.
- **The file submit re-runs its checks (assignment, row, lock, type, hash) after `await readFile` and grades the fresh row.** Keep both passes: the first avoids reading an ungradable file; the await lets an admin lock or edit meanwhile.
- **Lifecycle writers (publish, visibility, restore, lock) bump `tasks.updated_at`**, or a stale form's `expectedUpdatedAt` skips its 409.

#### Multiple-choice challenges

- **`tasks.choices` is JSON `[{id,text}]` in author order; `correct_answer` is the JSON id array.** `validateChoices` (`lib/choices.js`): 2..`MAX_CHOICES`, unique text, at least one right and one wrong.
- **Identity, never position.** Ids are minted once and never change; import keeps them verbatim and refuses a bad row (`storedChoiceRowError`). `shuffleChoicesFor(choices, userId, taskId)` is deterministic and per participant ("the third one" is not shareable).
- **A choice answer is one or more of THIS challenge's option ids and nothing else; anything else is refused 400 before grading, like an empty `any` answer.** `parseSubmittedIds` yields `[]` for an array holding a non-string. Recalculate under `any` reads an older row whose stored selection is an empty list as empty (`storedChoicePickedNothing`, `lib/choices.js`); its "(nothing selected)" text proves nothing alone, as an option may carry it (residual: see *Known open gaps*).
