- **All-or-nothing on the set (`gradeChoice`); no partial credit**: auto-end, leaderboard dedup and solve rate need a boolean `is_correct`.
- **The grading hash covers `choices`**: editing an option changes the question, not the ids. Editing option text or adding a wrong option is deliberately not frozen: no grade can move.
- **Grade from `submissions.answer_canonical` (id array), never `answer` (display text).** Recalculate re-derives older rows by option text (`lib/choices.js` `legacySelectionIds`) and leaves ambiguous ones alone (`unresolved`).
- **The PUT derives `correct_answer` from `choices` before the freeze check** (the freeze tests what will be stored) and validates before attachments leave staging.
- **Correct ids reach a participant only after a full end with `reveal_answers_on_end`, or as a solver while one of their correct rows still grades correct against the CURRENT key.** A key change on a live challenge re-grades nothing, so a solve it no longer matches gets `answer_changed_since_solve`, not the new key. For a sealed challenge, see *The sealed answer*.
- **The tap layout (`public/js/features/choice-tap.js`) is presentation only** (same ids, `submitAnswer`, hash, modal). `choiceTapApplies` keys on the policy's `choice_layout` (`tap`) plus `CHOICE_TAP_MQ`, never on kind. It MOVES the countdown and chips (writers find them by id), restoring them on every exit; re-layout waits out an open modal or in-flight answer. `_tapPending` is a per-task `Set`, and the submit `finally` touches tiles only while the reused card still shows that challenge: one flag lets A's answer swallow B's taps.
- **The device query is pinned by fixed tables, never by reading `CHOICE_TAP_MQ`**: the size/input matrix in `tests/booth-choice-tap.spec.js` and `BOOTH_TAP_PROJECTS` in `tests/cross-device.spec.js`. A new Playwright project gets the standard card until a decision is recorded there.

#### The sealed answer

- **`lib/answer-seal.js` is pure and the only judge**: ask its predicates, never test `tasks.answer_sealed`; facts are passed in because `lib/scoring` reaches `lib/config`, which exits without `BASE_URL`. The column's ALTER sits below both `tasks` rebuilds and outside try/catch: a swallowed ALTER unseals everything.
- **`SEALED_FIELDS` = `correct_answer`, `explanation`, `hint`.** The redactors iterate it; the tasks export and participant surface name fields by hand and must follow it.
- **Authorship is `tasks.created_by_id`, and the seal binds every tier**, so it is not in `taskEditDenied` (which exempts global admins). Sealing at create needs an author and no proxy (`sealAtCreateDenied`); imported and older rows have none.
- **One-way door:** `0 -> 1` after create is refused for everyone (the key has been on the wire, in versions and logs); `1 -> 0` is the author's or a revealed superadmin's; an echo is not a change.
- **No seal on a keyless type** (`sealTypeDenied`), checked at create, PUT (stored flag + incoming type), restore (current flag + snapshot type) and the task import's overwrite (stored flag + file type; skipped and counted, `sealed_type_skipped`: the overwrite never writes `answer_sealed`).
- **`sealedEditDenied` guards PUT, restore, recalculate (`flipped` is an oracle), publish, visibility and tags**: above the tier gate in the last three, just after it in PUT and restore. Assign, grants, History, lock and delete are not gated: a sealed challenge must stay removable without its author.
- **Redact on the server, per row.** `GET /api/admin/tasks` carries `can_see_answer`; the client must not re-derive it (`S` is on `window`). Snapshots are redacted at READ, never at write (restore needs them whole). The submissions and audit `?q=` searches apply the seal CASE (a match count is an oracle), both from `submissionAnswerProjection(req)` (`lib/answer-seal.js`, lazily requiring its facts), never a copy. Submit omits, not zeroes, `answer_length` in a sealed challenge's `challenge_events` details. The editor omits withheld fields from the body, or COALESCE wipes them.
- **The superadmin's reveal is recorded before it is shown.** `POST /api/admin/tasks/:id/sealed-reveal` checks `actingIsSuperAdmin(req)` (the human's live role, never the session flag) after 404, competitor lock, scope and the author no-op. `recordSealedReveal` (`.immediate()`) runs BEFORE the `sealedReveals` session marker (a session saves even on a 500); every session regenerate drops the marker.
- **The participant task GET (`routes/participant.js`) keeps a sealed challenge out of the end-of-event and solver reveal** (answer, explanation, free hint). Only the author (asked of `actingAdminId`) and a superadmin are exempt; for a non-competing superadmin the open is the reveal, so GET and hint POST record first. A failed record withholds with reason `unrecorded` (GET, 200) or 503s the hint, charging nothing; `unrecorded` is deliberately not in `SEALED_HINT_WITHHELD_REASONS`.
- **The record is a `task_versions` `sealed_reveal` row whose `details` (`sealRevealHistoryDetails`, `lib/audit.js`) stores the impersonated seat as an id (`acting_as_id`), never an email, IP or agent** (retention-exempt; `_revealSeatFor` resolves the seat per reader). It is not a version: restore refuses it and History skips it as a diff baseline.
- **A reveal never widens a competitor.** Competing is asked of the hand (`competitorEventsForTask(db, actingAdminId(req), …)`); a paid sealed hint is readable only by a scoring participant (`userScoresOn`), never under impersonation. Competitors get `hint_withheld` from every admin projection and the export; it is the one marker editor and preview key on, and `competing_in` tells a competitor withhold from a seal withhold.

#### Attempt caps

- **Caps are `tasks.attempt_cap` and `competitions.attempt_cap`, never `competition_tasks`** (assignment is DELETE-then-INSERT); grants live in `attempt_grants`. `lib/attempts.js` owns every derivation; do not recount at call sites.
- **Resolve `task ?? comp`, never `||`; 1..1000; NULL is the only "no cap".** `resolveAttemptCap` throws on a row lacking the key. Cleared cap boxes send `null` (absent: unchanged).
- **An attempt is a stored submission row**: dedup folds and 429s spend nothing, hints never do.
- **Exhausted submit is 200 `refused:'attempts_exhausted'`, never 4xx** (scanner reputation); the client checks `d.refused` before `!d.correct`. Exhaustion refuses an unbought hint, never a bought one.
- **`submitAnswer` toasts the refusal and a last wrong attempt**, since `openTask(taskId)` re-renders the card and wipes `#submit-result` (re-fetched: a grant may land mid-flight). A rejected fetch or unparseable body claims neither outcome, avoids the wrong-answer class and keeps the card, so the typed answer survives.
- **`attempts` rides every submit response via `attemptsWire` (`server.js`), auto-end paths included, and the challenge GET projects the same `attemptState` by hand**; cap keys are absent when uncapped, and on the challenge GET for a changeable Quick question (no cap binds it); raw `tasks.attempt_cap` is stripped. **The list (GET /api/tasks) carries only the boolean `out_of_attempts`** (unsolved, every attempt spent, cap binding: `outOfAttemptsTaskIds`, one `_standing` with `attemptState`), never a cap or a count; Next challenge and the strip's position pass over it (lock: `tests/next-challenge-out-of-attempts.spec.js`, each row checked against the challenge's own read).
- **Grants work on an ENDED event without clearing `ended_at`** (grant, then revive: the recovery after an all-finished auto-end); the response flags `competition_ended`. Grants accumulate; uncapped is 409 `not_capped`.
- **Never hide Grant Attempts on a guess.** `_taskMayHaveCap` (`challenges-admin.js`) keeps ended events and reads an unloaded competitions model, or an event missing from it, as an unknown cap, never uncapped; `openAttemptGrant` fetches the events and decides.

#### Freeze, lock and Booth fit

- **Ended-session freeze: call `taskFrozenByEndedCompetition(taskId)` (`lib/competitions.js`), never a new predicate.** Any ended slot freezes the scoring fields: PUT refuses `correct_answer`/`answer_type`/`difficulty`/`active` changes; restore also `status` and `choices`; publish, visibility, delete and (un)assignment refuse (all 409); import skips. `active`/`status` reorder final ranks without moving scores. Refusals append `FROZEN_REMEDY`; check a named workaround is not the same bypass.
- **Recalculate (superadmin) skips frozen competitions (`frozen_skipped`), never refuses.** A live session needs a second confirm (`live_session_confirm`); the strand report covers only rows this run flipped.
- **Lock (`tasks.locked`, superadmin toggle) blocks PUT, restore, recalculate, attachments and a `qualifying:` tag change that flips the challenge (it re-prices stored answers) for everyone; publish, visibility and assignment for non-superadmins; submit and hint (423) for all.** Publish, visibility, restore and recalculate re-assert it inside their UPDATE (TOCTOU close); the task PUT and assignment routes check only before writing.
- **`lib/challenge-fit.js` owns what a Booth Event may hold**: no `file` answer, no `needs:*` tag, via policy keys (see *The three event kinds — the policy table*). `needs:` is negative (any value restricts; `KNOWN_NEEDS` is wording only) and never participant-visible.
- **Fit is checked on the prospective state at every door**: assign, bulk (skips `unfit`), PUT, tag add, restore, import overwrite, kind change. Ended events are exempt; the participant list is never fit-filtered.

#### The editor layout

- **The compact editor (`#admin-task-form.ce-form`) is presentation only; every value still lives in its old element.** `#tf-diff` is a hidden `<select>` (what the save path reads and the scoring lock disables); the segmented radios mirror it via `_tfDiffSync()`, called LAST in `openTaskForm`/`editTask`, after every enable/disable pass. Hint, Explanation and Author rows collapse on every open (`_tfSecReset`); a spec typing into one opens its `[aria-controls="tf-…-body"]` toggle first. `#tf-hint-group` is the whole hint row, so its visibility still means "hint offered". The seal's long note `#tf-seal-note` sits behind `#tf-seal-tip` and opens by itself only for the proxy refusal. The rich-text toolbar keeps strikethrough, colours, font, size and clear formatting in `.rt-more` behind `.rt-more-btn` (lock: `tests/challenge-editor-compact.spec.js`, geometry at 1440×900 and 390×844).
- **A rich body goes read-only only through its editor's `setReadOnly(reason, {also})` (`ui/richtext.js`), never a `contentEditable` write elsewhere:** it disables every toolbar control at full strength (`--muted` glyphs, dashed edges), closes open menus and states the reason under the bar as each control's description. The challenge form's reasons (`_tfLockedReason`, `_tfSealedReason`) follow the predicate of the door they name, and a challenge with `can_edit` false opens read-only, no Save; a locked challenge, a competitor's and an organiser's on a challenge not theirs offer no attachment write (no Add, a chip keeps Download only: `challenges-admin.js` `_tfAttachReadOnly`, exactly where the attachment routes refuse; a seal alone never does) (scanner: functional-core-b Docs hygiene; lock: `tests/richtext-readonly-toolbar.spec.js`).

#### Versions and restore

- **The restore UPDATE's hand-kept column list is locked by `tests/tasks-drift.spec.js`**: every scoring column incl. `choices`, never `answer_sealed` (a pre-seal snapshot would unseal) or `created_by_id`. An incoherent choice snapshot is 409.

#### Assigning challenges

- **`PUT /api/admin/tasks/:id/competitions` takes a desired set plus `expectedAssignments`**, narrowed to the actor's scope for both diff and concurrency check, with `competitionDenied` on every id first.
- **ONE door puts bank questions into an event, and ONE into events from the bank** (owner ruling, 27 Nov 2026): inside an event, its Questions section's **+ Add from bank** (`openAddTasksToCompetition`, the picker; the Overview checklist's **Add from bank**, `ws-add-from-bank`, opens the section and that same door); on the Question bank, the selection bar's **Add to events** (`challengesBulkAssign`: one ticked question opens its own dialog, `openAssignTaskToComps`, which also takes it out of events; several, the bulk one). The workspace ⋯ menu has no such door, bank rows have no **Assign to Events** item, there is no top bulk button, and rows inside an event carry no checkbox. The Events-list row's **Assign Challenges** is still there (that menu is another change's; `tests/menu-door-parity.spec.js` holds it out of the ⋯ menu). Lock: `tests/question-doors.spec.js`.
- **The Assign Challenges dialog is a set operation behind one confirmation.** Baseline is assigned ∩ listed (published only), unrendered assignments are preserved, adds run before removals, and rows the actor cannot unassign show ticked and disabled. `assignPickedTasks` runs bounded concurrency over the per-task routes, never the bulk endpoint: only they name the refused challenge.
- **Bulk `POST /api/admin/tasks/assign-competitions` is best-effort**: each refused pair counts in `skipped`, never a 400, with the single-assign invariants (per-event `task_added` audit `via:"bulk"`, renumbering, cache busts). Its toast renders every skip key plus `unfitReasons`; a new per-task refusal needs a ledger key and a toast consumer, or an all-skipped batch reads "0 assigned".
- **An event workspace never filters its Challenges section.** `_loadAdminTaskList` sends no competitionId; the event's own challenges sort first, the rest of the bank dimmed below them (where "+ Add from bank" picks from), and the empty state's `hasFilter` ignores the open event (lock: `tests/challenge-empty-state.spec.js`). Its Participants section DOES narrow, client-side, to the event's members until "Search everyone on the platform" (`#p-ws-everyone`, `S.pWsShowEveryone`; for a role scoped to its events, "Show people from your other events", matching its list: `wsEveryoneLabel`) is ticked; each non-member row then offers Add to event, and counts follow what is shown. The table defaults to Participant, Company, Status, Score, Last active, Actions (every cell carries `data-pcol`; Score only inside an event and with `leaderboard.view`, else the Columns menu says why: `forceHiddenNote`), the rest behind Columns (localStorage `ha:p-cols`); specs acting in other cells call `showAllParticipantColumns` (`tests/ui-admin-helpers.js`) before the first goto.
- **The bank row stays quiet:** its topic (never its tags) and at most `EVENT_CELL_SHOWN` event chips (the open event first), the rest behind a "+N" that expands the cell in place (`toggleCellChips`, `features/challenges-admin.js`); nothing is hidden for good, and tag semantics (`public-domain`, `needs:*`, `test/dry run`) stay server-side.

#### Categories, rich text, attachments

- **A participant's `categories` is the question's topic name, else its legacy `category:<name>` tags** (`lib/topics.js` `participantCategories`, over `lib/categories.js`), parsed server-side only; participants never get `tags`.
- **Rich-text tables pass three allowlists** (`_RICH_TEXT_OPTS` in `lib/sanitise.js`, `renderRich`/`normalisePastedHtml` in `public/js/ui/richtext.js`), all spreading `RICH_TABLE_TAGS`. sanitize-html checks attribute names, not values: `_transformCell` clamps spans to `_MAX_CELL_SPAN` and must return `th` `scope` itself. Cell styles use `_RICH_CELL_STYLES`, widths percent-only (Word's pt widths overflow phones); never admit `url()` (beacons) or `position` (overlays). **Every tag `allowedAttributes` gives a `style` has its own `allowedStyles` rule, and the empty `"*"` rule keeps nothing on one without:** sanitize-html filters a style only by the tag's rule or `"*"`, and with neither stores it as written (lock: `tests/richtext-colours.spec.js`); a paste mirrors each rule in `RICH_STYLE_RULES`. A table section (`thead`/`tbody`/`tfoot`) takes `_RICH_SECTION_STYLES` (tint, alignment); older content may hold a section style stored unfiltered, so `renderRich` keeps no other property on one either.
- **`normalisePastedHtml` is UX; `lib/sanitise.js` is the boundary.** It regex-strips comments, `<?xml?>` and `o:/w:/m:/v:` tags before parsing (comments survive a node walk) and unwraps unsupported elements (deleting loses text). It keeps on each tag exactly the server's style rule (`RICH_STYLE_RULES`; none on p, div or li), reading a style as postcss does: CSS comments dropped, a `;` inside quotes no separator, the property name matched exactly (never lower-cased: the server's match is case-sensitive), never through CSSOM (which expands Word's `background` shorthand). A span's bold, italic, underline or strikethrough becomes b/i/u/s, and a b/strong saying it is not bold (Google Docs' `<b style="font-weight:normal">`, which a save stored bold) is unwrapped; its only narrowings beyond the server are Word's junk values, a dropped `!important` and literal colours mapped to names (next bullet).
- **A pick from the editor's Text colour or Highlight menu stores a NAME as the custom property that draws it, never a hex** (`color:var(--rt-red)`, `background-color:var(--rt-hl-yellow)`): no literal reads on both a day and a night surface. Each look × mode gives every name an ink and a tint, measured at AA by the generator (`scripts/theme/looks.js` `rtInk`/`rtHighlight`). **Never a class or data attribute drawn by a rule:** the browser's editing (joining lines, a paste at a line's end) re-serialises moved content with its computed colour inlined and attributes dropped; an inline var() survives. The sanitiser admits a literal or exactly a listed name's var() for that property (`RICH_COLOURS`), every other var() dropped; older literals stay (operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026). A copy or drag inside the editor carries computed literals, so the paste normaliser and a drop turn an exact match of this look's ink or tint back into its name; a paste turns any other literal into the nearest name or none (`_rtNearestName`, OKLCH: a neutral (C < 0.04) is grey unless L > 0.9, dropped, and L < 0.3 with C < 0.08 is dropped, so text follows the theme; any other colour takes the name whose anchor hue is nearest, from ONE fixed table beside `RICH_COLOURS`, `RICH_COLOUR_HUES` (never the look's inks, which differ by mode: the same paste must store the same name by day and at night; every look × mode's ink of a name and a CSS keyword for it fall nearest their own anchor, lock: `tests/richtext-colours.spec.js`)), so no pasted colour is unreadable in the other mode; a drop maps only an exact match (it scans the whole editor, older literals included). Picks are custom DOM work (`setRichColour`, splitting carriers at the selection's ends, never through a block), as execCommand writes literals, so the browser's history never sees one: **each pick, and every other custom DOM edit (Inline Code, Code Block, Insert Link with Update and Remove Link, the Table menu's row, column and Delete table items, a code language), is an undo step of the editor's own** (`_rtStep`; `_rtEdit` for the others; every other toolbar command, Insert table… included, is execCommand, the browser's step; a new one joins `RT_OPS`, scanner `tests/richtext-undo.spec.js`): its mutations are recorded and reversed node for node (never re-parsed from HTML, which would detach the text the browser's own steps point at), one stack, Ctrl+Z/Cmd+Z and the browser's Undo take it first, Ctrl+Shift+Z/Cmd+Shift+Z/Ctrl+Y put it back, and any change it did not make (typing, a paste, Bold, a list, a font or size) ends the editor's steps (`_rtWatch`); `_rtRecorded` marks code (`_CODE_LITERAL`) inside the step, or the marking observer's later writes would end it; the undo keys are taken on `.rich-editor`, so they work with the focus on a toolbar button, but inside an open toolbar menu they only stop the browser's undo reaching past a step; a read-only editor takes no step and is not changed. A colour picked at a bare caret waits for typed text or an IME composition there, which is never cancelled or touched and is coloured once committed (locks: `tests/richtext-colours.spec.js`, `tests/richtext-undo.spec.js`).
- **Images: png/jpeg/gif/webp only** (SVG runs same-origin), 8 MB and 16 MP caps, stored as `/api/images/<uuid>`; width is bounded by CSS, and client `ATTR_OK.img` omits `style`.
- **`reserveAttachmentBudget` (`lib/attachments.js`) checks committed plus in-flight bytes and reserves synchronously**, released in a `finally`; per-admin and global quotas, free space on `DATA_DIR` and `UPLOADS_DIR`.
- **`preflightAttachmentBudget` reserves before multer**, on the declared length or `ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` when chunked (else chunked bypasses it). The handler releases `req._attachPreflightRelease` then reserves the real size, no `await` between; `res` finish/close is the backstop (`release()` is idempotent). A pre-multer refusal goes through `refuseBeforeBody` (`lib/http.js`), draining the rest of the body unread: answered at once on a kept-alive connection, after the body for a client that asked to close (HTTP/1.0, nginx's default upstream; Node closes on finish). Below the cap never send `Connection: close` or destroy mid-upload: Firefox and WebKit show the reset as a network error and drop the answer (locks: the DRAIN arms of `tests/multer-limits.api.spec.js`; the engine witness in `tests/cross-browser.spec.js` fails only under CROSS_BROWSER=1). Only a body over `REFUSE_DRAIN_MAX_BYTES` (2 × `ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES`, declared or counted) is cut once answered.
- **Upload handlers clean up on refusal.** Declare `_movedAbs` above the `try` (the catch unlinks it); a `fileFilter` rejection still calls `cleanupUpload(req)`: a rejected part does not consume `maxCount`, and only `files: 1` refuses a second file part before it is stored.

### Scoring

#### Points ladder
- **`lib/scoring.js` owns `POINTS` (easy 50, medium 100, hard 150), `PENALTY` (5 per wrong answer) and `HINT_DEDUCTION_RATE` (0.3 of face value). Every server site imports them; never write a literal.** Recalculate (`routes/challenges.js`) rewrites stored scores, so a hand-written ladder would rescore history against a stale table.
- **Client mirrors: `DIFF_PTS` (`public/js/ui/icons.js`), the client hint rate and the "30% deduction" copy.** If they diverge, the participant is quoted one price and charged another (see *The mechanical locks — what is already pinned*).
- **Stored signs differ: `submissions.points_delta` on a wrong row is negative, `hint_reveals.points_deducted` is a positive magnitude.** Score adds the first and subtracts the second. "Points lost to incorrect" is the negated stored sum (never count × `PENALTY`); wrong rows are not de-duplicated per task.
- **The admin penalty breakdown comes from the same `wrongRows`/`hintRows` queries that compute `score` (`lib/leaderboard.js` `_scoreRows`, read by `buildLeaderboardEntries`),** so `score + pointsLostTotal` equals points earned. A second aggregation would read live data while score comes from the cache. Anyone no board lists (an event team's leads on an unranked event) is scored by `scoreTotals` over the same statements, never a sum of its own.

#### Leaderboard ranking invariant
- **One comparator: `compareLeaderboardEntries(a, b, usePresence)` in `lib/leaderboard.js`, called by `sortLeaderboard`. Score DESC is always first, `userId` `localeCompare` always last** (keeps rank byte-stable across polls).
- **Elapsed regime (default): `solveTimeMs` ASC is the only tie-break before `userId`.** `allSolved` only shapes `solveTimeMs` (see *Solve clock*); never make it a primary key, or a lower-scoring finisher outranks a higher-scoring non-finisher.
- **Presence regime (`measure_presence` AND `time_basis==='presence'`) is a block partition:** on a score tie, finishers before non-finishers; finishers by `_presenceMs` ASC then elapsed; non-finishers by elapsed only.
- **The comparator must be a strict weak ordering; do not "simplify" the block away.** Finisher-pair-only presence without the block cycles even with all values distinct; the users query has no ORDER BY, so a cycle resolves arbitrarily and persists into `achievement_badges.rank` (placement certificates) and `group_leaderboard_snapshots`. Rejected: presence only on elapsed ties (guts the feature); presence for everyone (the barely-present non-finisher outranks the hard worker); presence after elapsed (still cycles).
- **The group board uses `compareGroupBoardRows`; never re-derive it elsewhere.** Solo/solo delegates to `compareLeaderboardEntries`; any other pair: score DESC, then (presence events only) groups ahead of score-tied individuals, then `solveTimeMs`, then `0:groupId`/`1:userId`. The type partition exists because a group row can be `allSolved`, which would otherwise cycle. Presence is never read for a group row.
- **The ranking basis is resolved only in `_presenceBasis`/`_usePresenceRanking`, read by all three boards (participant, admin, group),** so no two surfaces disagree. An event can measure presence without ranking on it; exports still carry the figure. Lock: `tests/presence-display.spec.js`.

#### Solve clock
- **`solveTimeMs` = reference end − `competition_users.login_time` − closed absence gaps inside the window, clamped to ≥ 0 unconditionally** (a negative value sorts ahead of every real time). Reference end: the finish time; else (late entrant) the late reference end; else the effective end if ended, otherwise now. On a global-mode board an unfinished participant's end is then capped at their own slot's effective end. Lock: `tests/solve-clock.api.spec.js`.
- **`login_time` is the anchor (see *Competition lifecycle*); `_stampLoginTimeIfLive` also stamps a late entrant whose window is open (`req.lateEntry.canPlay`).**
- **When the anchor is written is the event's `clock_start` (owner decision, 27 Nov 2026), asked only through `lib/event-policy.js` `clockStartsAtFirstOpen(row)`** (throws on a row without the key; a Program reads `entry` whatever it holds). `first_open` (the seed for a new Booth Event and Competition): the entry doors write nothing, and `lib/middleware.js` `stampClockOnFirstTouch` writes it at the participant task GET of an assigned, published, non-qualifying challenge (after its 404s), or after a STORED answer or a hint, live or a late entrant's open window, never under impersonation, with `anchorLoginTime(..., {scoringOnly:true})` and a `first_joined` `via:"first_open"`. A Quick question starts nothing: the board's fallback anchor and "on the board" EXISTS read scoring activity only for such an event, so entering, reading the list or answering a Quick question leaves a participant off the board. `entry`: the column DEFAULT, every row from before the column (a one-way door: a board's ranking basis never moves). One column for both, so every `login_time` reader (absence restore, removal, exports) reads the first touch unchanged. **Entering is still taking part** (owner decision, 27 Nov 2026): a removal or leave of somebody who entered and opened nothing records an absence with a NULL anchor (`lib/competitions.js` `recordRemovalAbsence`, the one absence writer: `login_time` or `last_login_time` set), restoring no clock on a re-add, and the late-entry took-part check (`_tookPartBeforeEnd`) counts such an absence removed at or before the end (an absence exists only after an entry, so the removal bounds it; `last_login_time` would not, as a visit after the end moves it) (lock: the first-open ABSENCE arm of `tests/late-entry-assignment.api.spec.js`). Clone keeps the source's clock (lock: the clone arm of `tests/first-open-clock.api.spec.js`). Presence accrues nothing before a first-open clock starts. Locked once any slot starts (400 `clock_start_locked`; the admin row's `clock_start_locked` comes from `clockStartLock`); refused on a Program (`clock_start_forbidden`); a kind change to a Program sets `entry`; clone copies it, templates carry it (absent: the kind's seed); excluded from the competitions export. Participants are told on the challenge list (`clock_starts_at_first_open` on their event rows, `#clock-note`). Lock: `tests/first-open-clock.api.spec.js`.
- **A rejoin restores the saved anchor, never re-anchors** (see *Competition lifecycle*): re-anchoring would clamp a finisher's time to 0 and hand them the tie-break.
- **Late entrants rank on a separate board (`buildLateLeaderboard`: same entries and comparator), off the main and group boards.** A partition clause in the one comparator would risk non-transitivity.
- **A late entrant's reference end is `lateEntryRefEndMs`, floored by the caller at their last correct answer.** `lateEntryRefEndMs` stays pure; the floor uses `?? -Infinity` because `Math.max` with NaN returns NaN.

#### Presence needs corroboration
- **Net active time counts 5-minute buckets (`BUCKET_MS`, `lib/presence.js`), a one-way door** (see *Decisions taken once, because they cannot be taken twice*).
- **Presence comes only from server-observed requests, never the keepalive itself, sessionised events or client-reported active time.** The keepalive's `inLiveCompetition` arm fires from an idle tab, so counting it restores wall-clock time and changes rankings; sessionising events cannot see reading; a client-reported figure lets a client win every tie-break.
- **A request counts if it carries `X-Client-Active: 1` or matches the action allow-list `_isPresenceActionRequest` (`server.js`).** Polled endpoints (`/api/participant/pulse`, `/api/status`, the leaderboard) need the header. Header-only would let a client or header-stripping proxy bank zero, and zero wins the tie. Keep it an allow-list (never a deny-list of polls) and never add a polled endpoint.
- **`_recordPresenceIfActive` accrues only while the governing entity is `=== "live"`** (the participant's own slot; a foreign or deleted slot falls back to the competition), mirroring `_stampLoginTimeIfLive`'s live test. Never test `!== "ended"`: it counts the upcoming lobby and penalises early arrivals.
- **It skips impersonated requests, events with `measure_presence` off, a first-open clock not yet started (`clockStartsAtFirstOpen` and `login_time` NULL), and finished participants (`participantsFinishedAll`: solved everything or out of attempts).** The last is the completion freeze: without it a finisher who stays to read the board loses the tie to one who closed the tab.
- **`recordPresence` is a single `INSERT … SELECT FROM competition_users … ON CONFLICT … WHERE last_bucket < excluded.last_bucket`.** The SELECT enforces membership; the conflict clause makes each bucket idempotent. Do not add session state: it would emit Set-Cookie on GETs, and route guards do not cover `/api/status`.

#### What participants may see
- **`GET /api/leaderboard` caps the main, late and group boards at `PARTICIPANT_LEADERBOARD_BOARD_LIMIT` (20), resolving `me` from the full list first; admin boards are uncapped.** No total participant count may appear anywhere (allow-list lock: `tests/functional-security-c.api.spec.js`). Client column sorts re-order only rows on screen; they never fetch more or derive a total.
- **`stripInternal` drops every `_`-prefixed key from participant entries, so new internals in `buildLeaderboardEntries` must start with `_`** (a named deny-list would ship the next field). `buildAdminLeaderboard` re-publishes the penalty fields and `presenceMs`, which is `null` (not 0) when presence is not measured.
- **Identity columns (first/last name, company, email) come from `_adminIdentityMap` in `buildAdminLeaderboard`, never `buildLeaderboardEntries`,** which feeds the participant board and poll cache. The group board never gets identity fields; its participant copy drops `ADMIN_PENALTY_FIELDS` and `memberCount`.
- **The admin presenter view (`S._rkHideIdentity`) hides company and email by not rendering them; never `display:none`** (CSS-hidden cells can still be found and copied). `_rkCols()` drives the header, colspans and cells; the search (`_rkMatches`) reads the same toggle via `_rkAnon()`. The toggle is per tab, unpersisted and a display control, not a permission, so CSV export carries every column. The static `<thead>` in `index.html` must carry `data-rk-cols` so `_rkHeadStale` can force a rebuild.
- **Other views replace the single board's card** (an unranked event's Progress card, per-session boards, a cleared scope's all-live grid), so every writer of `#admin-rankings-body` calls `_rkEnsureBoardCard` first: a write into a missing body throws inside `setAdminCompetitionScope`, aborting the workspace open (lock: `tests/open-ended-dates.spec.js`, the reopen arm).
- **Admin boards show the first `RANKINGS_PAGE` (50) rows with a "Show all" expander, deliberately not paged** (paging breaks standings continuity). Sort, expand and search re-render from `S._rkCache` (`_rkRerenderFromCache` in `features/rankings-admin.js`), never re-fetch, or the all-live view sends one request per competition per timeslot each time.
- **`S._rkCache` deliberately has no invalidator; do not add one.** It is keyed on the request URL and read only while `_rkSortOnly` is set, so Refresh, a scope change and tab entry miss by construction. A submission landing between load and sort appears only after Refresh, as intended.

#### Unranked kinds
- **For an unranked kind both leaderboard routes answer 200 with an empty board, never 403:** participant gets `ranking_enabled:false` (the key is on both branches), admin gets `[]`. The client treats non-ok as lost access and would eject the participant.
- **Points still accrue and are stored; only rendering is suppressed, so do not fork `lib/scoring.js` per kind.** Hidden: the board and `#header-score`; the Leaderboard tab stays, inactive, saying "This event has no leaderboard" (not locked: the spec holds only that no ACTIVE tab shows). Visible (accepted disposition): the pre-submit penalty warning, the wrong-answer result with running score, per-challenge points. The admin Rankings panel shows Progress instead. Lock: `tests/ranking-suppression.spec.js`.

#### Group leaderboard
- **The group board renders only when `group_leaderboard_enabled` is on (default off; read fresh by PK: no cache to invalidate) AND at least 2 bound groups each have an eligible on-board member;** otherwise participant gets `null`, admin `?groups=1` gets `[]`. Solo members (`direct_assigned=1`) appear on their own; group-only members appear only inside their group. Group score is the members' mean; penalties are sums.
- **Once `competitionFullyEnded`, the board is served from `group_leaderboard_snapshots`** (`_saveGroupSnapshot`, INSERT OR IGNORE: first write wins). Groups are reusable across events, so without it a later membership edit would rewrite a finished result. Rules:
  - `freezeGroupLeaderboardSnapshots` must run at every not-ended → ended transition; any new end path must call it (the scheduler catches only time-expired events), and `enqueueCertificateDispatch` beside it (see *Automatic issue and the congratulations email*);
  - compute the snapshot fresh (see *Persist versus poll*);
  - never freeze a `null` produced because the flag was off;
  - any new revive path must call `invalidateGroupLeaderboardSnapshot`.
- **After the end, only groups with `competition_groups.assigned_at` at or before the effective end (or NULL, for legacy rows) are counted,** so a group bound during the late window is not ranked.
- **Never rename the global slot key `__global__` (`_GROUP_SNAP_SENTINEL`):** it would orphan every frozen board. Real slots are keyed by the bare timeslot id (`_groupSnapKey`), and a foreign slot is refused before any snapshot read or write.

### Groups and tags

#### Participant groups

- **`competition_users.direct_assigned` decides what live sync may remove.** `1` explicit and sticky; `0` group-origin, the only rows reconcile drops (`lib/group-sync.js` `reconcileCompetitionMembership`).
- **Every promotion to direct emits `membership_promoted`, guarded on the old value** (assign route, `_selfEnrollEnrollTxn`); unrecorded, a later group removal keeps the member.
- **Manual removal of a group-desired member writes a `competition_group_exclusions` tombstone**, else reconcile re-enrols them. Cleared by direct assign, self-enrolment, or leaving the last bound group wanting them via member-removal routes (`_clearTombstonesForGroupLeave`); group delete and unbind keep it.
- **Reconcile asks `competitionAdmission` per removal, never status** (see *Competition lifecycle*). It skips blocked, unapproved and `staffRowRefusal` users, so role and staff-tag changes re-run `reconcileUserGroupCompetitions` (the role POST rewrites the staff tag).
- **Removing a group chip cascades** (eviction, mail); its confirm says access *may* end (client cannot see `direct_assigned`).
- **Groups are `groups` rows that persist when empty; import is add-only:** `_rehydrateGroups` (`server.js`) creates missing groups and only `INSERT OR IGNORE`s memberships (replacing would evict and mail).
- **Member-clash rule: at most one bound group per participant per competition** (the team board averages each participant into one group). Only where `group_leaderboard_enabled = 1` (filtered in the detectors' SQL, not an early return); ended bindings count (revivable). 409 `{error, clash}` before any write (an invitation may already have created its `newGroups`).
- **Clash paths:** bind (assign route `groupIds` only) `detectBoundGroupClashes`; member add `detectMemberAddClashes`; invitations `detectMultiGroupAddClashes`; registration, the invitation claim at sign-in and import skip the clashing group, never failing (import counts `group_clashes_dropped`).
- **Only a superadmin may add or remove an admin/superadmin in a group** (membership live-syncs them into events): 403 before any write (`routes/groups.js` `_privilegedGroupTargets`). Invitations skip and count those groups (`skippedPrivilegedGroups`); group delete is not gated.
- **Groups page row flags** (`routes/groups.js`): `can_write` (scope, not competing), `can_delete` (plus ownership), `competing`; `can_bind` is scope alone: the assign route guards the target event, and a competitor check would hide groups from the picker.
- **Fan-out caps:** ≤ `BULK_ADMIN_MAX` members/request; ≤ `RECONCILE_MAX_PER_CALL` per pass, overflow in deduped detached passes (`_scheduleReconcileDrain`), counted in `reconcileDrainPending()` before `setImmediate` so shutdown waits.
- **Names are unique on `groups.name_key`** (`lib/util.js` `_groupNameKey`, folds ß/SS that NOCASE misses); every insert/rename writes it. Non-ASCII allowed.

#### Tagging system

- **Three dimensions, each a join table:** `user_tags`, `task_tags`, `competition_tags`. User and competition lists merge in their permanent tags (tasks have none). Task and competition tags are lowercased; user tags trimmed, case kept. A tag DELETE takes the tag as a path segment: `encodeURIComponent` it (`test/dry run` has a slash).
- **`staff` and `benchmark` are permanent user tags** (`PERMANENT_TAGS`), always listed (analytics chips). `staff`: off leaderboards unless the event's `staff_compete` is on; auto-applied to admins, unremovable from them. `benchmark`: shown apart; a rival door on compete events (see *Roles, scope and impersonation*).
- **Participants see only `category:<name>` task tags** (see *Challenges*).
- **`public-domain` (`PUBLIC_DOMAIN_TAG`, `lib/event-admin.js`), the shared-pool task tag, is matched by exact SQL equality** (`taskVisibilitySql`, export scope). The tag POST refuses near misses (`publicDomainNearMiss`: separator/invisible variants, `publicdomain`) with 400 `public_domain_near_miss`; never fold them (that widens visibility platform-wide), so "public domain" is unusable as a label. Client Fix adds the real tag, then deletes the miss.
- **`test/dry run` is a stored value**, `DRY_RUN_TAG` (`lib/competitions.js`, mirrored in `ui/format.js`): default analytics exclusion, `isDryRunCompetition` badge refusal, ribbon. Lowercase, no comma (analytics comma-joins tags); any other spelling rejoins analytics and becomes badge-eligible. `/api/my-competitions` needs `withTags: true` or the picker's dry-run badge vanishes on the 15 s poll.

### Achievement badges and certificates

#### Bases and eligibility

- **Three bases, chosen at mint from `eventPolicy(comp).certificate` and the caller's board rank, stored in `basis`.** Placement: rank 1-3 with points above zero (`earnsPlacement`, `lib/badge.js`; operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026) on an attempt kind (medal, rank, score). Participation: any other attempt-kind participant who attempted, a top-three place on zero or less included; the participant podium cards still show whoever holds places 1-3. Completion: a Program learner who finished (every scoring challenge solved or out of attempts) with a grade of 60% or more, the grade frozen on it (see *Program grades and Try again*). An issued medal certificate keeps its medal even at zero or less: every surface showing an issued certificate (the holder's list and Leaderboard card, the organisers' list, Rankings top three, `/credential`, artwork, top-3 ZIP) reads its frozen `basis`, never the score; the participant podium's tier chip follows the points rule, naming no medal there (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026; a superadmin can revoke any certificate; locks: `tests/placement-points.api.spec.js`, `tests/rankings-top-three.spec.js`).
- **`_mintEligibility` (`routes/badge.js`) is the one predicate.** The mint and the `claimable` list of `GET /api/me/certificates` both call it, so the UI never offers what the mint refuses. The mint passes `fresh:true` (it freezes rank and score); the offer reads the cache. Only `canShareBadge` (`routes/leaderboard.js`, gates `#lb-badge-cta`) restates it: UI-only, but change it with the predicate.
- **Attempt kinds require `competitionFullyEnded`; completion kinds require the learner's grade (`lib/program-grade.js` `programStanding`: finished and passed), no lifecycle gate.** A placement is unknowable until the board is final; a Program deadline can be a year out. Finished is `participantsFinishedAll` for one learner (owner decision, 27 Nov 2026: a Program certificate grades performance, so running out of attempts finishes too and the penalties count against the grade); zero published scoring challenges is never finished. Refusals: `not_completed` (unfinished), `grade_below_pass` (under 60%).
- **"Attempted" is `participantAttempted`: a submission or hint reveal in this competition.** Feedback does not count: `task_feedback` has no competition id.
- **Benchmark-tagged participants never mint** (not on the board); nor do staff and event admins unless staff-compete is on, nor dry-run events (`isDryRunCompetition`). A late entrant gets participation while the window is open, whatever their late-board rank; a later placement needs a superadmin to revoke it and issue it again.
- **Mint order in `POST /api/competitions/:id/badge`:** impersonation scope refusal, membership, existing unrevoked badge returned, revoked 403, then `_mintEligibility`. Existing precedes lifecycle refusals (a minted badge is a fact; the held CTA re-POSTs to fetch it); scope precedes existing because a token is a live capability. The read-only impersonation carve-out is in *Roles, scope and impersonation*.
- **Minting is idempotent per (user, competition, timeslot)**: a repeat returns the first mint's frozen facts and emits no `badge_minted`, so a test owns its fixture and mints last.
- **The dialog checks stored `basis` before wire `placement`** (itself basis-derived: see *Frozen snapshots and the live name*), so a completion certificate never gets a placement title (`public/js/features/badge-share.js`). Held certificates match events by id, never name; `heldCertificateFor` (badge-share.js, also read by the Leaderboard card) prefers an unrevoked one, else a revoked one shown as revoked, with no mint (lock: `tests/certificate-surfaces.spec.js`). `_hideCta` hides and empties `#cert-cta`: a hidden node must not keep another event's date and code. The board's certificate card (`features/leaderboard.js`) shows a held entry over a minute old until its re-read answers (stale, never absent), waits for the read before drawing the card when nothing has been read for the event yet (its first paint, after the dialog: sweep #289), repaints only when what it draws changed (`_certSig`; witness "no repaint" with `certRereadSettled()`, never a wait) and offers nothing for a revoked certificate (lock: `tests/leaderboard-cert-card.spec.js`).

#### Frozen snapshots and the live name

- **`_badgeArt(row, comp)` is the single resolver for PNG, PDF and public page; all render on demand.** Safe only because facts freeze at mint: `score_snapshot`, `date_snapshot`, `basis`, `rank` (real, e.g. 7), `late_entrant`, `name_match_snapshot`. A revive or moved deadline must not rewrite an issued certificate.
- **No issue stores the share image** (`_NO_STORED_IMAGE`, `routes/badge.js`; operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026): the participant's mint, the automatic issue and Issue again write `image_id = ''`. Each still renders it first, as the check that the artwork can be drawn now: without a renderer the mint answers 503 and the automatic issue waits (`render_unavailable`), never issuing a certificate nobody can download. A deployment that has lost its renderer answers 503 for `badge.png` as for the PDF; a certificate whose row still names a stored copy (`_storedBadgePng`, issued while mints stored one) is served that copy, until its certificate or account is deleted, which deletes it (locks: the sweep #43 arms of `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`, a server spawned without its renderer through `tests/fixtures/no-badge-renderer.cjs`).
- **Design is live, facts are frozen.** `_badgeArt` also carries `design` (`resolveCertificateConfig(db, comp.id)`, read on every render) and `verifyLink` (the QR target, NULL without a code); only `buildCertificateSvg` reads either. A design edit re-skins issued certificates, wording and the `show` switches (score, date, QR, code) included (operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026: designs stay live), so no design key may substitute a holder fact or hide the late-entrant marker; the facts stay frozen and `/credential` keeps them. The ended-event lock covers an event's own layer only: the global design still reaches an ended event's certificates (same ruling).
- **The certificate tier is `certificateTier` (`lib/certificate-config.js`): the frozen `basis` first, the rank second.** A participation basis with a podium rank (a late entrant minted while the window is open) is a seal and "Participant", never "1st Place"; `badgeBasisLabel` and `isPlacementBasis` (`lib/badge.js`: the wire's `placement`, the share card, the public page) follow the same rule, as does a held certificate's Leaderboard card (its `tier`, never the board rank).
- **The date (`_badgeEndDate`) is when the event ended for that participant:** completion takes their last answer to a SCORING challenge, right or wrong (when they finished; with everything solved, the last correct one; `notQualifyingSql`: a Quick answer never dates it; event end would print a future date); otherwise their own session's `timeslotEffectiveEnd` via `competition_users` (the row has `timeslot_id` only in per-slot mode), else `competitionEffectiveEnd`.
- **The holder's name is read live** (`_badgeName`); `name_snapshot || nickname_snapshot` is the last fallback. A legacy badge shows the person, never the handle (lock: `tests/badge.api.spec.js`). Legacy NULLs: no score; date via live `_badgeEndDate`; basis from rank.
- **Printed name:** `certificate_given_name` + `certificate_family_name` override only when both are set (`certificateNameParts`). Two fields: matching needs a frozen family part without a salutation list. `certificateName` puts the family name first with no space when both parts are Han, kana or Hangul (张伟; operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026), the override included; the matched parts do not move. **`certificatePrintedName` (`lib/badge.js`) is the one rule for the printed name** (`_badgeName`; before issue, the admin board's `certificatePrintedName` and the account's `certificate_printed_name` on `/api/me` and the sign-in answer; once issued, `name` (`_heldName`, what the artwork prints) in `GET /api/me/certificates` and `GET /api/admin/competitions/:id/certificates`): the browser never composes it (the Issued list, the Rankings top three, which names an issued place by its certificate, the Leaderboard card; locks: `tests/certificate-scripts.api.spec.js`, `tests/certificate-designer-state.spec.js`, `tests/rankings-top-three.spec.js`, `tests/leaderboard-cert-card.spec.js`).

#### Public page and publish-on-share

- **`_badgePageWithheld(row)` withholds every page until the owner's Share stamps `shared_at`**; downloading must not publish a crawlable page naming the holder. Wire `published` calls it. Only the owner's `POST /badge/:token/shared` and the mailed share link's POST (see *Automatic issue and the congratulations email*) stamp `shared_at`; it is write-once, never backfilled.
- **`settings.badge_page_share_gate_at` is a grandfather floor**, stamped once (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`); older pages stay live.
- **One uniform 404 body (`_badgeNotFoundHtml`, no argument) for invalid, revoked, orphaned and unshared**; a distinct answer is an oracle. It and the live page are drawn by `verify-pages.css` alone (no `style` attribute) with the platform look on `<html>` (lock: `tests/badge-public-page.spec.js`). PNG and PDF routes stay ungated: they are how the holder collects.
- **Sharing is LinkedIn link-intent only** (`_shareUrl`, `share-offsite/?url=` the page; no `w_member_social`, no stored tokens); LinkedIn crawls its OpenGraph tags (`og:image` = `badge.png`, 1200x627). The dialog's synchronously opened popup navigates only on `published === true` from the share POST.
- **Retrieval is audited on the competition's History** (actor or `anonymous`, owner, `by_owner`): `badge_certificate_downloaded` per PDF, `badge_shared` per Share, `badge_image_downloaded` only with `?dl=1`, since crawlers fetch the og:image. **The congratulations email's PDF link carries `?from=email`** (`lib/certificate-dispatch.js` `certificateMailPdfUrl`/`isCertificateMailLink`; operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026): mail scanners and link prefetchers fetch every link in a mail, signed out, as the holder's own click from it would, so such a download is recorded `via:"email_link"` and History and the Audit page say it *may be a mail scanner* (not when the holder's own signed-in session made it). A marker, never a capability: it changes no answer, and the holder's own surfaces link the PDF without it (locks: `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`, the mail's link and the record; `tests/certificate-mail-link-history.spec.js`, the wording).

#### Credential code and /credential

- **`public_code`: 12 chars of the `lib/human-code.js` alphabet, printed 4-4-4.** One-way door (printed). New mints only; legacy NULL is never backfilled and prints no verify line.
- **`POST /credential` is byte-identical for a wrong surname and an unknown code**, else the code reveals its holder. JS-free, `noindex`, `no-store`; a revoked certificate resolves and says so.
- **Failures-only budgets** (`_credFails`, hourly, 429): 30 per source, and 10 per code from each source, keyed on the code AND the source (operator rulings, 24 Nov 2026: a posted code must not let one reader block every verifier; a source is a network, `lib/login-code.js` `originKeyFor`'s IPv4 /24 or IPv6 /64, as one IPv6 connection holds a whole /64), checked before lookup; counting successes would lock out a cohort check. Overflow evicts expired then oldest, never `.clear()`.
- **`name_match_snapshot` freezes parts, never keys**, so fold improvements apply retroactively. `credentialMatchKeys` emits the family name and its tokens, each as `credentialNameKey` plus `credentialNameKeyDigraph` (NFC, then delegates: Müller = Muller = Mueller); never make the canonical fold digraph. `_DIGRAPH_MAP` is exactly ä/ö/ü/å/ø: a letter joins only if the fold leaves a bare vowel and a two-letter spelling is conventional (`_XLIT_MAP` already folds æ/œ/ß).
- **Keep the fold narrow: the surname is the second factor** (lock: `tests/completion-credential.spec.js`: Løvland ≠ Lovlund, þ is th, a codepoint sweep for normalisation-invariance and idempotence). `credentialNameKey` applies `_XLIT_MAP` last, never `_transliterateAscii` (it empties non-Latin surnames). `_credentialNameChanged` compares frozen parts with today's at exactly the lookup's tolerance. A Chinese, Japanese or Korean name printed as one word matches whole (as printed, less the salutation the parts never hold) or by its family part, never its given part (`credentialMatchKeys`, from the frozen parts; operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026); the page's surname hint says so (locks: `tests/certificate-scripts.api.spec.js`, `tests/completion-credential.spec.js`).

#### Automatic issue and the congratulations email

- **`lib/certificate-dispatch.js` is the one dispatcher for every end path.** End paths never issue or send: each calls `enqueueCertificateDispatch(compId)` beside its `freezeGroupLeaderboardSnapshots` (grep `enqueueCertificateDispatch`; Close late entries now is one), which upserts `certificate_dispatch_jobs` (PK competition + `user_id`, `''` for the event; only an event over now gets a row). A Program's finishing answer (correct, or spending the last attempt) calls `enqueueCompletionCertificate` (a per-person row, queued only at 60% or more), and a challenge edit that can complete a learner (visibility, the PUT's `active`, unassign, a restore or import overwrite moving it in or out of the count, Recalculate, a challenge becoming qualifying) calls `enqueueCompletersOf` after its commit with the events whose count it changed (`routes/challenges.js` `_queueCompleters`), which queues only learners complete now who had not solved that challenge before (`solvedBefore` where the write changes answers): one complete before is never mailed by an edit; a Program never gets an event row. A new end path must call it too (proximity scanner: `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`; each session route's end and a Program's wrap-up driven through the queues: `tests/session-end-mail-queues.spec.js`).
- **The worker decides at RUN time:** event gone, not fully ended (revived), effective end before `automaticMailFloor()` (the later of `settings.certificate_dispatch_floor_at`, the grandfather floor, and `restore_mail_floor_at`, a mark-sent restore's; a missing grandfather floor fails closed), dry-run, or `email.enabled` off in the certificate design → the job is dropped; so is a Program completer's job queued before the restore floor (`queued_before_restore`), and a failed send first tried before it is not retried. Event jobs settle 5 minutes (`EVENT_SETTLE_MS`), so an End Now undone at once mails nobody. A job is deleted only by `enqueued_at` match, so a re-enqueue during a pass is never lost.
- **Eligibility is the mint's:** `_autoIssueCertificate` (`routes/badge.js`, wired by `setCertificateIssuer`: lib cannot require a route) runs `_mintEligibility` with `fresh:true`, returns a held certificate as a fact, never replaces a revoked one, and re-asks revocation, membership, the account and whether the person still counts on the board (`getStaffOrBenchmarkSet`, uncached; refusal `off_board`, which also `reset()`s the job's board memo) in its write transaction. **A late entrant whose window is open is deferred** (the job's `due_at` moves past the deadline), never minted: an automatic mint would lock them into participation. **An event job whose late window is open waits for it to close whatever its pass found**: a later joiner or first answer is issued only by that job (a window lapsing by itself is no end path). Blocked, denied and unapproved accounts are skipped. It shares `_mintInputs`, `_BADGE_INSERT_SQL` and `_badgeInsertValues` with the participant's mint route, whose persist stays inline (the placement scanners read the handler). Its persist is `.immediate()`; an issue failing for any reason but the renderer keeps the job for a later pass (`ISSUE_RETRY_MS`; counted in `last_error` as `issue_failed:<n>`, never in `passes`, at most `MAX_ISSUE_RETRIES`, then the pass carries on to the late window's wait or done), which skips everyone already issued.
- **Each board is built once per job, never per participant:** `_mintEligibility` takes an optional `boards` memo (`_eligibilityBoards`, one main and one late board per slot, built lazily with `fresh:true`); the dispatcher keeps one per job only across the back-to-back passes of one run (a capped pass), dropping it whenever it defers, re-ends, closes or re-arms the job for a timed retry (render or issue failure), as the boards are fresh when built but not final (who counts can still change; lock: `tests/certificate-dispatch-fresh-boards.spec.js`); the tick reports `boardBuilds` (`lib/leaderboard.js` `leaderboardComputeCount`). A per-participant fresh build made a 3000-person pass O(n²); with the memo, `perf/certificate-dispatch-scale.cjs 3000` measures 2 board builds (lock: the board-builds arm of `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`, seen failing with the memo removed, ignored by eligibility or not passed by the dispatcher).
- **Never twice:** `certificate_emails` is keyed on the badge token and a `sending` row is written before the send, so no re-run, revive + re-end or second end path mails a certificate again; a new certificate (first mint after a revive or a revoke) has a new token. **At most once across a crash:** boot marks `sending` rows `unknown`, never retried automatically. A failed send is retried to `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (3), 5 then 30 minutes after it failed, and a given-up one never again (lock: `tests/certificate-email-ledger.spec.js`, with Resend's `no_certificate` and `certificate_email_in_flight`, the ledger put back exactly when a Resend's admin session ends between its render and its re-check, and the shutdown drain waiting for a send in flight); one pass starts at most `MAX_SENDS_PER_PASS` sends per job, a soft cap (up to `CONCURRENCY` − 1 more), the next pass sending the rest; a missing grandfather floor issues nothing (`no_floor`, said by the certificate list and the top-three download) (lock: `tests/certificate-dispatch-gates.spec.js`, also a revoked certificate never replaced). Resend (`POST /api/admin/competitions/:id/certificates/resend`) is the only other re-send and runs even with the switch off. **Every send re-asks the holder after its render** (`_holderIneligible`: revoked, blocked, denied or unapproved, and for the automatic pass and its retry not a member): settled `skipped` (`last_error` the reason), never retried, no share link; Resend is refused instead (409 `account_inactive`, `certificate_revoked`), the ledger untouched (sweep #212; lock: the "sweeps #212 and #405" describe of `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`). The PDF is `_certificatePdfFor`, the one render for the public and admin downloads and the attachment.
- **The mail is warm and per tier (`email.message_<tier>`) and carries a "Copy my LinkedIn post" link: a ONE-TIME capability (`lib/certificate-share.js`): 32 random bytes, stored only as a sha256 in `certificate_share_links`, bound to the certificate (cascade; a revoke kills it), seven days.** Minted before each send to an address that has not opted out (an opted-out address gets neither; the certificate is still issued), so a retried send mints another; an unused one expires. **The daily retention pass deletes a link 30 days after it expired, used or not** (`SHARE_LINK_PURGE_AFTER_MS`, `purgeExpiredShareLinks`; operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026): until then its page says it expired (or was used), after it the page is the uniform not-found (lock: the sweep #347 arm of `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`). `GET /badge/:link/post` shows the post and consumes NOTHING (mail scanners GET every link); its POST consumes by compare-and-swap, stamps `shared_at` (the holder's publish-on-share: whoever holds the mail may press it, and the page's note says it publishes; operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026) and records `badge_shared` `via:"share_link"`, answering 200 with `ok`. The page's script (`public/js/features/certificate-share-page.js`, outside the SPA) opens the composer in the click's own task and fetches a fresh token just before that POST (the served one may belong to a session another tab replaced, and a refused token ends the session it is sent with), saying so when the POST is lost (lock: `tests/certificate-share-page.spec.js`). The path reuses the `/badge/<capability>` log scrub. `linkedinPostFor` is the one post builder (share page; signed-in card via `GET /api/me/certificates/:token/linkedin-post`); its `/credential?code=` link is added only while the event's design prints the code (operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026: the code or the QR; the QR is drawn only beside the code, so `show.qr` alone prints neither; lock: `tests/linkedin-post-verify-link.api.spec.js`). LinkedIn cannot create @tags from a URL: the page says to pick Checkmarx and the organisers from LinkedIn's suggestions (lock: the share-link arm of `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`).
- Audited per attempt as `user_events.certificate_emailed` (`status`, `attempt`, `via`: `automatic`/`retry`/`resend`/`reissue`; the last two carry `reqContext`), beside `email_sent`; an automatic mint records `badge_minted` with `via:"automatic"`. Under test nothing runs by itself; drive it with `POST /api/test/certificate-dispatch-tick` (lock: `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`).
- **Admin routes** (`scoped`, `competitionDenied`; Resend also the competitor lock): `GET /api/admin/competitions/:id/certificates` (the token for a superadmin only, each credential `code` only for a holder of `certificates.lookup` (`_certificateCode`: else the list is the code-to-holder table the audited lookup withholds; the PDF still prints it, one audited download each), the Issue again answer for a holder of `certificates.revoke`; `counts` beside the rows, `?summary=1` for counts alone; one ledger query and one share-gate settings read per list (`certificateEmailStates`), never per row; lock: `tests/certificate-surfaces.spec.js`), `…/certificates/:userId/certificate.pdf`, `…/badge.png` (audited `by_owner:false`, `private, no-store`).

#### Revocation and the two indexes

- **Revoke: `POST /api/admin/badges/:token/revoke`, superadmin only**; repeat is 409 `already_revoked`. The reason (max 500 chars) is recorded as `badge_revoked` on the participant's History, attributed to the admin.
- **Issued certificates never change by themselves; Issue again is the one way past a revocation** (operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026): a revive leaves them as issued, which the revive confirms say from the admin list's `certificates_issued` (`certificatesKeptOnReviveNote`, `features/competitions-admin.js`, also in the assign warning). `POST /api/admin/competitions/:id/certificates/reissue` (`certificates.revoke`, superadmin tier; `_adminCertificateGate` write) runs `_autoIssueCertificate` with `reissue`: the mint's eligibility (`fresh`), a new token and code, the acting admin re-asked inside the write (`adminAuthzDenied`, after the render), refused while a live one stands (409 `certificate_held`, the partial index settling a race) or the board is not final (`not_ended`, `late_entry_open`); audited `badge_reissued` (user) and `badge_minted` `via:"reissue"`, mailed at once and detached like Resend (`sendReissuedCertificate`, `certificate_emailed` `via:"reissue"`): the event's email switch and dry-run tag hold it, neither floor does. The Issued list answers it per revoked row from the route's own checks, in the route's order (`canIssueAgain` or `issueAgainRefused`, `_issueAgainRefusal`), only to a holder of `certificates.revoke` who is not competing, and not on `?summary=1`; the token (the Revoke button's key) stays superadmin-only. The holder's own mint and the automatic issue still refuse a revoked one (lock: the Issue again describe of `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`).
- **`uq_achievement_badges_user_comp_ts` is partial on `revoked_at IS NULL`; `uq_achievement_badges_public_code` is not**, so a revoked code resolves as revoked, not forged. The partial index is safe only because of the revoked re-mint 403, re-checked inside the write transaction after the render await.

#### Certificate design (configurable)

- **`lib/certificate-config.js` owns the design: defaults <- global (`settings.certificate_config`) <- event (`competitions.certificate_config`; NULL inherits); both layers edited by admins and superadmins only, event admins preview and download (operator ruling, 29 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026; held twice, `EVENT_ADMIN_FORBIDDEN` and `requireGlobalAdmin`), merged field by field.** Each stored layer is PARTIAL (only the keys set). The defaults reproduce the operator's gold reference. Sections: `text` (`{platform}` is the only placeholder), `labels` (per tier `headline`, which takes no placeholder and prints as typed: a platform name of up to 120 characters would be cut in its 30-character slot; and `sub`, which takes `{platform}`), `colors` (six-digit hex only; `frame` has no default: unset follows the tier metal), `show` (logo, score, date, qr, code, pattern, shield, medal), `email` (switch, subject, intro, one `message_<tier>` thank-you per tier, and the LinkedIn post's `linkedin_post`/`linkedin_hashtags`; no field may hold a line break).
- **Strict on write, tolerant on read.** `validateCertificateConfig` refuses an unknown key, a wrong type, over-long or control-character text, an unknown `{token}` or a non-hex colour with a named 400 (`code` + `field`), and `text.*`/`labels.*` wording the badge fonts cannot draw (`canRenderText` on the line as drawn, in capitals too, `{platform}` left out and filled with the current name) as `certificate_config_undrawable` (operator ruling, 23 Nov 2026), on write only (`drawable:false` on the read path: a stored one keeps rendering until its layer is next saved; a later platform rename is not checked against stored designs; the email fields are not drawn and not checked); `sanitiseStoredConfig` (every read) keeps the valid fields of a stored layer and drops the rest, so a rollback still renders. A new field goes in the module's tables, swagger's `CertificateConfigLayer` and `tests/certificate-config.spec.js`.
- **Routes (`routes/certificate-config.js`):** GET/PUT/DELETE `/api/admin/certificate-config` (global admin; settings event `certificate_config`); GET (scoped) and PUT/DELETE (global admin) `/api/admin/competitions/:id/certificate-config` (404, competitor lock (lock: `tests/certificate-design-competitor-lock.spec.js`, the designer read-only for a competitor and an event admin), `competitionDenied`, then the ended rule: past the effective end only a superadmin writes, 403 `certificate_config_ended_locked`; History `certificate_config_changed`; deliberately does not bump `updated_at`, the event form's token). Both PUTs replace the whole layer, so they take the read's `version` (the stored layer's digest) as `expectedVersion`: 409 `certificate_config_changed` when it moved, unchecked when absent; the designer sends it (lock: `tests/certificate-designer-state.spec.js`). POST `/api/admin/certificate-config/preview` (any admin, limiter after the guard; sample data over global <- event <- an unsaved `config` layer; mints, stores and audits nothing; prints a zero placeholder code).
- **The per-event layer is EXCLUDED from the competitions export** (no importer); the global layer leaves with the settings export. The admin list carries only `certificate_config_overridden`; `_compWire` strips the raw column.

#### Program grades and Try again

- **A Program learner is graded (owner decision, 27 Nov 2026): `lib/program-grade.js` is the rule; never a second sum.** Points are `lib/leaderboard.js` `scoreTotals` (the boards' `_scoreRows`: correct answers less every wrong-answer penalty and hint), floored at 0 and capped at the maximum, the face value (`lib/scoring.js` POINTS) of the published, active scoring challenges assigned (`notQualifyingSql`: a Quick question is in neither). Bands `GRADE_BANDS`: 90 Excellent, 80 Very good, 70 Good, 60 Satisfactory, inclusive at the lower edge, decided on the exact fraction in integers (`gradeFromPoints`; 59.96% is not 60%); `permille` is the share in tenths of a percent rounded DOWN, so the percentage shown never reads as a band not reached; under 60 is `try_again`, no certificate. The grade counts once the learner has FINISHED (`participantsFinishedAll` for one learner); before then they are shown where they stand. Lock: `tests/program-grades.api.spec.js` (each boundary, penalties, hints, Quick questions, mint, automatic issue, retake), `tests/program-grades-ui.spec.js` (the learner's card).
- **The grade is frozen at mint** (`achievement_badges.grade_band`, `grade_permille`; `_badgeInsertValues` takes `grade` from the verdict, one statement for both writers) and read everywhere through `routes/badge.js` `_frozenGrade` (artwork, share card, `/credential`, the holder's and organisers' lists, the mail's fact line). A certificate without one (every non-Program certificate, and completion certificates issued before grades) renders exactly as before: `buildCertificateSvg` takes the band's wording only for the `completion` tier with a known band (`_gradeOf`, `lib/badge.js`).
- **The design:** `labels` takes the tiers and the bands (`lib/certificate-config.js` `LABEL_KEYS` = `TIERS` + `GRADE_LABEL_KEYS`, which equal `GRADE_BAND_KEYS`: locked); a graded certificate prints its band's headline and sub with the completion tier's colours and seal, and the percentage under `text.grade_heading` where a placement prints its score (`show.score`). The designer lists the bands beside the tiers (`CERT_TIERS` entries with `colour: 'completion'`), and the preview takes a band as `sample.tier`.
- **Try again (`POST /api/competitions/:id/program/retake`, `startProgramRetake`) MOVES the attempt, never filters it.** One `.immediate()` transaction re-asks `retakeRefusal` (Program, live, member, finished, under 60%, no live certificate), writes `program_attempts` (attempt number, grade, and the attempt's `submissions` and `hint_reveals` rows to scoring challenges as JSON, every column as stored) and deletes those live rows, so every reader of a score, solve, attempt count, hint (its `(competition, user, task)` primary key frees for a new reveal) or finish sees the new attempt alone with no predicate to forget. Kept: Quick answers (not graded; the event team reads them), `challenge_events` (analytics keeps every row), attempt grants, feedback, `login_time`. Recorded as `program_retake_started` (`user_events`, with `reqContext`); busts the leaderboard and me caches after commit. Refused under impersonation for every tier (`impersonation_retake_refused`, the join's reason). A learner who passed does not retake to improve. **A new table holding per-attempt rows for a participant in an event decides here whether Try again moves it.** The Audit page's submissions source and the Submissions page show the live rows only; a closed attempt lives in `program_attempts` (the full export's `grades.csv`, the learner's History).
- **Where it is read:** GET /api/me/certificates `programs` (each Program's standing and `canRetake`, from `retakeRefusal`: the Challenges page card, `badge-share.js` `programStandingHtml`), `GET /api/admin/competitions/:id/grades` (`leaderboard.view`, scoped; the Rankings Progress card's Grades list), the full event export's `grades.csv` (a Program's only).

#### Certificate PDF

- **Two canvases:** `buildCertificateSvg` (1536x1024, on A4), `buildBadgeSvg` (1200x627 share card, fixed copy, not configurable); `renderBadgePng` reads width off the SVG. The certificate artwork is vector (no embedded bitmap; its PDF carries it rendered once as a 1536x1024 raster): a struck medal (podium tiers) or an award seal (participation, completion), leaf-built laurels, a guilloché frame band and faint security linework, all from `_metal(base)` ramps of the configured colours. The verification block (ID heading, code, verify line, QR) is certificate-only, shrink-only, never ellipsised; a badge with no `public_code` draws none of it. **The QR is dark modules on a light plate whatever the palette** (`_qrInks`: the darker of background and text on a plate of the lighter tinted half-way to white, or black on white when those modules and that plate would be under 128 luma apart; the default palette draws as it always did), on a plate leaving a four-module quiet zone: readers take darker as dark (lock: `tests/qr-decode.spec.js`).
- **The QR comes from `lib/qr.js`** (no dependency): byte mode, level M by default (byte-identical to its first release; the certificate relies on it), versions 1-10; it throws above capacity rather than truncating, and the renderer then omits the QR, never the code. The certificate's QR encodes `BASE_URL/credential?code=<code>`: GET `/credential` pre-fills the code and `_scrubLogPath` already redacts `?code=` (encoder algebra locked in `tests/certificate-config.spec.js`; versions 1-10 and the certificate's drawn mark DECODED by the suite's own reader, `tests/qr-decode.js`, which also refuses any wrong function-pattern module (finders and separators, timing, alignment, the dark module), in `tests/qr-decode.spec.js`). **The event JOIN QR is level H with the event code in a reserved centre** (`encodeQr(text, {level:"H", centre})`, `qrSvg`; served by `POST /api/admin/join-qr`, `open`): the data modules under the label are cleared and the encoder moves up a version until every Reed-Solomon block's damage is at most half its budget minus one, else it refuses (the reservation locked, each reserved symbol decoded with no function-pattern exemption, in `tests/join-qr.spec.js`; the route's SVG decoded from 120-1600 px rasters with its code drawn, the label box its one exemption, and the worst reading under the label inside each block's capacity less one, in `tests/qr-decode.spec.js`). **Every join QR an organiser is shown or downloads carries the code (operator rule, confirmed 17 Nov 2026):** the Join Link dialog, its PNG download, the workspace pill and its full-screen view all ask through `self-enroll.js` `joinQrDataUri`; when that request fails, the dialog preview, the pill and the full-screen view fall back to the vendored level-M `qrcode.min.js` (no code in its centre), and the PNG download refuses rather than print a code-less QR (lock: the dialog arms in `tests/join-qr.spec.js`).
- **resvg trap:** rendering the certificate with a CROPPED `viewBox` (zoomed previews) has panicked resvg (`geom.rs`; the process aborts). Production renders only the full canvas; crop pixels after a full render instead. Space text with `dx` on `<tspan>`, never literal spaces: SVG strips whitespace at a tspan boundary.
- **`@resvg/resvg-js` loads lazily; a missing binary is `BadgeRenderUnavailable` (503), never a boot failure.** It cannot read woff2, so three Inter TTFs (400/600/800) ship in `lib/fonts/badge/` (not `public/`: no browser reads them, and the 17 MB CJK face would cost a compression per request); each needs name IDs 16/17 or `font-family="Inter"` reaches only Regular, and 700 resolves to ExtraBold (lock: `tests/badge-fonts.spec.js`). The certificate is set in Inter, the platform's family too (operator ruling, 29 Oct 2026, confirmed 17 Nov 2026): name ExtraBold, title SemiBold, recognition line Regular; `LICENSE-inter.txt` (SIL OFL 1.1) sits beside them; no serif or italic face ships. **Unmodified Noto Bold faces draw the scripts Inter lacks (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026), `FALLBACK_FACES`:** Arabic, Hebrew, Thai and one CJK face (Chinese, kana, Hangul); OFL 1.1 in `LICENSE-noto.txt`, each file's source URL and SHA-256 in `SOURCES-noto.txt` (lock-pinned). **resvg does not fall back per glyph:** at the first character Inter lacks it reshapes the WHOLE line with the first face having it, keeps that if every glyph matched, else copies glyphs in by position if their count is unchanged, else prints `.notdef` boxes. `_drawPlan` models this for `canRenderText`, `measureText` and `_nameExtentEm`, admitting a line only when Inter or ONE face draws all of it ("Dr. 张伟" is all the CJK face's; "Dr. محمد علي" falls back). **resvg also advances a cluster by its widest glyph**, so no Devanagari face ships (राहुल would print as रहुल), a spacing mark (Mc) is never drawable, and `escXml` decomposes Thai SARA AM before the renderer sees it (`_resvgText`, HarfBuzz's own Thai rule). A face for another script first needs the legibility arm: each sample drawn by its face alone and as wide as Chromium's shaper draws it, within 3%. `_fontFilesFor(svg)` hands a render only the faces its characters need (the CJK face costs ~20 ms a render), so a Latin certificate gets the three Inter files and renders byte for byte as before. Each face's measured `asc`/`desc` (`markAsc` for stacked Arabic marks; the CJK face's cover the Latin it draws) widen the name slot via `_nameExtentEm`, zero for an Inter-only name (lock: the face-extents and pixel-diff clearance arms of `tests/badge-fonts.spec.js`).
- **`renderBadgePdf` has no PDF dependency**; text strings are UTF-16BE (`_pdfTextString`). Fully async (`renderAsync`, promisified `zlib.deflate`); never `*Sync` on public routes.
- **A name is never cut:** `fitNameLines` shrinks and wraps to two lines, never ellipsises or upper-cases. `fitToWidth` ellipsises labels only; measure before `escXml`.
- **Font coverage:** if `canRenderText` fails, the nickname prints and freezes as the match parts (`_badgeMatchParts`; the mint's inline copy must change with it); its automatic congratulations email still goes out (operator ruling, 23 Nov 2026, sweep #437). Unknown metrics count as renderable. **A salutation that alone stops the name being drawn is left off** ("Dr. محمد علي" prints محمد علي; operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026); no other part or character is ever dropped ("Anna علي" prints the nickname). The mint answer's `name_fallback` / `salutation_dropped` (the share dialog's one note) are `_heldName`'s, read in `_badgePayload` for a held certificate too, as the dialog re-POSTs to open one; `name_fallback` blames the fonts, so it goes out only where they are why (not for a `_mintedWithNickname` certificate whose holder's name the fonts now draw; locks: `tests/certificate-scripts.api.spec.js`, `tests/certificate-name-notes.spec.js`). **A certificate frozen with the nickname (match parts `{given: "", family: <nickname>}`, `_mintedWithNickname`) keeps Inter-only coverage and leaves no salutation off at render** (`certificatePrintedName(…, {fallbackFaces:false})`), so one minted before the fallback faces still prints the nickname its lookup matches until Inter draws the holder's name (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026: issued certificates keep what they printed); every other certificate follows the holder's live name (lock: `tests/certificate-scripts.api.spec.js`). A name in a script no face draws (Devanagari, Georgian, Tamil, an emoji) still falls back; specs use a Georgian name as that fixture.
- **Lock artwork by rendering it, not by scanning text:** `tests/certificate-code-artefact.spec.js` asserts clearing `public_code` changes the PDF bytes, after a control that one badge renders byte-identically twice; `tests/certificate-config.spec.js` renders each tier twice (byte-identical) and pairwise distinct, and asserts an event override changes, and clearing it restores, a minted certificate's PDF.

### Event team and persona reports
