`lib/event-team.js` owns the data, `lib/team-reports.js` the queue and the sends, `lib/team-report-mail.js` what each persona is told and how it looks, `lib/qualifying.js` the questions, their order, the hot rule and the opener, `routes/event-team.js` the routes, `public/js/features/event-team.js` the workspace section `admin-event-team-tab`. Locks: `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`, `tests/event-team-ui.spec.js`, `tests/event-team-section-ui.spec.js`, `tests/qualifying-organiser-views.spec.js`, `tests/pick-one-authoring.spec.js`.

- **Team members are recipients, not accounts** (name + address per role; no login). Admins and superadmins add or remove them and use Send now on any event, event admins on their own (`events.edit`, `scoped`, `competitionDenied`); the competitor lock binds every tier, and the wire's `can_manage_team`/`manage_refusal` come from it (operator ruling, confirmed 17 Nov 2026; the organiser grant stays global-admin only). **Adding a recipient and Send now also take what reading the reports takes** (`_reportDataRefusal`): `participants.view`, plus `export.data` for a Marketing or SDR member or a send to either, asked by role even when no lead makes a file (those reports carry Marketing's CRM file and an SDR's own leads; `_sendCarriesFile`); the GET's `add_refusal`, `send_refusal` and per-role `refusal` let the section withhold each with its reason. **Since event admins add recipients of participant data, the allowed-domain list (`settings.team_report_allowed_domains`, superadmin only) bounds them, asked at add AND at send**; a removed domain stops the mail (`refused` in the ledger). An audit row names the member id and domain, never the address (nor does the mail's `email_sent`: see *The send chokepoint*). **A removed member's address leaves the event's send ledger** (the recent sends; sweep #149, operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026): once it holds no role on that event's team, its rows become `removed:<tm_ fingerprint>:<domain>` (`lib/team-reports.js` `forgetRemovedRecipient`; the fingerprint is the mail's own `to_fp`), read as "removed member (<domain>)". Never delete them: they are the once-only key and what `_nudgeWentOut` reads, and the address added back takes them back (`restoreRemovedRecipient`). Rows an older release left under a removed member's address were rewritten once at boot (`db.js`, marker `team_report_removed_recipients_at`) with the form, fingerprint and domain of `lib/team-recipient.js`, their one definition: it requires only crypto, so db.js shares it with the live paths (a second copy would leave rows the address added back cannot find). A claim sends nothing to an address no longer holding the role its message was composed for (removed mid-pass), and a send in flight settles on the rewritten row (lock: the sweep #149 tests in `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`, the boot rewrite and the one-definition scanner included). The lead CSV's header row is fixed: built-in questions by key, an organiser's own in "Other answers".
- **A lead row names the person's other events (`other_events`, `first_seen_event`/`first_seen_source`) only to a reader who may open them** (operator ruling, 15 Nov 2026): `buildReportData` names none (fail closed; what it needs sits in a WeakMap, never on a row a route spreads); each reader is given `reportForReader(data, canSee)`: the actor's scope (`canSeeScope(scopedCompetitionIds(req))`) in the app and the download, each recipient's account (`memberEventScope`, the CTA rule; none without an account) in `composeRun`, and both at once in the preview (`readerScope`, which also narrows its managers' comparison as `?peer=1` does). **A sent manager's comparison covers only the events their APPOINTER may open now** (`managerPeerScope`, `lib/team-reports.js`: `added_by` through `memberEventScope`, the built-in superadmin every event; also the manager's own events when their address is an account scoped to its events), or an organiser could add their own address and be mailed other organisers' figures (lock: `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`, "a manager's comparison covers only the events their appointer may open"). An earlier event outside reads `"Another event"` (lock: the last describe of `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`).
- **A qualifying challenge is an ordinary challenge tagged `qualifying:<key>`,** parsed server-side like `category:`; participants get a boolean `qualifying`, never the tag. **Only a pick-one question can be one** (`qualifyingNeedsPickOneDenied`, `lib/qualifying.js`: the reports read an answer as the place of the option picked): the tag route and the task PUT refuse 400 `qualifying_needs_pick_one`, restore 409, the task import skips and counts (`qualifying_not_pick_one_skipped`; and, as each event holds its own same-titled copy and a row names a challenge by title, an overwrite that could mean several where a qualifying question is involved, `qualifying_copy_skipped`, and a row adding a malformed `qualifying:` tag, `qualifying_tag_invalid_skipped`; sweep #413); a PUT changing neither type nor options is not asked, so an older one of another type stays editable, and its hint costs nothing, as Recalculate already priced it (lock: `tests/qualifying-pick-one.spec.js`). Create seeds per-event copies of `DEFAULT_QUALIFYING` (`add_qualifying`, else the kind's seed); a template spawn seeds them only for an explicit `add_qualifying:true` (absent: none; the create form and the quick spin offer the box on every create, kind-seeded). **It scores 0 (submit and Recalculate) and is excluded, not "trivially satisfied", from every count of what finishes an event: `notQualifyingSql` at `publishedTaskCount`, `participantSolvedAll`, `participantsFinishedAllDetail`, the leaderboard's finish time, the submit fast path and `participantAttempted`, its answer and its hint query alike (and `lateEntryStateFor`, `_badgeEndDate`, the Overview pulse; scanner: `tests/qualifying-counting-sites.spec.js` holds the named sites and flags any other `.prepare(` counting an event's challenges or a participant's correct answers without a recorded EXEMPT; a count of another shape, such as solves read from `challenge_events`, is held only once named; behaviour: `tests/qualifying-exclusions.api.spec.js`); only its FIRST answer may run the all-solved scan, and the early end waits until every scoring participant has answered every published one (`qualifyingAnsweredAll`, `lib/qualifying.js`; operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026), a changed answer completing nothing.** A new counting site uses it too, or a marketing question moves a tie-break or withholds a certificate. Adding or removing the tag is a scoring change: an ended session freezes it at the tag routes and restore (409 `qualifying_tag_frozen`), and a restore that changes it renumbers the challenge as they do. A change that flips whether the challenge is qualifying (the tag routes, restore and the task import's overwrite, which also clears its numbers) re-prices its STORED answers and hint costs in every event holding it as Recalculate would, in the write's transaction (`lib/qualifying-rescore.js`; points only, never `is_correct`), records `scores_recalculated` on each event whose rows moved and busts each holding board after commit; a LOCKED challenge refuses the flip (403 `challenge_locked`, every tier, as Recalculate; restore and the import already skip it) (sweep #91; lock: `tests/qualifying-tag-rescore.api.spec.js`). A staff-compete event refuses an explicit `add_qualifying` (`QUALIFYING_IN_COMPETE_EVENT_ERROR`) and skips the default: it admits sealed challenges only, and a qualifying question (a pick-one Any answer) can never be sealed, so it asks none. **The reports never read a SEALED qualifying challenge's answers** (an older or imported row; `_qualifyingRows`' `withheldIds`, kept out of the scoring figures too), nor a sealed free answer's (lock: `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`, "a sealed qualifying question's picks…").
- **They come last, in a fixed order: numbered in their own level `4.NN` (`QUALIFYING_NUMBER_LEVEL`, `compareQualifyingKeys`, `_assignTaskNumbers`),** so a scoring challenge added later still numbers ahead of them with no special case in `compareTaskNumbers` or its client mirror. The tag routes clear the number on a change (`db.js` moved older ones once: `qualifying_numbers_moved_at`). `GET /api/tasks` sorts with `compareTaskNumbers`, which the list, the play bar and Next all follow.
- **To a participant it is a Quick question** (`_isQualifying`, `features/participant-tasks.js`): no difficulty chip, attempt count, rating, points or "Correct"; `showThanks`, not `showCongrats` (the submit answers `qualifying:true`); out of the difficulty chips, in All. The participant's own counts leave it out too (`_runTasks`; the progress record's `quick` mask).
- **"Played" means answered anything, a qualifying question included; challenge figures are scoring challenges only.** **The report ranks on the boards the event ranks on** (operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026, "Rank per session"): `lib/leaderboard.js` `rankedBoards` (the boards the mint's `placementPodium` reads too) and `boardSlotOf`. `podium` is one group per board with a place, `{session, late, places}` (each session's in per_slot mode, the late entrants' once their window has closed), places by `earnsPlacement`; each lead's `rank` is on their own board, with `rank_session`/`rank_late`. A global or sessionless event has one unnamed group (lock: the per-session describe of `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`, a two-session per_slot event against a global twin).
- **Its answer can be changed while they can still play (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026):** the submit stores a new pick on a qualifying pick-one question as another row, past already-solved and the attempt cap (never on a scoring challenge, nor a qualifying question that is not a pick; one predicate, `changeableQuickQuestion` in `lib/qualifying.js`, for the submit, the task GET and the list's `out_of_attempts`); the reports and `my_choice_ids` read the latest (`submitted_at, rowid`). A pick is compared with that latest answer, never the 3 s fold: the same pick is no change at any age (`deduped`, `qualifying`, nothing stored), any other is, even back to one made a moment ago. The task GET's `answer_changeable` offers Change answer / Keep my answer, the tap card naming the current pick.
- **Analytics leaves qualifying challenges out** (`whereSql`, a 100% row would measure nothing), and its filter bar offers neither a qualifying challenge nor its tag. The workspace Overview's pulse still counts them as answers (accepted). **Organiser screens know a qualifying challenge by its tag through one client test, `ui/task-search.js` `isQualifyingTag`/`isQualifyingTask`; never a copy** (locks: a source scanner and, by behaviour, View as participant in `tests/qualifying-organiser-views.spec.js`, Analytics in `tests/analytics-sort-export.spec.js`).
- **`classifyLead` reads an answer by its POSITION in the author's order, so once anyone has answered a qualifying question its options keep their places (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026):** the task PUT and restore refuse a move, a removal, an insert above another option or dropping them all (409 `qualifying_options_fixed`, `lib/qualifying.js` `answeredOptionsChangeDenied`/`qualifyingOptionsFixed`); the task import's overwrite skips and counts it (`qualifying_options_fixed_skipped`). A reword keeps the option's id (the editor's `_anyOptionsFromForm` keeps a line's id by its text, else by its line, but only when that accounts for every stored option: a removal makes it a guess); one may be added at the end. An ordinary pick-one question is not bound. The library row's `options_fixed` drives the editor's note and pre-save check, sent only where `can_edit_scoring` holds (whether anybody answered is not told to a caller the PUT's scope gate refuses first).
- **An event asks each qualifying question once (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026):** another event's may be added (the wording is shared, answers stay per event), never one whose key the event already holds through any challenge, a draft included (`qualifyingKeyHeldDenied`). The one, PUT and bulk assign doors refuse it (400 `qualifying_key_held`; bulk `skipped.qualifyingHeld`, in its toast), as do the tag route and restore (409, naming another organiser's event only to its organisers: `_heldTopicClause`); the task import's overwrite skips and counts it (`qualifying_key_held_skipped`, in its summary). Add from bank leaves such questions out and lets one per topic be ticked, and a single question's Add to events dialog disables the event, both from the bank row's `qualifying_keys` (the server's parse). Clone and spawn never share one.
- **`enqueueCertificateDispatch` also calls `enqueueTeamReport`**, before its own Program early return, so a new end path needs nothing more. A Program completer's call (`userId`) queues nothing.
- **Worker rules as in *Automatic issue and the congratulations email*:** decide at run time; a revived event's job, of any kind (the nudge included), is dropped, and so is a wrapup, delta or nudge whose event ended before a mark-sent restore's floor (`restoreMailFloor`, never the grandfather floor), whose week's Monday digest also counts as sent. `team_report_runs`: once per (event, kind, end key). `team_report_sends`: claimed `sending` before the send, a leftover marked `unknown` at boot and never retried, and a pass a shutdown cuts keeps its job (lock: `tests/team-report-delivery.spec.js`, with the allowed-domain look-alikes, the `late:` deferral, Send now stamping nothing, the cut wrap-up's re-run stamping nobody and Close late entries now re-keying the `late:` delta); a `failed` row is retried by the job's next pass (`MAX_PASSES` 3, after 5 and 30 min), the run row waiting for a pass with nothing failed, or the last. A pass that THROWS is retried after 30 min and, at its `MAX_PASSES`-th, given up: job dropped (by `enqueued_at`), a warning logged, `team_report_given_up` on the event's History (`_crashedJob`). **"Reported" means received:** a run stamps `first_reported_at` (its FIRST delivery's `sent_at`) only on the leads that pass built and a later pass stamps nothing, so a lead who joined meanwhile waits for a delta (a wrap-up leaving such leads after the late window closed queues one; an end's report, the wrap-up or a later end's `end:` delta, queues the `late:` delta for a window still open, as that end's delta replaced any pending one), and a delta's later pass re-sends its first delivery's leads to whoever is retried (lock: `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`). Send now (`runManualReport`) answers within 10 s (`deferred`; the rest runs under the shutdown drain), refuses a second while one runs (409 `team_report_send_running`), answers 500 `team_report_send_failed` on a part-way failure, and is recorded on the History either way. Kinds: `wrapup` (end + 15 min), `delta` (only leads with `first_reported_at` NULL; keys `late:<deadline>`, `end:<end>`; Close late entries now re-keys a pending `late:` delta to the deadline it stored, due 15 minutes later), `weekly` (Program), `nudge` (48 h after a wrapup, or after a re-end's `end:` delta while none has reached anyone (`_nudgeWentOut`: a ledger row `sent`, `sending` or `unknown`; one that ran with nobody waiting does not count), never a second: owners, plus a count to Marketing, of "waiting" hot leads only, defined below; none waiting sends nothing — operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026), `manual` (Send now; stamps nothing as reported). Dry-run events and empty teams send nothing. A PUT moving an ended event's end re-queues its report for the new end (`routes/competitions.js`; operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026): the pending job, keyed on the old end, would be dropped as stale.
- **A read stores nothing:** `GET …/team-report` shows the split the next report would make (`owner_preview`); `persistLeadOwners` runs only in a report. Owners already stored are kept, so a late update never reshuffles a share.
- **A report is a list of blocks (`para`, `kpis`, `bars`, `flag`, `table`, `section`, `leadCard`) that each render to both parts of the email,** so the text cannot drift and `E` is the one escape. Inline styles and table layout; **a value inside `style="…"` uses single quotes only** (a double-quoted font name ended the attribute, leaving a serif page and a blue link; the UI spec reads the button's computed style). `DARK_SHEET` is for clients that honour it; every colour is also inline. `previewHtml` strips it for the app's frame, whose inherited CSP would refuse a `<style>` element.
- **Preview email is a `sandbox=""` iframe filled by `setSafeSrcdoc` (`core/trusted-types.js`, refuses an unsandboxed frame),** a sink `tests/html-sink-scan.js` knows. `#modal-box`'s width rule holds it at 420 px whatever `wide` says, so the dialog sets the width and hands it back on close.
- **The in-app persona views show the email's own call to action** (`focus` on `GET …/team-report`), so the two cannot disagree. `?peer=1` answers only the managers' comparison, fetched once per event when that view opens and kept by the client across reloads: it rebuilds up to three other events' reports, narrowed to a scoped actor's own events (an average of one event is that event's figures), so `peerAverage` caches it (`_peerCache`). **Email tables stay at three or four columns and KPI tiles flow as inline-blocks, so a mail fits a 360 px phone with no media query** (a client may strip the sheet; lock: `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`).
- **One meaning of "waiting" (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026): a hot lead still to follow up is one `lib/event-team.js` `isContactable` admits (not marked contacted, not opted out)** in every count (48 h reminder, SDR report, managers' "Hot still to contact" row and Assign count, in-app SDR tile and Managers' row). The app takes each lead's verdict from the server, never a copy (`contactable` on `GET …/team-report`; the contacted mark's answer carries it only for a caller holding `participants.view`, as it tells whether they opted out); the mark's answer updates only the report it was sent against (the same person can be a lead in another event). Locks (`tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`): swapping `T.isContactable` in process moves every mail's count, the reminder's included; the routes are beyond a swap's reach, so a source arm pins every `contactable` the server sends to one call of `isContactable` or `leadContactable` (its verdict over the reports' own opted-out test, `_optedOutOf`); screens: `tests/event-team-ui.spec.js`.
- **One link per email, and only where it opens (operator ruling, 14 Nov 2026):** the call to action is in the body, and no other `<a>` may be added (lock asserts it per persona); it is a button only for a recipient whose address is an account able to open the event (`lib/event-team.js` `memberOpensEvent`: the PRIMARY address, `requireAdmin`'s re-read, `events.view`, in scope); anyone else gets the step in words to act on without the page (`cta.textNoLink` where the button's words need it) and no link at all (preview `cta_link`). An SDR's card carries each lead's address, and an SDR's report (not the 48 h reminder, not Marketing standing in) attaches a file of their own leads. A wrong answer to a sealed challenge is counted, never quoted, in the mail and in the app. The report reads and the CSV take the competitor lock: they hold rivals' answers (lock: `tests/competitor-lock-event-reads.api.spec.js`: the preview, the lead file and the contacted mark, every tier, beside the scope refusal; through an organiser's panel the two reads ask the seat and the hand, while the mark meets the impersonation write guard first, so only its door's seat half is held, through the built-in superadmin's hand; the lead file's `data_exported` carries the request context).
- **Email links are `/?event=<id>&section=<key>`,** read at boot and consumed once an admin nav is built (`features/event-team.js`), through `openEventWorkspace`, which first loads the events model if it lacks the event (without its row there is no name or status strip, and no pane loader finds it); they carry no capability.
- **`task_feedback` has no comment column,** so low ratings are reported without comments.
- **A No to the follow-up quick question leaves the person out of every lead list** (operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026): `followUpDeclined` (the built-in `follow_up`'s second option, by position); `buildReportData` keeps them in `data.declined`, never `data.leads`, so no report, file, card, owner split or lead count holds them, and only `summarise`'s joined, played and answer counts read both. Where people join an event with a team the join card says what it receives (the token preview's `shares_leads`, `sharesLeads`; a signed-in join, which shows no card, gets it as a toast); a participant an organiser adds is told nowhere (lock: the follow-up describe of `tests/event-team-reports.spec.js`).

### Email and alerts

EMAIL_RULES.md owns address rules (format, uniqueness, primary/secondary, change confirmation); its §7 lists every outbound mail. A new `sendEmailLogged`/`sendEmailBackground` reason needs a line there plus a scenario (or an `EXEMPT` entry with its reason) in `tests/email-scenarios.spec.js`; the spec's manifest is checked both ways against the reasons in the code. The spec reads what a capturing relay received: `email_sent` cannot tell "not sent" from "not logged".

#### Sender and transport
- **Build From only through `resolveSender()`, as the object `{name, address}`; never concatenate:** nodemailer re-parses a string From, so a platform name holding `<addr>` would take over the visible sender and MAIL FROM. Name `_safeDisplayName(getPlatformName())`, read per send; address the relay in force's sender (`_senderAddress`, the export's `SENDER_ADDRESS` getter): `EMAIL_FROM` parsed once at boot by `_extractSenderAddress` (bare or `Name <addr>`, name ignored), or the bare address a superadmin saved with the relay (next bullets). Both shapes are tested: a lost branch falls back silently (lock: `tests/email-sender-name.spec.js`). `lib/email.js`.
- **`PATCH /api/admin/settings` refuses a platform name with control characters (CR/LF included) or over 120 characters** (`#settings-platform-name` has that maxlength). `_safeDisplayName` strips control characters at the sink (legacy rows, `COMPETITION_NAME` fallback), uncapped: a third limit would drift. `<`, `>`, quotes and commas stay legal (`resolveSender` makes them safe). `routes/settings.js`.
- **One sender ladder**, shared by `sendEmailLogged` and `/api/admin/test-email`; `from` is spread last, so no caller can override it. `_bulkEmailHeaders` takes the send's resolved `from.address` as given.
- **No Reply-To and no `envelope` option:** replies go to the sender address; the relay signs DKIM.
- **The relay in force is ONE decision, `effectiveSmtpConfig()` (`lib/email.js`; owner decision, 27 Nov 2026): a relay a superadmin saved in Settings → Email (SMTP) (`settings.smtp_config`, `lib/smtp-settings.js`) replaces `SMTP_HOST`/`SMTP_PORT`/`SMTP_USER`/`SMTP_PASS`/`EMAIL_FROM` as a whole, never field by field; else the environment.** Boot stops FATAL only when neither names a relay. While the saved password cannot be decrypted (`.smtp-key` is BACKUP_EXEMPT, so a restore or a move loses it) and the environment names a relay, the environment's relay is used and the card says so. `SMTP_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT` and the pool sizes stay env-only. Routes: `routes/smtp-settings.js` (GET `settings.view`; PUT, DELETE and the unsaved-values test `ops.smtp`, non-grantable, superadmin only).
- **A save swaps the pool live (`reloadSmtpTransport`): `transporter` is a stable facade; each send counts on the pool it started on, and a replaced pool is closed only once its last send is answered** (nodemailer's `close()` fails a message still in its queue). The slot gate spans pools. The health state restarts at null and, outside tests, a probe runs (lock: `tests/smtp-settings-delivery.spec.js`).
- **The saved password never leaves the server and is sent only to the relay it was saved for:** encrypted at rest (AES-256-GCM, `DATA_DIR/.smtp-key`, 0600, `wx`); reads say only `password_set`; `smtp_config`, `smtp_password` and `password_enc` are on `_REDACT_KEYS` (the settings export redacts the row); the audit (`smtp` settings event) names changed fields and only the host, port, security and sender values. An empty password box keeps the password in force only for the same host and username, else 400 `smtp_password_required`; relay error text has it (and its base64) cut out. Locks: `tests/smtp-settings-delivery.spec.js` (no file under DATA_DIR holds it), `tests/smtp-settings.api.spec.js`.
- **Subjects keep the `${platformName} — <thing>` prefix:** to shorten a subject, shorten the platform name. The IP-ban alert builds its own.
- **The transport is a pool** (`pool: true`; `SMTP_POOL_MAX_CONNECTIONS` default 16, twice the bulk fan-out's 8 lanes; `SMTP_POOL_MAX_MESSAGES` default 100): a sign-in burst opens at most that many relay connections and queues the rest in memory, in `sendEmailLogged` before the erasure and opt-out checks (`hack_arena_smtp_sends_pending`). Shutdown calls `closeTransport()` after the mail drain, which still waits for a pooled send (lock: `tests/smtp-pool-drain.spec.js`; the bound, six held mails over two relay connections, queue counted: `tests/smtp-pool-bound.spec.js`). Never set it under 8: `tests/email-scenarios.spec.js` parks eight sends at once.
- **SMTP sets `requireTLS`, failing the send rather than downgrade** (port 465: implicit TLS): opportunistic STARTTLS would let a fake relay collect AUTH credentials and links. Sole opt-out: `SMTP_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT=1`, never inferred from `NODE_ENV`.
- **`#smtp-banner` renders only when `smtp_healthy === false`;** `null` (the state after every boot) means the probe has not run.

#### The send chokepoint (`sendEmailLogged`)
- **Every attempt is recorded, even with a null userId** (invitations, the IP-ban alert): with a user as `user_events` `email_sent`, without one as an orphaned audit event carrying the address, except an address proof's (see *Email first — the address proof and the sign-up grant*) and an event team report's (`event_team_report`: the domain and a `tm_` fingerprint). Suppressions likewise (`skipped:"unsubscribed"` / `email_suppressed`).
- **It returns `false` on an SMTP error or a suppression; the SMTP call never throws out,** but its pre-send DB lookups can reject, so callers attach a `.catch`. Count deliveries by summing return values, never by `recipients.length`.
- **Erasure is checked once the send holds a relay slot (a queued mail is checked when it goes out, not when it was queued) and again after it** (`_recipientErased`; lock: `tests/smtp-pool-bound.spec.js`). Already erased: nothing is sent; an orphaned `email_suppressed` (`skipped:"recipient_deleted"`) is written without the address. Erased mid-send: an orphaned `email_sent` replaces the `user_events` row, which has no FK, so a late one would revive an erased user's history.
- **`_BULK_EMAIL_REASONS` is the opt-out set:** only those reasons carry List-Unsubscribe and are suppressed for an unsubscribed address; every other reason is transactional, sent to everyone. Deliberately transactional: `account_approved_bulk`, the same mail as `account_approved` (one operator button must not have two opt-out behaviours); `event_team_report`, internal staff mail to the allowed domains, whose footer (no link) tells a recipient to ask the event's organiser to remove them (operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026). Deliberately bulk: `certificate_awarded`, announcing a certificate the holder can always collect (an opted-out address still gets the certificate).
- **Invitations keep opt-out apart from delivery failure** (`isUnsubscribedAddress`). `unsubscribedInvites` gets no registration link, since sharing it by hand would bypass the opt-out; `undeliveredInvites` gets it, withheld (`linkWithheld: true`) when the actor's scope does not cover the whole invitation (`_linkRevealable`).
- **`sendLoginCodeEmail` returns the promise; `POST /api/auth/code/request` must not await it:** the handler answers `{ok:true}` whatever the account state, and waiting would let response time reveal the outcome. `sendEmailBackground` returns undefined, so never use it where delivery is reported.

#### Detached sends and fan-out
- **Wrap every detached send in `trackDetachedSend()` (`lib/util.js`) with its `.catch` already attached:** shutdown waits on `detachedFanoutPending()` and `reconcileDrainPending()`, and an untracked send is silently lost when it closes the DB. Known exception, not yet tracked: the IP-ban alert (`deliverAlert` in `server.js` `_raiseIpBanAlert`).
- **Increment a detached-work counter before `setImmediate`, not inside the callback,** or the drain cannot see a queued pass.
- **Bulk assign, approve and invite commit first, then fan out through `_runBoundedWithDeadline` with a 10 s deadline** (`EMAIL_FANOUT_DEADLINE_MS` in `routes/participants.js`; `routes/invitations.js` hard-codes `10_000`): sends pending at the deadline carry on detached and the response sets `emailsDeferred: true`, so its counts are a floor. The browser gives up at 30 s; a client timeout on a committed operation invites a duplicate re-send. **A request reporting ONE mail waits through `awaitDeliveryWithDeadline` (`lib/util.js`; grep it for the doors), 10 s by default**: `true`/`false`, or `null` while queued (`emailDeferred` / `deferred:true`); a send that REJECTS (its own lookups threw before the relay) reads `false`, logged at warn. The superadmin's test email (`POST /api/admin/test-email`) is deliberately unbounded: it reports the relay's own answer, and pressing again only sends another test. The email-code door's `send_failed` lifts its re-send suppression (`liftResendSuppression`).

#### Wording at send time, per recipient
- **Dates use the recipient's zone via `formatEmailDate(d, tz)`;** a missing or unusable zone falls back to UTC and names it. Invitations (no recipient row) render in UTC.
- **The late-entry line is decided per recipient** (`lateEntryMailLineFor`); invitations use the event-level `lateEntryMailLine`.
- **Reminder lead-time wording comes from `reminderLeadTime` at send time:** a reminder sent under 55 minutes (`REMINDER_ABOUT_AN_HOUR_MIN`) before the start states the real minutes, not "1 hour". Once its start (the session's for a sessioned reminder) is reached it answers null and the claimed row is never mailed: settled `sent=1` with a `reminder_skipped` user event in ONE transaction (on a failure the claim is released for the next tick).
- **The reminder claim and mark-delivered UPDATEs are bound to the exact `send_at` and `timeslot_id`,** so a row rescheduled mid-batch is skipped, not salvaged; a new writer that moves a pending reminder must DELETE the row or accept a one-tick skip. The event and session names are re-read after each claim (`namesNowStmt`), never taken from the batch's read (lock: `tests/reminder-name-at-send.spec.js`).
- **Build labels from raw values and escape once, at the HTML body.** Subjects are never HTML-escaped. `buildEmail`'s `greeting`, `linkText` and `fallbackNote` are plain text it escapes for the HTML part; `body` is its one HTML argument, decoded for the text part with `&amp;` last (lock: the fallbackNote source arm of `tests/certificate-dispatch.spec.js`).

#### Unsubscribe
- **`GET /api/unsubscribe` only renders a confirmation form; only `POST` changes anything** (RFC 8058 one-click): scanners and link prefetchers GET every URL in an email.
- **An emailed GET (new ones included) may act only if it passes three tests:** it does what the recipient asked for, grants no further capability, and repeating it reports the truth (idempotent). `GET /verify` passes (see *Authentication & authorization*); unsubscribe and `/confirm-email` fail and keep the GET-then-POST shape.
- **Unsubscribe pages use a small self-contained shell, not the SPA**, because they must work signed out and with JS disabled.
- **`GET /api/admin/unsubscribed` returns `total` as a real `COUNT(*)`, with `shown`, `truncated` and `limit` (1000), and the UI says when the list is cut:** a silently cut list makes an admin conclude a missing address never opted out. `routes/admin-misc.js`.

#### Unsubscribe secret
- **Tokens are HMAC-signed with `DATA_DIR/.unsubscribe-secret`** (0600, created on first boot), not derived from `SESSION_SECRET`, so restarts never break 365-day URLs already sent (loss and backup: see *Data storage*).

#### IP-ban alert (`lib/admin-alerts.js`)
- **Every active superadmin (not blocked or denied, primary address) is always emailed.** `channel` only records the primary surface (`in_app` when a live admin session exists). Never suppress the mail for a signed-in admin: a live session does not mean anyone is watching, and the organiser is often on the blocked address, their own poll getting 429s.
- **`deliverAlert` owns the per-recipient fan-out**, with a try/catch per recipient so one bad address does not lose the rest; `emailed` counts real successes.
- **`alertCopy` is the one source of the mail's wording, mirrored by the modal (`public/js/features/admin-alerts.js`).** `verdict` comes from the probe ratio alone, so `mixed` can carry `routed_4xx === 0`; both say some traffic "reached real pages" only when `routed_4xx > 0`. (mirror-lock limits: see *Stated gaps — what these do NOT cover*)
- **Dismissal has two tiers:** any admin dismisses for themselves; global dismissal is superadmin-only (403 otherwise) and suppresses that IP for the TTL. Alerts clear themselves when the block lapses. State is in-process on purpose, because the ban it describes is too.
- **The copy must never recommend a control the platform will refuse** (it checks `accepts_trusted_ips`, `already_trusted` and the READER's `can_set_trusted_ips`, set per caller by `GET /api/admin/alerts`: `events.edit`, in scope, not competing; one who cannot is told to ask an administrator who can).

### CxOne (Checkmarx One) provisioning

`lib/cxone.js` provisions; `lib/cxone-identity.js` holds the pure identity, group and adoption rules; `lib/cxone-ledger.js` and `lib/cxone-withdraw.js` remove accounts the platform created (operator-facing scope of removal: `AGENT_AUDIT_MEMORY.md`).

#### CxOne account-creation opt-out
- **`competitions.cxone_disabled` and the kind's `cxoneProvisioning` are enforced at ONE gate:** `cxOneProvisionUser` returns before any work unless `cxoneProvisioningApplies(comp)` (`lib/event-policy.js`: the kind allows accounts AND the column is 0; a Booth Event never creates them, owner's decision, 27 Nov 2026; `_provisioningScopeRevoked` asks the same). Create and PUT refuse a Booth Event with accounts on (400 `cxone_provisioning_forbidden`), store 1 whatever is sent otherwise, a kind change to booth turns them off (recorded in `edited`), the per-event key override PUT is refused for booth (DELETE stays open), templates refuse it and a clone into booth names `cxone_provisioning` in `forced_settings`. Lock: `tests/booth-cxone-forbidden.api.spec.js`. Every trigger goes through `enqueueCxOneProvision`; a new provisioning function skipping the gate is not covered.
- **Never `await cxOneProvisionUser` inline:** the upstream hops outlast the SPA's 30 s abort, and the shutdown drain sees only queued work. Where: the bounded queue `CXONE_MAX_INFLIGHT`.
- **Fail closed before `getCxOneConfig`:** return when a supplied `competitionId` has no row (it would fall back to the global credentials), the user row is gone, or `_provisioningScopeRevoked` says so. A call with no `competitionId` is the legitimate global-default path.
- **`_provisioningScopeRevoked` asks in a fixed order (event exists, not opted out, still a member),** since deleting an event deletes its membership rows. Asked at the queue wait, after the token and before the grant; it never withdraws (leaving an event never removes a realm account). Enqueue sites write the membership row first.
- **An event deleted mid-provisioning throws (`if (!comp) throw`). Never write `!comp ||`:** it grants the global default group under a deleted event. An existing event with no group of its own still falls back to that group.
- **`cxoneDisabledDefault` applies only when `cxone_disabled === undefined`.** The create form seeds the checkbox from the kind's default, an edit shows the stored value (see *The three event kinds — the policy table*). The CxOne section renders for every admin tier where the kind's `cxone_provisioning` is `allowed` (none for a Booth Event, so its save sends no `cxone_disabled`); ticking `#comp-cxone-disabled` disables the override fields.
- Having no participant notice or consent checkbox is accepted, and so are older Booth Events and Programs whose stored 0 was never normalised (see *Accepted audit dispositions (intentional, do not re-flag)*).

#### CxOne API key discipline
- **`getCxOneConfig` has two key tiers:** the event's own key when it decodes, else the global default key in `settings`.
- **Never send a raw key.** `GET /api/admin/competitions` SELECTs `cxone_api_key` to compute `cxone_configured` / `cxone_has_api_key`, so destructure it off before any spread. `_REDACT_KEYS` (`lib/log.js`) scrubs both key names from logs and exports.
- **`PUT|DELETE /api/admin/competitions/:id/cxone` is ADMIN-level by design; do not tighten it.** `/api/admin/default-cxone` is superadmin-only. Both validate with `decodeCxOneApiKey`, which accepts only Checkmarx hosts (`CXONE_HOSTNAME_ALLOWED`).
- **`sanitiseCxOneError` is the only error sink:** it redacts `Bearer` and `refresh_token=` values, caps the text at 500 chars and feeds both `console.error` and the `cxone_provisioned` error the History dialog renders verbatim. The cap fits the remedy sentence at ordinary name and address lengths; very long addresses can still push it past.

#### CxOne username stability
- **`competition_users.cxone_username` beats the current nickname** for create and search and is written back on success, so the realm identity survives a rename.
- **`cxone_user_id` records WHICH account,** compared against prior bindings across ALL competitions, as a returning participant has no row yet. A change is reported (`cxone_user_id_changed_from`, present only then), never refused: a legitimate re-point looks the same as an accident. Not backfilled: NULL means the binding predates the column.
- **Proof standard (`cxOneMatchQuality`): only the verified primary address (`emails`) is proof.** A match on the secondary or an unverified primary (`assertedEmails`) returns `unverified_email`, deliberately absent from `CXONE_ACCEPTABLE_BASES`. A username match counts only with `hasPriorBinding`, because nicknames are only locally unique and the secondary is never confirmed. Resolve the addresses inside `cxOneProvisionUser` from the user id, never a call-site SELECT, and pass them to the pure classifier.
- **The Keycloak search covers the username and every address, with `exact=true`:** safe only because of the proof split, it exists to name the found account in the refusal. `exact=true` is not the identity fix: its filters are contains-matches capped at 100, and the classifier re-filters to exact equality.
- **`resolveCxOneGroup`: an exact name match wins, else a single case-folded match; an ambiguous fold is refused.** A loose match reports `group_matched_ignoring_case` and `group_actual_name`. History records `group_name` / `group_assigned` only when a group is configured, and a missing grant never renders as success.
- **Re-ask erasure after every guarded await, before the next remote call or durable write:** `_userStillExists` before anything exists in the realm (after the token), `_erasedMidFlight` after. The group lookup asks after `groupsResp.json()`, as a fetch resolves at the headers. Sole exception: the accepted-search branch, where `transferTo` guards itself by requiring the owner to exist. The catch asks before `console.error`, because refusals carry the erased name and address.
- The matrix is unit-tested at module scope, because `CXONE_HOSTNAME_ALLOWED` refuses a localhost IDP. Only a source arm in `tests/cxone-binding.spec.js` holds the cross-competition changed-from lookup; the id write-back is also asserted through `tests/cxone-provision-harness.cjs`.

#### F2 — a deleted participant's Checkmarx One account is removed from the tenant
- **Operator ruling: deleting a participant removes every account the platform CREATED, of any age.** An already existing account that was only matched (`already_existed:true`) is the tenant administrator's.
- **The ledger is `cxone_realm_accounts`,** keyed on `cxone_user_id` alone with no foreign key (a row must outlive its participant) and not exported (it holds local user ids; an import mints fresh ones). Rows are created, transferred, stamped and owner-deleted through the pure `lib/cxone-ledger.js`; the runner's state writes (claims, realm confirmation, failures, holds, overrides, dismissal) are in `lib/cxone-withdraw.js`. `created_email_sha`, `created_baseline` and `held_signal` are the columns listed in `lib/schema-required.js`.
- **Record a row only for an account that is provably ours:** a 201 plus a UUID from the Location header of OUR create, recorded in that branch before any further await; never one found by a 409 match or by search. Store the realm origin, tenant, username and `createdEmailSha()`, never the address. A creating job also writes `created_baseline` (role ids and attribute names read back after the grant, an erasure ask after each read: stage `during_baseline`) through `recordCreationBaseline`, claim- and owner-bound and write-once.
- **Deletion is an explicit marker:** `stampDeletionForUser` runs inside the `DELETE FROM users` transaction and the runner is kicked after commit. A row whose user is gone without a marker is `unmarked`, never acted on: a missing user row proves nothing (a partial restore or a hand edit can cause it).
- **The runner takes one row at a time,** picked by a claim compare-and-swap that repeats the one selection predicate `_SELECTABLE` (stamped, not given up, unclaimed or stale, due by `next_attempt_at`, user absent). Each row goes through these steps in order:
  1. instance origin;
  2. a local living binder (transfer, no remote call), or a living participant's record naming the account that cannot be read (hold `binding_unreadable`, no remote call);
  3. credentials for the row's realm;
  4. the realm's own record;
  5. PROVENANCE (`PROVENANCE_WINDOW` and an equal username, never overridable);
  6. the same person (`_addressHolder` with `handOverOnly`): hand-over only, before any adoption sign, since it removes nothing and their own deletion re-runs every step (operator, 21 Oct 2026);
  7. ADOPTION;
  8. the address holder, then (DELETE path only, unless an override waives it) the role-visibility probe and the holder again;
  9. a synchronous re-check;
  10. announce (`beginRealmRemoval`), DELETE.
- **ADOPTION holds an account as `adopted_by_tenant`, naming the sign in `held_signal` (shown on the list row),** on any of: a federation link, an email no longer matching `created_email_sha`, a full page of groups, any group outside `_platformGroupNames(row)` (the realm's default groups dropped first), or the role, attribute and post-deletion credential signs tabled in `lib/cxone-identity.js` (operator rulings, 19 Oct 2026: existing credentials, required actions and `enabled` never hold). A credential the realm dates up to `CREDENTIAL_CLOCK_MARGIN_MS` (the provenance window's 15-minute lag) before `pending_since` counts as after it: two clocks are compared, and the margin leans to a hold. The group set is scoped to the row's own realm, so another tenant's same-named group cannot suppress the hold; a row with no realm gets an empty set. A failed or malformed read is classified, never "none".
- **Role visibility is proven, never assumed** (Keycloak 26.6.7+/26.7.3+ silently filter mappings the key may not view): before a DELETE the runner asks for the `realm-management` client and needs `access.view === true`, else the overridable `roles_unreadable` (operator ruling, 19 Oct 2026; its only source; a 401 retries); a hand-over never meets it. An empty `realmMappings` is `default_role_removed`; a 403 on roles or credentials is `account_details_refused` (`read_refused`'s remedy is false for a key that just read the user). A `roles_unreadable` override waives only the probe; an `adopted_by_tenant` one all of step 7 and the probe (a hidden role is one more adoption sign), signs added later included (design decision, not a ruling).
- **Only a living participant whose VERIFIED PRIMARY is the account's address, with matching first and last names, may take the account over.** Any other holder of the address holds it (`address_held_by_living_account`, re-checked weekly).
- **`livingBinder` is the one definition of "a living participant is bound":** a LIKE scan, so it is asked only where safety needs it (each row's local-binder check and synchronous re-check, and `_withdrawalOwnershipLost`) and never inside a request. **A living participant's `cxone_provisioned` record that names the account but cannot be read (not JSON, or not an object) answers `BINDING_UNREADABLE`, never "nobody"** (sweep #407, operator ruling 24 Nov 2026): unless a readable record or binding names someone (they get it), the runner holds the account `binding_unreadable` (overridable; the override waives only unreadable records) and the job's in-flight withdrawal skips. It is a Symbol: a caller asking only "is anybody bound?" stops, and one taking it for an id throws (lock: `tests/cxone-binding-unreadable.spec.js`). `scheduleRealmWithdrawal` defers with `setImmediate`, and `cxOneWithdrawalsBusy` counts the deferred kick so the shutdown drain waits for it.
- **A row ends only by `deleteOwned` or `forgetGoneAccount` (account gone), `transferTo` (the one un-pend) or Mark removed — never for its age** (operator ruling, 19 Oct 2026: it is the only way left to finish the erasure). Past `EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS` from `pending_since` it is flagged `past_retention` (`_pastRetention`, never stored), counted outside the per-state partition and noted in its own try after the selection loop, so noting never stops a removal (locks: the F2 deleters arm in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`; RT1–RT7 in `tests/cxone-erasure-withdrawal.spec.js`).
- **Failures are classified, never echoed:** `last_error` is `status/code`, as a response body can carry the realm's copy of the person's details. Giving up needs BOTH 30 days AND 5 attempts; a hold never charges an attempt.
- **Both gates fail closed:** nothing runs without a health predicate (`server.js` `_instanceInService` in production); under test the automatic kick runs only with `CXONE_WITHDRAWAL_AUTORUN_IN_TEST=1`, which `tests/server-env.js` pins off, and the sweeper never starts. No test-only endpoint: `tests/cxone-provision-harness.cjs` calls `runWithdrawals` directly with its own health predicate.
- **`backfillFromHistory` seeds rows at boot only from history proving the account is ours:** a living participant's `cxone_provisioned` event with `success:true`, `already_existed` explicitly false, no `matched_by` key and a UUID id. Never admit matched rows or use `competition_users.cxone_user_id`: it does not record how the account was reached. Backfilled rows record no realm (`realm_confirmed=0`) and start `instance_unconfirmed`, so a copied database cannot delete a real installation's accounts. **An unconfirmed row's `_candidates` are only the event's current key and the global default, never every key the platform holds;** a confirmed row takes only keys whose origin and tenant are its own.
- **Superadmins act under Settings → Checkmarx One account removals.** Every reason's copy is the server's `WITHDRAWAL_REMEDY`, sent beside each row (the client adds only placeholders for rows with none). Remove anyway is offered only for `OVERRIDABLE_HOLDS`; overrides are kept as a set. An override must send the `held_reason` the client RENDERED, as `expected_hold`, and its `held_signal` as `expected_signal` (omitted when none): a missing hold returns 400 `cxone_withdrawal_expected_hold_required`, a different hold or sign 409 `cxone_withdrawal_hold_changed`. Schedule removal and Remove anyway confirm the account may pass to its owner instead (`_WD_HANDOVER_NOTE`), so "cannot be undone" is said of the removal alone.
- **Participant DELETE reports `cxone_withdrawals_scheduled`, `cxone_accounts_left_in_place`, `cxone_accounts_unrecorded` and `cxone_records_unreadable`,** each only when non-zero, built once by `deleteUserCascade` (`lib/cxone-ledger.js` `cxoneDeletionCounts`, read in its transaction before the history goes), so the reaper's `account_reaped` carries the same keys (lock: the reaper arm of `tests/cxone-withdrawals.api.spec.js`); the last three counted from `user_events` (`accountsOutsideLedger`), so a set-up record past `EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS` is no longer counted; the scheduled count is the ledger stamp's (the ledger is never purged); `already_existed` (not `matched_by`) decides which count an account falls into. The last counts set-up RECORDS (`lib/cxone-ledger.js` `_recordObject`, the reader it shares with `livingBinder`, cannot read them, or their `success` is not a boolean), never folded into `unrecorded`; its toast asks for the tenant to be checked. An unreadable `cxone_binding_cleared` subtracts nothing, so a damaged record makes the counts over-state rather than under-state. `has_cxone_realm_account` goes only to a session DELETE admits (`routes/participants.js` `_mayDeleteAccounts`: a superadmin, or a custom role holding `participants.delete`, read live; never through an impersonation; operator ruling, 24 Nov 2026), and the participants cache key carries `:del` from the same predicate; the delete toast sends a deleter without `ops.cxone` to a superadmin, not to the removals page.
- **Residuals: open and NOT accepted.**
  - Accounts the ledger never recorded are unreachable: old ones, those of jobs that failed part-way or of participants deleted before history recovery, and creates with no answer or usable id (counted by reason in `hack_arena_cxone_create_unconfirmed_total`, e.g. `post_unanswered`).
  - Checkmarx One Access Management (tenant-, application-, project-scoped) assignments are not read; whether they appear as Keycloak role mappings is unmeasured.
  - Attributes the realm's user profile hides from the key are not in the record, so cannot hold it.
  - The adoption hold cannot see a second installation that grants a same-named group or binds the account through the email match.
  - Rows with no fingerprint, every backfilled row included, skip the email check.
  - The grantable group set is rebuilt from current events, so a deleted or renamed event's group holds the account.
  - A revoked key gives no warning until a removal fails.
  - A key that cannot read the realm's default groups holds the account.
  - Ledger rows (user id, event, username, unsalted address hash, creation baseline) outlive the erasure until the account is withdrawn, passes to a living participant or the row is dismissed, however long that takes.
  - Groups granted to accounts the platform did not create are not revoked.
  - Projects and scans are never touched.
  - The shutdown drain can cut a withdrawal short; claims are cleared at boot.

#### P2 — the provisioning job's withdrawal re-asks whose account it is
- **A job whose participant is erased mid-flight withdraws only the account IT created in this run** (its own 201's Location id), never a matched or searched one (the operator's "Delete it" ruling).
- **`_abandon` calls `_withdrawalOwnershipLost` synchronously, with no await before the DELETE.** It skips on `transferred_to_living_account`, `claim_lost`, `bound_to_living_account` or `binding_unreadable` (a record naming the account cannot be read); a read that throws is `ownership_unreadable` and also skips; another removal already in flight is `removal_in_flight`. A replacement participant can be matched to the account while the job is suspended. (lock: `tests/cxone-replacement-takeover.spec.js`)
- **Ledger writes after the DELETE are owner-bound.** `deleteOwned`, `forgetGoneAccount`, `markInFlightRefusal` and `markInFlightSkip` need the owner's `userId` (`_requireOwner`); `transferTo` ends every claim and re-keys only to a user that still exists. Both guards stay on purpose: a writer that forgets one is caught by the other.
- **On a `bound_to_living_account` / `binding_unreadable` / `ownership_unreadable` skip, call `markInFlightSkip`,** or the runner never selects a row written after the cascade's stamp. A refused DELETE is handed to the runner (`withdrawal_retry_scheduled`).
- **`cxone_provision_abandoned` is an ORPHANED event (app.jsonl and its `orphan_events` copy), never `user_events`.** `realm_account_withdrawn` is present only when a withdrawal was attempted.
- **Both removers announce before the DELETE (`beginRealmRemoval`, in the block of the last ownership check) and settle through `classifyRealmRemoval` (2xx/404 removed, other 4xx refused, else unknown).** The job asks `realmRemovalBlock` before `transferTo` (no await between), its write-back and its catch, plus `realmRemovalSince` after a 409 that found nothing, and DEFERS: `cxone_provisioned {deferred}` naming `matched_account_id`, never `cxone_user_id` (`livingBinder` reads that as a binding), retried through the queue. An unknown removal is re-read a minute later by an armed kick; step 2 skips the hand-over until then. (locks: `tests/cxone-replacement-takeover.spec.js`; placement `tests/audit-v8111.spec.js`)
- **Every "the realm says it is gone" goes through `accountGoneFromRealm`, which must not call `livingBinder`:** it forgets a row re-keyed meanwhile and clears each living binding — `cxone_user_id` AND `cxone_username` (the erased person's nickname; kept, it re-arms `hasPriorBinding`) — with `cxone_binding_cleared`, re-queued unless scope is revoked. `_transfer` on a tombstone repairs instead of handing over.
- **A re-assign of an existing member provisions only through `requeueDeferredCxOneProvision`** (no link, latest row a deferral, no retry pending, no job queued or running: `_provisionJobs`): the remedy History names once retries run out.
- **Residual: open and NOT accepted.** A crash forgets the registry: a link made after the restart is handed the account at step 2 with no realm read, though its DELETE may have landed.

### Security hardening highlights

Order is load-bearing (`server.js`): scanner COUNT, `session(...)`, `_dropRevokedSessionCookie`, scanner BLOCK, `impersonationGuard`, `eventAdminGuard()`, the admin repeated-query refusal, then routes (see *Roles, scope and impersonation*).

#### Rate limiting
- **`rateLimit(windowMs, max, keyPrefix[, opts])` (`lib/middleware.js`) keys per IP; `keyExtra` narrows the key, `ipBackstopMax` adds a coarse per-IP ceiling.** The key is built by `lib/util.js` `rateBucketKey`, which never throws: a `keyExtra` value that is neither text nor a finite number shares one fixed per-source bucket. `/api/login` keys by (IP, normalised `username`, else `email`), `/api/auth/password` and `/api/auth/sign-in-method` by (IP, address), each with a per-IP backstop (`RATE_LIMIT_LOGIN_*`): a NAT'd room must not share one bucket, yet cross-address guessing from one IP stays capped. The code redeem: 10 per (IP, address) a minute, with the same per-IP backstop (`RATE_LIMIT_LOGIN_IP_BACKSTOP_PER_MIN` a minute; operator decision, 22 Nov 2026): it answers 200, so the scanner block never sees it, and the account lock refuses no guess. The code request: 5 per (IP, address) per 15 minutes, with the backstop's rate over that window (`RATE_LIMIT_LOGIN_IP_BACKSTOP_PER_MIN` × 15). The code request, the redeem and the untie, the method lookup and the password door each have their budget, window and address key held by behaviour, and their backstop where they have one (the untie has none: it guesses nothing) (lock: `tests/signin-door-limits.api.spec.js`). **Address proofs also spend a per-IP budget** (`RATE_LIMIT_PROOF_IP_PER_15MIN`, 150 per 15 minutes; operator ruling, 11 Nov 2026), so one source cannot make the platform mail unlimited new addresses: charged in `_sendEmailProof` by `ipBudgetExceeded`, refused SILENTLY (uniform `{ok:true}`, orphaned `email_proof_refused{reason:"ip_budget"}`), as a 429 for new addresses alone would say which have no account. Codes to existing accounts are not charged (capped per account). **A proof refusal is recorded only up to the same per-IP rate** (`email-proof-refused-ip`; past it counted by reason, `hack_arena_email_proof_refusals_unrecorded_total`, the answer unchanged), as the caller chooses the addresses (lock: `tests/proof-refusal-record-cap.api.spec.js`). Trusted networks and `RATE_LIMIT_ALLOWLIST_IPS` bypass both, so a booth rush from one NAT belongs in the event's trusted networks.
- **After an identity guard (`requireAdmin`/`requireSuperAdmin`/`requireParticipant`), never before** (`requireAdmin, requireSuperAdmin, exportLimiter`): an anonymous 401 must not drain a bucket the room shares; the scanner tracker handles anonymous 4xx. Context guards (`requireCompetitionContext`…) stay after the limiter; anonymous-by-design routes (login, the password door, the sign-in-method lookup, csrf-token, sign-in codes, `/credential`, badges) keep it first, as their only control. (lock: scanner in `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js`)
- **Each bucket stores its `windowMs`; `_sweepRateLimitBuckets` deletes it only past that window:** a 5-minute sweep would under-enforce longer windows. Keep the sweep a named function.
- **`RATE_LIMIT_ALLOWLIST_IPS` and live trusted network IPs bypass every `rateLimit()` limiter and the scanner tracker (`_rateLimitAllowlisted`), never the account lockout or the sign-in-code send caps (`lib/login-code.js`).** Parse IP lists only with `parseIpAllowlist`/`canonicaliseIp` (`lib/util.js`; `LOG_SKIP_IPS` too): a private parser misses non-canonical IPv6.
- **Trusted IPs are bounded** (`lib/comp-allowlist.js` `validateTrustedIps`): 10 entries, IPv4 CIDR no wider than /16 (no `0.0.0.0/0` from a form), IPv6 CIDR refused (never accept what matching cannot honour); inert before `start_time`, expired 2 h after end, computed per lookup (cache rows, never verdicts). Lookup failure means not allowlisted.
- **Saving a non-empty trusted-IP value lifts matching scanner blocks even if unchanged,** because the alert says "save the address"; audit `trusted_networks_changed` on a change, `trusted_networks_applied` on an unchanged re-save (`routes/competitions.js`; see *The three event kinds — the policy table*).
- **Use my current address** (beside the field, `useMyAddressForTrustedIps`) reads `GET /api/admin/my-address`: `canonicaliseIp(req.ip)` and its `range` (`lib/util.js` `ipAddressRange`), `events.edit` at the gate (`open`) behind `requireAdmin`, not requirePermission, which would refuse an organiser's impersonated panel where the form still saves. The form adds only a `public` address, once; any other range adds nothing and says `TRUST_PROXY` is probably wrong. Offered with `adminCan('events.edit')`, disabled with the field (locks: `tests/my-address.api.spec.js`, `tests/my-address-ui.spec.js`, the my-address arm of `tests/trusted-networks-e2e.api.spec.js`).
- **NAT storm checklist:** ingress sends `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`; `TRUST_PROXY` matches the hop count (one private `ip` across the access log means one shared bucket and scanner counter); venue in trusted networks (no restart); env allowlist (restart). Write mapped `TRUST_PROXY` subnets in IPv4 or full `/104`: `::ffff:10.0.0.0/8` trusts no IPv4 peer and `/api/csrf-token` answers 503 `session_cookie_blocked`.

#### Scanner reputation
- **COUNT runs pre-session (sees static 404s); BLOCK (`_scannerBlockMiddleware`) runs post-session so admin tiers are exempt:** else the organiser on the venue NAT cannot reach the control that lifts it.
- **`_scannerBlockExempt` asks impersonation FIRST, re-reading the acting admin's row,** never `s.impersonator.userRole` or target-shaped flags; then flags; then `users.role` (participant view clears `isAdmin`). One `_ADMIN_TIER_ROLES` Set, `event_admin` included.
- **Not counted: 429 and 413 (limiter answers; a room's camera-roll avatars must not block it), 5xx, `_isIdentityProbe401`** (anonymous GET 401 on `/api/me`/`/api/admin/me`, asked on every boot), **and `_isStaleSessionRefusal`** (a GET's 401 or a CSRF refusal, `req._csrfRefused`, whose session cookie names a session the server no longer holds, with no identity in the one it ran: a browser finding out its session ended; operator ruling, 17 Nov 2026; any cookie claims it, which buys an anonymous GET's 401 only). 415 counts. A live block survives the window roll while counters reset (counting has no admin exemption).
- **Hit paths are scrubbed and `flattenPath`-copied at ingest:** `POST /join/<token>` is an unrouted 404, and a bare slice retains the request line.
- **Silence (no alert) needs positive evidence of malice; ambiguity alerts** (`lib/scanner-classify.js`, pure). Both arms share `ROUTED_4XX_MAX`, a security property: credential stuffing buried in junk 404s must not silence itself. `probeEarlyBlockDue` reuses the silence predicate.
- **The probe signal is `req.route` at finish, not a list.** `classifyHit` buckets each hit `routed4xx` (had a route), `probe4xx` (unrouted 404) or `other4xx` (the rest: `/docs` gate 401s, benign well-knowns). `other4xx` only dilutes the probe ratio (as probe evidence it would silence a `/docs` walker; as routed it would force alerts). Never extend `PROBE_SIGNATURES` against noise: an unbounded list, and signatures lower the early-block bar. Distinct paths are a 64-bucket bitmask, never a per-IP Set (memory amplifier).
- **Silent is not invisible:** only `_raiseIpBanAlert` is gated; log and `scanner_blocked` audit fire for both verdicts. `silence_basis` is not a fourth `verdict` (metrics key on three). Alerts: see *Email and alerts*.

#### CSRF
- **`csrfProtection` is per-route,** so CSRF precedes multer's temp-file write. Refusal handling: see *Sessions and CSRF*.

#### Request-body validation contract
- **`validateBody(body, schema)` (`lib/validate.js`) validates and normalises;** handlers read `_v.values`. `int` strict, `date` string-only, `stringArray` rejects non-strings, `bool` coerces or rejects (pass-through let `"false"` enable a flag). Edit schemas are `allOptional(createSchema)` so they cannot drift. Task `title` and CxOne fields stay un-migrated (ruling).
- **Un-migrated handlers type-guard each body text field before string methods or conversions (`.toString()`, `String()`, `Number()`, `parseInt`) or binds** (`typeof` or `_firstNonStringField`; `_capsOk` does not count), refuse whitespace-only names, store dates normalised from a date STRING (a number passes `isNaN(new Date(x))`). Fix scanner failures with a guard.
- **`req.body = {}` is preset; `csrfProtection` refuses an unparsed body (415 `unsupported_media_type`) or top-level array (400 `body_not_object`),** else the preset reads it as empty. AFTER the CSRF check, so a cross-site text/plain form still ends the session; not global, which would turn probe 404s into 415s. Multipart needs a stack function named `multerMiddleware`; a renamed one fails closed (lock: `tests/request-body-shape.api.spec.js`). `/credential` and `/api/unsubscribe` sit outside.
- **Id lists from a request are deduplicated and capped at `BULK_ADMIN_MAX` (400 above),** bounding what one request asks for: a duplicated invitation event (`routes/invitations.js` `competitionIds`) once provisioned Checkmarx One twice per invitee.
- **Query:** `refuseRepeatedQuery` (400) applies only to `isAdmin` sessions on `/api/admin/`: anonymous callers keep requireAdmin's 401, and unrouted admin paths keep the router's 404, which the scanner counts as probe evidence. `firstNonScalarQueryParam` may return `""`: test `!== null`. `offset`/`page` go through `clampPagingIndex` (a huge float 500s SQLite).

#### ONE path normaliser for every gate
- **Every path gate compares `normaliseRoutePath(req.path)` (`lib/util.js`):** decode once, collapse slashes, lowercase, strip one trailing slash, as serve-static and non-strict routing do; the gap is the bypass, and over-normalising is safe (matches more). Users: grep `normaliseRoutePath(`.
- **Add a new gate to the generated path-variant lock** (`tests/functional-security-b.api.spec.js` `VARIANTS`): a hand-written table holds only reported spellings.

#### Body caps
- **Credential paths cap JSON AND urlencoded at 16 KB** (`_AUTH_PATHS`, `_PUBLIC_FORM_PATHS`) ahead of the 5 MB parsers; one type alone is a bypass. `_PUBLIC_FORM_PATHS` stays separate: `_AUTH_PATHS` is the anonymous sign-in and sign-up doors.
- **The sign-up (`/api/register`) is in `_AUTH_PATHS` because it takes no photo:** a preset picture (`VALID_AVATARS`) or none; anything else, an `/api/images/` reference included, is 400 `avatar_not_accepted` before any write (a picture is set in the profile). The 128 MB import parser (`_importJsonParser`) sits on the two import routes only, after limiter, auth and CSRF; the global 5 MB parser skips `_IMPORT_PATHS`.
- **Every multer instance is built by `boundedMulter` (`lib/multipart.js`), which refuses at boot limits that do not bound a request's text** (rules in its header; lock: `tests/multer-limits.api.spec.js`, which also fails any other multer require). `fieldSize` is the largest value admitted (multer hands busboy `fieldSize + 1`), hence `3 * SUBMIT_ANSWER_MAX_CHARS`; the boundary tests there fail if a multer bump moves it.

#### Log scrubbing of capabilities (standing rule)
- **Any URL carrying a capability, identity or answer gets a `_scrubLogPath` rule (`server.js`, write time, `/gi`) in the same commit;** path segments need their own. Regular admins read logs; backups copy them. `?token=` (365-day unsubscribe) decodes to an email; `?q=` is redacted on every path (Submissions search carries answers). Keep the read-time `?code=` pass (`_CODE_REDACT_RE` in `redactLogEntryForRegularAdmin`): older lines still hold raw codes.
- **Every other sink uses it too:** CSP reports, proxy diagnostics, perf spans, scanner paths (locks: every sink `tests/request-log-context.spec.js` arm (e), behaviour `tests/log-capability-scrub.api.spec.js`).
- **`_describeRequestError` (`server.js`) is the one place an error becomes a response.** Body-parser 413 names the limit and size sent; JSON-parse, charset, multipart and param-decode failures get fixed sentences (parser text can quote the body or a capability); the log line uses `logMessage`, never spreading the error (MulterError `filename`). `MulterError` has no `.status`: `_MULTER_TOO_LARGE` picks the 413 codes and their sentences (the submit form's over-long `answer` gets the handler's own); any other is 400. Otherwise only an `err.expose` 4xx speaks; a 5xx is generic. A connection that closed mid-request (`_clientClosedRequest`: multer's "Request aborted"/"Request closed", raw-body's `request.aborted`, ECONNRESET, each only once the socket is gone; usually the client, but Node's requestTimeout looks the same, so the line names neither party) is one warn line, status 499, and no response, so no access line, no 5xx and nothing for scanner reputation; the upload's temp file and budget go as for any refusal (lock: `tests/upload-client-abort.api.spec.js`). Once `res.headersSent` the generic handler writes nothing (a second write throws, then Express's final handler cuts the finished response's socket) and cuts only a partial response: finished means the handler's `res.end` returned (`_markEndCalled`, mounted right after `compression()`), never `res.writableEnded` alone, which stays false while compression flushes (lock: `tests/request-error-reporting.spec.js`).
- **A catch echoes `e.message` only behind `userError`/`conflict`/`forbidden`** (lock: `tests/caught-error-echo.spec.js`): driver text names schema.

#### Optimistic concurrency
- **Admin edit PUTs and self-enroll regenerate take `expectedUpdatedAt` and 409 when stale; absent is unchecked.**
- **An edit form sends the token of the row it was FILLED from (`S._tfEditUpdatedAt`, the event form's `overlay._updatedAt`), never a list model re-read at Save:** lists reload under an open form (the 15 s events poll, tab return), so a re-read passes another admin's change. The form's own writes update its token.
- **Lifecycle writers bump `updated_at` on row and parent** (see *The competition PUT*; lock: `tests/functional-core-a.api.spec.js`).
- **New checks are an in-transaction CAS, not a pre-check (fails open);** Profile version: see *Edit-profile dialog contract*.

#### Security headers and CSP
- **Headers are hand-set in `server.js`** (nosniff, frame DENY, strict referrer, deny-all Permissions-Policy, COOP/CORP, HSTS unless `HSTS=0`; no COEP). Cookie `httpOnly`, `sameSite: lax`.
- **The main CSP (every response but the maintenance page, which has its own `default-src 'none'` policy): no third-party origin, Trusted Types enforced, `style-src-elem` never `'unsafe-inline'`** (it stops injected HTML styling a fake login). `/api/csp-report` logs extension reports at debug. SVG ingest refused. See *Architecture*.

### Audit trail

#### The standing event rule
- **Every state-changing action emits an event-log entry; every user-caused entry spreads `...reqContext(req)` (source IP).** Operator ruling. Read-only acts may be logged too (exports emit orphaned `data_exported`).
- **Exactly two exemptions:** request-less background work (scheduler, reaper, CxOne, boot; actor `"system"`), and changes confined to the acting admin's own view (per-admin IP-ban alert dismiss; the global one is audited). The first is about scope, not actor: a `"system"` event inside a request (`timeslot_cleared`, `login_success`) carries the requester's context.
- **`recordChallengeEvent` is outside the rule:** analytics only, never app.jsonl (see *Analytics*).
- **Emit whenever the write happens, and SELECT the column the emit reports.** A conditional emit under an unconditional write is a silent state change.
- **Only the app.jsonl mirror and orphaned side channels are non-fatal (try/catch).** A throwing DB emit rolls its transaction back; a broken mirror fails silently, so only a row-asserting test catches a wrong or missing emit.
- **Not gaps; do not re-flag:** `select-timeslot` emits `active_competition_changed` only if its own write moved the pin (`select-competition` normally records it). Import's `user_added`/`added_to_competition`/`group_added` (`via:"import"`), `group_bound`, `superadmin_login_success` and `email_change_expired` exist.
- Locks: `tests/functional-core-b.api.spec.js` (direct and `queueEvent` emits with `req` in scope), `tests/event-log-attribution.api.spec.js`; blind spot: details passed by name.

#### Attribution and reqContext
- **Actor:** admin acts `getAdminIdentity(req)` (email or `"superadmin"`; under impersonation the acting admin); self-service the participant; self-registration, login `"system"`; invitation acceptance the inviter (`invited_by`); public credential lookups `"anonymous"`.
- **`reqContext(req)` (`lib/audit.js`) always sets `ip`, `user_agent` (300 chars), `req_id`, `session_id` (null if missing); `impersonated_by` + `acting_as` only while impersonating.** Spread conditional keys only when present, never as null: History renders every `details` key raw.
- **`req.id` is stamped before the body parsers and returned as `X-Request-ID`:** it joins app.jsonl, the access log and a user's bug report. The error handler logs a 4xx at `warn` (`Request refused: …`), a 5xx at `error` with the stack.
- **Deferred emits capture context at QUEUE time** (`reqContext(req)` before `queueEvent(…)`; likewise the detached sign-in-code send): the drain runs after the response.
- **Pass `context: reqContext(req)` into `lib/group-sync.js` reconciles.** Most `reconcile*Competitions` wrapper callers still omit it (known gap).
- **`_requestActor(req)` (`server.js`) is the one "who made this request"** (target identity plus `impersonated_by`) for the access log and `server.js` perf-spans; other spans carry only `req_id`.
- **Never log capability secrets:** invitations as `invitation_fp` (`capabilityFingerprint`), credential codes as `_codeFingerprint`; `lib/log.js` `_REDACT_KEYS` covers the rest. **Never reword the superadmin password banner** (on `console`): the Logs pane redacts it for non-superadmins by prefix (`server.js` `_SUPERADMIN_BANNER_RE`), so old lines would leak.
- **No `console.*` in `routes/`, `lib/http.js`, `lib/group-sync.js`; use `log.<level>(context, message)`, context first.** `lib/log.js` sends stray console output to app.jsonl as `legacy:true`, without `req_id` (lock: `tests/request-log-context.spec.js`); an Error argument as its stack (serialised, it loses message and stack but keeps a parser's `body`; lock: `tests/request-error-reporting.spec.js`). `log.error("msg", ctx)` drops every field; a scanner bans a string literal first in `routes/` and `lib/` only. `db.js` and `lib/config.js` keep `console` on purpose (tests read stdout; require cycle).

#### The app.jsonl mirror
- **Every `db.transaction()` whose body calls `record*Event` / `createTaskVersion` runs via `runTxnWithMirrorBuffer(() => txn())`, no "emit is last" carve-out,** so a rollback drops its mirror lines rather than log events the DB never saw. It runs the txn itself; never call its return value.
- **The mirror is eventually consistent, best-effort:** written on `setImmediate` after commit, enriched (`_resolveActor`) off the request path. `*_events` is the truth; shutdown drains `deferredAuditPending()`. `{mirror:false}` drops duplicate per-scope lines, never the DB row.
- **Orphaned events (`recordOrphanedAuditEvent`) have no `*_events` row:** deleted entities, exports, credential lookups, purges, every task event. Each is mirrored to app.jsonl AND copied to `orphan_events` (details through `_redactValue`; `competition_id`/`timeslot_id` from details, else the competition/timeslot scope id), the copy NON-FATAL (`swallowed`), so a failed copy never undoes the act it records; inside a caller's transaction it rolls back with it. The Audit page lists the copies; orphaned events older than the table exist only in app.jsonl (Raw logs). **A reader without `audit.raw_logs` sees a copy naming an event without its request context, or the fields `LOG_EVENT_RAW_ONLY_FIELDS` names for its type, and never a `LOG_EVENT_RAW_ONLY_TYPES` row** (`routes/audit.js`, read time, `withheld_fields`): its writer may have recorded people who are not the event's (a public certificate check, an erased recipient). A new orphaned type naming an event decides there what it carries (no scanner: *Stated gaps*). `deleteUserCascade` deletes the user-scoped copies with the user's `user_events`. **Never inline an answer into one:** the task `edited` event writes sealed and rich fields as `"(body changed — see task_versions)"` (`routes/challenges.js`).
- **`task_versions` holds full snapshots backing restore.** `recordSealedReveal` adds a `sealed_reveal` row plus an orphaned event with reqContext (see *The sealed answer*).
- **Event lists page row-stably** via `_eventListResponse` (`snapshot_tie` is the ROWID, never the UUID).
- **Every surface showing a stored `details` text reads it through `lib/audit.js` `parseStoredDetails`** (the History lists, the Audit page, the person export, a challenge's versions): text that is not JSON reads as `{details_unreadable: true}`, the row keeping its type, time and actor (History and the Audit page's details dialog say its details could not be read; on the Audit page its outcome is `unknown` (Unknown), unless its activity name already says it failed: `routes/audit.js` `OUT`), never a 500 for the list holding it. SQL hands stored details to a JSON function only in a `CASE`'s `json_valid` branch: those functions throw on such text, and SQLite neither short-circuits an `AND` in a value nor promises the order of a WHERE's terms (`userEventCompetitionScopeSql`, `userEventDetailsSql`; lock: `tests/history-unreadable-details.spec.js`).

#### Event-data retention
- **`lib/event-retention.js` `sweepExpiredEvents` purges the `*_events` tables and `orphan_events` past `EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS` (730),** daily + at boot, `PURGE_MAX_PER_RUN` per table per tick via a rowid subquery (no `DELETE … LIMIT`). Skipped under `NODE_ENV=test`.
- **Each `EVENT_TABLES` entry names its timestamp column** (`challenge_events`: `occurred_at`; the rest `changed_at`). A per-table error is only logged, so a wrong column silently keeps rows forever (lock: `tests/mutations-admin.api.spec.js`, which cross-checks the list).
- **A non-empty purge emits orphaned `events_purged` after its DELETEs, stamped now,** so the pass that writes it never deletes it; its `orphan_events` copy ages out with the window like any orphaned event (app.jsonl keeps the mirror line).
- **Deliberately excluded:** `app-*.jsonl` (rotated by `lib/log.js`, purged by `scripts/purge.sh` and low-disk cleanup), `entity_status_observations` (bookkeeping), `task_versions` (backs restore), `cxone_realm_accounts` (kept until resolved; see *F2 — a deleted participant's Checkmarx One account is removed from the tenant*).
- **Three non-audit tables ride the same pass on windows of their own, never in `EVENT_TABLES`:** soft-deleted event templates (`TEMPLATE_RESTORE_DAYS` after deletion), `certificate_share_links` (30 days after expiry, oldest first through `idx_certificate_share_links_expires`, capped at the run's limit) and `images` rows nothing references (`lib/image-sweep.js`: older than `IMAGE_SWEEP_GRACE_MS`, 7 days, for saves in flight; oldest first through `idx_images_created`, at most `IMAGE_PURGE_MAX_PER_RUN`). Each is reported apart from `counts`/`total` (`templates_purged`; `share_links_purged`, `share_links_capped`; `images_purged`, `images_capped`), and share links or images purged add their count to the pass's `events_purged` record, which a pass deleting nothing else then writes too; images purged also add `images_purged_ids`, exactly the ids the DELETE's `RETURNING` named (the operator's way to find one and restore it from a backup).
- **What references an image is `lib/image-refs.js`, a decision for every text or blob column** (`scan` for `/api/images/<uuid>` in its text, `ids` for a bare id, else `exempt` with a named reason; lock: `tests/image-sweep.spec.js`, both ways against a booted schema, and each scanned holder kept by behaviour). A new column that can name a picture (rich text, a copied avatar, a design field) is `scan`ned, or the sweep deletes what it shows a week on; a doubtful one is scanned. The sweep is ONE synchronous pass (collect, then delete, no await between), so no write can move a reference past it; never give it an await. That bounds only its deletions (`IMAGE_PURGE_MAX_PER_RUN`), not its reads: once any image is a week old every pass reads every scanned column in full, so it reports the collection's time (`images_refs_ms`) and logs at warn above `IMAGE_REFS_WARN_MS` (1 s). A holder that cannot be read stops the sweep.
